Visual references (Lucas sent eleven images as mood references; they are described here in words. Some are film stills or photos of real people: they set a look and a tone only, and no character is based on anyone's face.)

**01 · Bar, close two-shot** (`01-bar-close-two-shot.jpg`). A dim, crowded dive bar at night.
Two people pressed close in the foreground: she faces camera-left with a wary look; he stands
right behind her, chin near her shoulder, looking past her. Their faces are lit low and warm
from the side, skin with a soft sheen, and everything else sinks into brown-black. Behind them:
amber wall sconces, a red neon strip, a cold green glow from a drinks fridge, small red points
of light along the bar, all soft in shallow focus. Intimacy that reads as threat.

**02 · Candlelit bar counter** (`02-candlelit-bar-counter.jpg`). A cramped bar lit by vintage
lamps: a green glass lantern, a red fringed shade, candles in glasses on the counter among the
bottles, a chalkboard menu behind. Heavy red and green colour, black corners, film grain and a
vignette, like an old snapshot. A couple sits close at the end of the bar, her in a white top,
him leaning in.

**03 · Record bar** (`03-record-bar.jpg`). A listening bar: a floor-to-ceiling wall of vinyl
records behind the bar, warm dome pendant lamps hung low, rows of bottles, backlit hi-fi
units. Patrons in silhouette; a man and a woman seated side by side at the bar, seen from
behind. Warm amber, mostly shadow.

**04 · Doubled portrait** (`04-doubled-portrait.jpg`). A pale woman with long auburn hair
stares into the lens, flat and cold, against black; a blurred second copy of her turns away
beside her, like a double exposure. A self split in two.

**05 · Bathtub, black and white** (`05-bathtub-bw.jpg`). A black-and-white photo in a tiled
bathroom. A young woman in a polka-dot blouse, an apron and rubber gloves holds up the head of
a charred, blood-slick body slumped in the tub, and smiles at the camera as if posing for a
family snapshot. Domestic calm and neatness right next to horror: she is pleasant, the scene
is monstrous.

**06 · The morning after** (`06-morning-after.jpg`). A warm, yellow-tinged snapshot taken from
the pillows, like a phone photo with the flash on. A man with long locs and a tattooed arm sleeps
face-down into the pillow, mouth open; the woman beside him is awake, a hand pressed to her
forehead, staring off, bored and uneasy. Soft focus, crumpled patterned bedding. He is out cold;
she is somewhere else in her head.

**07 · The abandoned hotel inside** (`07-abandoned-hotel-interior.jpg`). Inside an old,
abandoned hotel: a carved-leg pool table in the middle of a bare plank floor, a tall rack of
cues, a wood stove, a long table with spindle chairs, pressed-tin walls, an open white door.
Low sun slants through tall windows and throws lace-like patterns of light across the wall and
floor; everything else is dust-brown shadow, sepia and still, untouched for decades.

**08 · The desert motel** (`08-desert-motel.jpg`). An abandoned roadside motel in flat desert:
a big peeling "MOTEL" sign on steel legs with a red "OFFICE" arrow, a gutted two-storey block, a
long carport, palm trees and dry scrub in front, a pale washed-out sky, dusty faded-film colour.

**09 · The joy ride** (`09-joy-ride.jpg`). A vintage silver open two-seater convertible, 1950s
shape, seen from behind on a winding mountain road at sunset above a sea of cloud; the low sun
flares from the left, the light gold and warm; two people in the seats. The romantic, free
version of the drive, which the cold open plays against.

**10 · Two faces, sepia** (`10-two-faces-sepia.jpg`). A sepia portrait: a woman's composed face
in front, three-quarter to camera, chin resting on her hand, calm and pleasant. Behind her, a
second version of her face, half in shadow, stares straight into the lens with dark streaks
running down from the eye like black tears. The monster behind the composure.

**11 · The open road** (`11-open-road.jpg`). An aerial of a red vintage open-top sports car,
alone on a straight two-lane road through golden grassland at sunset. Hazy, warm, low light;
the grass moves in the wind; hills far off in the haze. Wide, lonely and golden.

## Developed

**A vigilante who falls in love with every unpunished man she sentences drives her latest across the desert, laughing and filming him, still waiting for the dead man to love her back.**

Format: series_pilot. Series pilot. Lucas's hint names it, and his own engine repeats. Venus 'finds male killers, rapist, and DV and sex offers whose case get lost' and 'lures them and kills them over a date', so every episode brings a new man, a new game of decorum and a new wall. Across the season the stakes rise toward the one man who tells the truth at decorum and seems to love her back, and whether she can sentence him (A17). Runner-up: short film, which has one woman, one want and one cost. It lost because a short has to end the engine that the idea wants repeated. Build: D01 outlines the whole pilot. V01 is the cold open, the strongest self-contained sequence. It has no spoken lines, only her laughter, and its hook plays on its own.

Laughing, Venus drives through an empty desert town, filming the man asleep beside her, until his pale, open eyes show he is dead. At an abandoned motel, pistol in her back pocket, she unloads the luggage into the room she has kept for weeks: walls of Jean photos, none of them together. Flashback: a Thursday dive bar, Jean three nights drunk over a breakup, a stranger asking "have we met before?" She has watched him for months; his ex's domestic-violence report went nowhere. Her game, decorum, is her verdict: each lie costs him a straw. In her all-white apartment Jean wipes dried blood off the toilet; her meds sit untaken; a trance-soul track quiets her. She slides over her metal tin and asks for one true thing about love, his last straw; he gives her a line, and she opens it. By morning the "trip" has stopped his heart; she lies awake beside him, waiting for him to turn over, then remakes the white bed until it is spotless. At the motel she seats Jean at the table, takes the first photograph of them together, and pins it beside the first photos of the next man.

What makes it this idea and nobody else's:

- Kept: Lucas's cold open as he wrote it. The laughing joy ride with a man who seems asleep, the reveal of his pale, lost eyes, the pistol, the motel she keeps, and the wall of photos 'yet none of them together'.
- Kept: the decorum straw game and its dialogue, the names Venus and St. Jean ('J'), the all-white apartment with Jean in black as her black hole, the dried blood he wipes away, the untaken meds, the aftermath of the night, and the female-Dexter engine of luring unpunished men over a date.
- Obvious version: a gender-swapped Dexter. A cool, in-control vigilante seduces a predator, the last scene reveals she is the killer, and a voiceover lays out her code.
- Differs by what it costs her: Venus is not in control. She is in limerence with every man she condemns, and killing him is the only way he can never leave her. The pilot opens after the kill, so the suspense is why she still loves him, not whether she will do it. The cost shows on screen: the tin stays closed until his line about love, because she was hoping he would pass (A18).
- Added detail (A04): decorum is her private verdict. She already knows the true answers, and each lie Jean tells takes a straw off the house. His line about love costs him the last straw.
- Added ending (A06): the first photograph of the two of them together, pinned beside the next man's photos. It pays off Lucas's 'none of them together' and shows the series engine in one frame.
- Added payoff: Venus's 'let's take a trip' comes true twice, first as the tin and then as the road trip with his body.
- Added season question (A17): the one man who passes decorum. The pilot's pattern of one lie, one straw and one sentence exists so that a later episode can break it.
- Product role: none. This is an unbranded original drama with nothing to sell, so there is no product to become decoration.
- Taste notes: every one is followed and none is broken. Following 'Hands before faces', Jean's face is held back until the dead-eyes reveal. Following 'Sound design before music', the only music plays inside the scene. Following 'Restraint over explanation', her meds are seen but never named as the reason. 'The product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply because there is no product.

Story:

- **protagonist**: Venus, a vigilante who falls into limerence with every unpunished man she sentences
- **want**: for Jean to love her back and tell her one true thing, so the house they build could last
- **obstacle**: Jean is the man she has been hunting, and each lie he tells at decorum costs him a straw
- **turn**: in her all-white apartment she slides over her metal tin and asks for one true thing about love, his last straw; he gives her a line, she opens the tin, and by morning the 'trip' has stopped his heart
- **ending**: At the motel she seats Jean at the table, takes the first photograph of them together, and pins it beside the first photos of the next man.

## Strategy

**Punishing the man the system lost can't make him love her back.**

- **audience tension**: Viewer (who the show is for): people who follow true-crime cases online, on the night a case they have followed for months closes with nobody charged and they keep reading anyway [brief.audience; the moment is H1]. In their voice: I want the man who walked to be punished, yet months later I still look him up, waiting for a confession he will never give me. [H1] The loss they'd hate to admit: the man they want punished now has more of their attention than the person he hurt, and wanting justice has turned into a one-sided attachment that no verdict would end. The referent is the man who walked, which makes the viewer a direct mirror of Venus: she falls for the man she sentences, carries out the sentence herself, and still waits for him to love her back. Take the show away and the tension still exists. Viewer proxy (assumption, used only if V02 or S03 go public): followers of true-crime podcasts, forums and creator channels. Rejected drafts: H7 (guilt at cheering for a killer) and H10 (attachment to the victim, not to him).

Buyer (who acts): drama development execs at streamers and networks, at the point where they sort genre pitches into the few they take to script [brief.audience]. Proxy (assumption): a named list the producer or agent emails a private screener link to. There is no media buy. Execs buy when they believe this viewer exists. Their own conflict, a hook this audience already binges against the fear of buying a copy, is the Barrier and not the tension [H2, H8].
- **desired behavior**: Goal: a paid script commission or development deal for Lucas's pilot, the step before a series order. For the buyer, return becomes revenue if the repeating engine ('every episode brings a new man, a new game of decorum and a new wall' [F19, content only]) brings viewers back episode after episode [H9]. Release cadence is the buyer's call (see assumptions).
From: the exec reads the logline and files LIMERENCE with the other vigilante pitches [H2].
To: after watching V01 and reading D01, the exec requests the pilot script or books a meeting with Lucas [brief.objective].
Jobs:
V01 (16:9, 90 s, private screener link, laptop, sound on assumed): makes the exec feel the love story before the eyes land, so they open D01.
D01 (outline, read after V01): the exec decides here. It proves the episode's shape, that the engine repeats [F19], and the season question [F20].
V02 (9:16, 15 s, deck embed and forwardable link, must read muted): gets the colleague it is forwarded to to open V01.
S01 (2:3, deck cover): the proposition in one frame, with her laughing and him still.
S02 (16:9, deck page): shows the world's rule, his wall with no photo of them together [F2].
S03 (4:5, in-feed muted, posted only if the producer releases social): a reminder of decorum. It is not expected to produce a request.
- **product relevance**: Moment: between pitches, an exec opens the producer's screener link and watches V01. Without a want of its own, the piece gets filed with the comps as a gender-swapped vigilante [H2]. What fixes it: [F1] 'a desire for the feelings to be returned, not mainly for sex' turns her motive from control into longing, and [F2] 'none show the two of them together' shows that longing in one frame. Scene that must land: the V01 drive. Venus laughs and films a man who never wakes until his open eyes show why. The feeling it leaves is tenderness that carries on after the dread begins. D01 lands it again at the end [F17]: the first photo of them together, pinned beside the next man. For the season: because punishing can't make a man love her back, the season asks what she does with the one man who seems to, and whether she can sentence him [F20, A17]. That carries the proposition past the pilot into D01, where the exec decides. For the viewer, it speaks to the night a case they followed closes with nobody charged and the man who walked still has their attention [H1]. Barrier: fatigue with vigilante-killer shows and the reading of this one as a copy [H2, H8], plus a creator with no checkable track record supplied. Permission: not established. No credits, attached producer or prior work were supplied (see blocker).
- **proof points**: [F6] 'Venus lures the men and kills them over a date.' Supports: 'Punishing'.; [F13] 'every unpunished man she sentences' (logline, content only). Supports: 'the man the system lost'. For Jean himself this rests on F14, which is contingent on A02.; [F1] 'Limerence is infatuation marked by a desire for the feelings to be returned, not mainly for sex.' Supports: 'love her back'.; [F17] 'takes the first photograph of them together, and pins it beside the first photos of the next man.' Supports: 'can't make him'. The only photo of them together, the one her wall lacked (F2), is taken after he is dead, and the next man is already up. This proves what the pilot contains, not that it beats its genre. Rests on A06.
- **success criteria**: The To measure, as a rate. Metric: execs who request the pilot script or book a meeting, per 10 execs sent V01 and D01. An exec who does both counts once. Target: client to set. If none is set, the studio threshold is 2 per 10 (a go/no-go line, not a benchmark). Window: flight dates requested. Source: the producer's send log and calendar.; Metric: V01 play-through to the title card per unique screener open. Target: client to set. Window: flight dates requested. Source: screener-host analytics (this assumes a host that reports play-through).; Metric: D01 opens per V01 play-through, to show whether the cold open sends execs to the outline. Target: client to set. Window: flight dates requested. Source: document-link analytics if tracked, otherwise asked on the producer's follow-up.; Proposition read-back. Metric: of the 5 development readers in the H2 test, and later of the execs met, the share who say unprompted that the show is about her wanting him to love her back and not about a vigilante code. Target: studio threshold of at least 3 of 5 readers before the first send. For execs, client to set. Window: pre-send test, then each pitch meeting. Source: H2 test notes and the producer's meeting notes.; Muted read of V02. Metric: share of muted test viewers who can say the passenger is dead and she keeps filming. Target: studio threshold of 4 of 5. Window: before V02 is embedded or posted. Source: studio test.; 90-day measure. Metric: a paid script commission or development deal signed. Target: client to set. Window: 90 days from the first send. Source: producer and Lucas.
- **supplied facts**: F1 "Limerence is infatuation marked by a desire for the feelings to be returned, not mainly for sex." [verified_product_facts]; F2 "The walls where she lives are covered in photos of the man, and none show the two of them together." [verified_product_facts]; F3 "The bar game is called decorum; its goal is to see whether the house they build would last." [verified_product_facts]; F4 "She carries a gun in her back pocket at the motel." [verified_product_facts]; F5 "The man's name is St. Jean, called J." [verified_product_facts]; F6 "Venus lures the men and kills them over a date." [verified_product_facts]; F7 "The colour theme is white." [verified_product_facts]; F8 "The man is dressed in all black, as her black hole." [verified_product_facts]; F9 "Her meds are in her bathroom." [verified_product_facts]; F10 "She hasn't taken the meds in weeks." [verified_product_facts]; F11 "There is a small amount of dried blood behind the toilet seat in her apartment." [verified_product_facts]; F12 "Trance-soul music plays to quiet her from feeling crazy." [verified_product_facts]; F13 "A vigilante who falls in love with every unpunished man she sentences drives her latest across the desert, laughing and filming him, still waiting for the dead man to love her back." [development.logline; proves content only]; F14 "She has watched him for months; his ex's domestic-violence report went nowhere." [development.premise; proves content only; rests on A02 and A03, still open with Lucas]; F15 "Her game, decorum, is her verdict: each lie costs him a straw." [development.premise; proves content only; rests on A04]; F16 "she slides over her metal tin and asks for one true thing about love, his last straw; he gives her a line, and she opens it" [development.premise; proves content only; rests on A18]; F17 "At the motel she seats Jean at the table, takes the first photograph of them together, and pins it beside the first photos of the next man." [development.story_seed.ending; proves content only; rests on A06]; F18 "for Jean to love her back and tell her one true thing, so the house they build could last" [development.story_seed.want; proves content only]; F19 "every episode brings a new man, a new game of decorum and a new wall" [development.format_rationale; proves content only]; F20 "the one man who tells the truth at decorum and seems to love her back, and whether she can sentence him" [brief D01 label and development.format_rationale; proves content only; rests on A17]
- **hypotheses**: H2 Riskiest: V01's reveal moves execs from reading LIMERENCE as a gender-swapped vigilante copy to reading it as a show about her wanting him to love her back. It goes first because if it dies, the script request fails. If H1 dies, the proposition still stands on H7 or H10. Test: send the logline alone to 5 development readers, then V01. Each time, ask unprompted what it reminds them of and what it is about. Owner: producer. Kills it if: after V01, fewer than 3 of 5 say it is about her wanting him to love her back rather than about a vigilante code.; H1: when a case closes with nobody charged, people who follow it online keep watching the man who walked and wait on him for something he will never give (a confession, an apology, a sign). The attachment is to him, which mirrors Venus directly. Test: six short calls with people who followed a case that closed with no charge. Ask how it felt when it closed and what they still do about it. Keep it unprompted: do not name or ask about the man, and do not use the words love or attachment. No case detail from these calls enters the show. Owner: producer. Kills it if: fewer than 3 of 6 describe, unprompted, still looking up the man who walked or waiting on him for something. If they describe waiting on the victim, the family or the case instead, revive H10. If they describe only justice and guilt, revive H7.; H3: no series on air or announced leads with the clause only LIMERENCE owns: she falls in love with every man she sentences, and the sentence is how she keeps him. The nearest threats are listed from common knowledge, unverified and for internal use only. You and Killing Eve both lead with a killer's obsessive, unreturned love. In Promising Young Woman the heroine falls for a man who turns out to be implicated in the case she is avenging. Dexter leads with the code. Test: the producer checks the clause against You, Killing Eve and Promising Young Woman first, then scans current and announced slates and loglines. Owner: producer. Kills it if: any of them, or any series on air or announced, has a lead who falls for each person she punishes and uses the punishment to keep him.; H4: V01 works as the hook only if the tenderness holds after the eyes land, with her still laughing. Test: show a rough cut to 5 cold viewers and ask what they felt just before and just after the eyes. Owner: editor. Kills it if: most felt dread before the eyes (the reveal is telegraphed) or felt nothing tender (the love story did not play).; H5: an exec's script request depends more on the D01 pages showing the engine repeat and escalate [F19, F20] than on the pilot story alone. Test: ask the 5 development readers from H2 which D01 page would decide a script request. Owner: Lucas. Kills it if: fewer than 3 of 5 point to the engine or season pages. If they point to Venus's want instead, D01 leads with her want.; H6: the untaken meds, seen and never named, read as texture and not as the cause of the killings. Test: one sensitivity read of D01 by a reader with lived or clinical mental-health experience. Owner: producer. Kills it if: the reader takes the meds as the reason she kills.; H9: viewers come back from episode to episode for the repeating engine (the next man, the next round of decorum, the next wall), and that return is how the series earns for the buyer. Test: after V01 and a one-page D01 summary, ask 5 viewers from the true-crime proxy, unprompted, what they would want to see next. Owner: producer. Kills it if: fewer than 3 of 5 name something the engine repeats, such as the next man, the next round of decorum or whether she falls again. If they name only who Jean was, D01 has to bring the next man in earlier.; H7 Rejected: In their voice: I want the man who walked to pay, yet I feel guilty cheering for a killer. Every vigilante series already owns this moral-dilemma tension, so it fails the competitor-name test. Revive if: the H1 calls surface justice and guilt but never attachment.; H8 Rejected (exec tension): In their voice: I need a hook this true-crime audience already binges, yet I'd hate to be the one who bought the gender-swapped copy. It describes the buying decision, not what the show speaks to, so it is kept as the Barrier. Revive if: H2 shows execs never get past the copy question. The pitch then states the difference before the feeling.; H10 Rejected: In their voice: I grieved for someone I never met, yet nobody who knew them knows I exist. Here the attachment is to the victim. It mirrors Venus less directly, because she falls for the man and not for the person he hurt. Revive if: the H1 calls describe waiting on the victim, the family or the case rather than on the man who walked.

## Routes

In production: **R2_stretch_camera_roll**. Recommended: R2_stretch_camera_roll. Proposition: 'Punishing the man the system lost can't make him love her back.' Proof beat, 50-56 s: after his open eyes the phone comes back up, REC 01:04:55, her thumb rests on the flip-camera icon, then slides back; the lens stays on him. 18-26 s plants the same pause before the eyes, so after them it reads as 'not like this, not yet'. Success: at least 3 of 5 development readers say unprompted, after the 90 s V01, that it's about her wanting him to love her back; V02's post-eyes flip beat (11-13 s) serves the 4-of-5 muted read.
Gives up: the objective camera for most of the drive, Venus's face until 34 s, Jean's until 42 s, and full screen width for 40 of 90 s (0-34 s and 50-56 s).
Failure: at 18-26 s and 50-56 s the thumb on the icon is too small to read at phone size, and thumbs garble in generation. Fix: the C fallback keeps the choice: the screen flips to the front camera for under a second, catches both of them (his head slumped away at 18-26 s; his open eyes aimed past the lens at 50-56 s), then flips back to him. At 42-50 s (A16) the open eyes in a moving car may not hold; the fallback moves the reveal to the parked car at the motel.
Kill test: in the H2 test, fewer than 3 of 5 readers say after V01 that it's about her wanting him to love her back, and they read the phone as trophy-taking or as her keeping herself out of frame with a body. Then the photograph idea has failed, not the craft. Switch to R1, whose unreturned squeeze carries the want with no camera.
Case R1: tone ('Tender... Her laugh, her phone and her hands fixing his collar carry it') and the V01 label as written. Pick R1 if Lucas wants the drive in the objective camera with her face in the first act, or if the phone frame tests as a gimmick.
Case R3: the D01 label's season question (A17, the one man who tells the truth at decorum), read with H5. Pick R3 if readers say the engine pages (F19: new man, new decorum, new wall) decide a script request, because only R3 puts decorum in the cold open. Lucas must also accept V01 spending the bar flashback.

### R1_safe_unreturned_squeeze: She can take his life, but she can't make his hand close on hers.

- **emotional promise**: The ache of watching her squeeze his hand and wait, still smiling, for a squeeze that never comes back.
- **product role**: SAFE. No product (unbranded drama); the subject is Venus, played as a lover on a date. Job in frame: her hand asks for a response he can't give (the squeeze, the collar, the arm across his chest), which is the reciprocation limerence wants. First appearance: her hand in his at 0 s. Her face comes at 32 s and Jean's at 40 s (hands before faces). Brand assets: LIMERENCE title card on V01's final frame only and in V02's last 2 s. No logos: the car, phone, groceries and clothes are unbranded (A10). Proof: 'Limerence is infatuation marked by a desire for the feelings to be returned, not mainly for sex.' 'She carries a gun in her back pocket at the motel.' 'The walls where she lives are covered in photos of the man, and none show the two of them together.' 'The man is dressed in all black, as her black hole.'
- **visual language**: Palette: #F2EEE6 her white shirt; #111111 Jean's black shirt; #C9A45C dry gold grass at the roadside; #A9ADA6 the silver body of the open two-seater (A11); #B5452E the motel's red OFFICE arrow. Light: hard low sun from the driver's side raking across both seats, then flat dusk; in the motel room, last sun through gaps in boarded windows striping the wall. Framing: close and still at shoulder height across the two seats, hands first; Jean's head turned to the door, sunglasses on, until 40 s; one wide from the roadside when the dust devil crosses. Texture: road dust on the dash and on his black sleeves, sun-cracked seat leather, fine grain. Sound: tires on grit, wind over the open car, her laugh, the phone's shutter. No score (taste note: sound design before music). The title card lands in silence.
- **execution example**: V01 cold open, 90 s, 16:9. Keeps: Lucas's cold open in his order: laughing drive, filming, the eyes, pistol, untouched motel, unloading, wall with none together, food on the table. Look: close and still across the seats, hands first; hard low sun raking both seats; dust on black cloth; tires and wind, no score.
0-5 s [G, hand risky: first try her fingers lace into his and squeeze, his stay open; fallback G, her hand laid over his on the armrest, no grip shown]: close on the armrest; her laugh, off face.
5-14 s [G]: an empty desert town slides past behind him (shuttered pumps, one dead traffic light swinging). She straightens his collar; his head rests on the door, turned away, sunglasses on.
14-24 s [G+C]: over her shoulder, her phone records him 'asleep' in gold light, head toward the door; the shutter clicks; her laugh shakes the picture (C: generic REC dot, no brand UI).
24-32 s [G]: a dust devil crosses the road ahead. She brakes, laughing, and throws her arm across his chest the way you'd catch a passenger; his weight tips into her arm and stays there. She eases him back against the seat and drives on.
32-40 s [G, recurring face; fallback G profile only]: her face for the first time, laughing at the wheel, eyes on him.
40-48 s [G, first try in the moving car (A16); fallback G, parked at the motel]: a bump; his sunglasses slip; his eyes are open, pale and dusted, and don't blink in the wind. The one face shot.
48-56 s [G, hand risky: first try she pushes the glasses back, finds his hand, squeezes and waits a beat while his fingers stay open; fallback G, her hand laid back over his and held there, no grip shown]: she laughs softly and drives on.
56-64 s [G, hand risky; fallback G, pistol visible in the pocket, no hand]: dusk, the peeling MOTEL sign. She walks the frontage alone, pistol grip in her back pocket, and checks the padlock and the unbroken dust on the office step.
64-72 s [G]: she unloads his black bag, her bag and the groceries; through the windshield he waits in the seat.
72-84 s [G]: the room. Cans, water and oranges go on the table. She looks up at a wall of photos of him taken from far off, none of them together.
84-90 s [G+C]: hold on the table and the wall; LIMERENCE on the final frame, silent.
Shortest cut (V02, 15 s, 9:16, muted): 0-1.5 s her fingers close on his and his stay open; 1.5-5 s her phone records him while she laughs; 5-8 s the dust devil, her arm catches his weight; 8-11.5 s the glasses slip to his open eyes; 11.5-13 s she squeezes again and waits; 13-15 s LIMERENCE card.
Stills: S01 has her laughing at the wheel, her hand over his on the armrest; S02 and S03 as the brief specifies.
Recurring device: the squeeze she waits on. In each episode she holds the next man's hand and waits.
CTA: none. The exec should open D01 and ask for the pilot script; a forwarded V02 viewer should open V01.

### R2_stretch_camera_roll (in production): She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.

- **emotional promise**: The pang of her thumb resting on the flip-camera icon over his open eyes, then sliding back: not yet, not like this.
- **product role**: STRETCH. No product; the subject is Venus as the photographer who is never in the frame. Job in frame: her phone, a lover's camera she won't turn on herself. The flip-camera icon she touches and leaves shows the want: before the eyes (18-26 s) it plays as shyness; after them (50-56 s), as 'not like this, not yet'. The wall repeats it without her. The pilot pays it off when the first photo of them together, at the table, is her first use of the front camera (A06). First appearance: the phone's upright frame at 0 s. Her face at 34 s, when she lowers the phone; Jean's at 42 s. Brand assets: LIMERENCE title card on V01's final frame only and in V02's last 2 s. The phone UI (REC dot, running timer, flip-camera icon) is an original design with no OS or maker's marks; owner: production design; clearance pending with the producer under A10, not claimed as cleared. No other logos. Proof: 'The walls where she lives are covered in photos of the man, and none show the two of them together.' 'Limerence is infatuation marked by a desire for the feelings to be returned, not mainly for sex.' 'She carries a gun in her back pocket at the motel.' 'The man is dressed in all black, as her black hole.'
- **visual language**: Rule: while she films, the picture is her phone's screen, a narrow upright frame centred in black with a red REC dot, a running timer and a flip-camera icon (original UI). When she lowers the phone, the frame opens to full width: the world as it is. Palette: #D7261E the REC dot; #111111 Jean's black shirt; #F2EEE6 her white shirt; #C9A45C gold dust on the dash; #3E5670 blue dusk over the road. Light: inside the phone, low gold sun behind her flaring on the lens, sinking to dusk over about an hour of timer; at dusk the phone's own video light, small and hard, on the dust of his black collar. Outside the phone, flat blue dusk. In the motel, one battery lantern on the table. Framing: handheld at arm's length inside the phone, jolting when she laughs, with his head turned to the door, cheek on the headrest, so only collar, jaw and one arm of the sunglasses show until 42 s; outside the phone, locked off and still. Texture: inside the phone, compression, wind clipping the mic, a fingerprint smear on the lens; outside it, clean and quiet. Sound: the phone's mic, with wind roar and her laugh loud and close. When the frame widens, the wind drops to tires and engine heard from outside the car. No score; the title card lands in silence.
- **execution example**: V01 cold open, 90 s, 16:9. Keeps: every must-include beat of Lucas's cold open in his order, built on his own 'taking pictures and videos of this man'; what changes is the camera, since much of the drive is seen as she records it. Look: her phone's upright frame with a REC timer while she films, full width when she lowers it; low gold sun to blue dusk across about an hour of timer; #111111 black shirt, #D7261E REC dot; wind and tires, no score.
0-4 s [G+C]: the phone frame, REC 00:00:04, low gold sun. Jean's head is turned to the door, cheek on the headrest: black collar, jaw, one arm of his sunglasses. The edge of an empty town behind him. Wind clips the mic; her laugh, loud and close, shakes the picture.
4-10 s [G+C, hand risky; fallback G, her hand resting on his collar, no adjusting]: REC 00:06:12. She comes to a full stop at a stop sign nobody else will ever reach; the engine idles. Her free hand enters the frame, brushes dust off his black shirt and straightens his collar. He doesn't stir. She drives on.
10-18 s [G+C]: jump cuts, REC 00:21:40, 00:38:15, 00:52:30. Open desert passes behind him; the sun is lower each time; his head never moves.
18-26 s [C over G, hand risky; fallback C over G: the screen flips to the front camera for under a second and catches both of them, her face cut off by the top edge, his head slumped away from her against the door, sunglasses crooked, then flips back to him]: REC 00:53:10. Her thumb slides to the flip-camera icon, rests there, then slides away. The lens stays on him. She laughs.
26-34 s [G+C]: blue dusk, REC 00:58:40. The phone's video light comes on; the dust on his black collar glints.
34-42 s [G, recurring face; fallback G profile]: she lowers the phone to her lap and the frame opens to full width for the first time: the open car at dusk, the wind dropping to tires. Her face, laughing at the wheel, eyes on him.
42-50 s [G, hand risky, first try in the moving car (A16); fallback G, parked at the motel]: too dark for sunglasses now. She lifts them off and turns his chin toward her with two fingers. His eyes are open, pale and dusted; the wind moves his hair and they don't blink. The one face shot.
50-56 s [C over G, hand risky; fallback C over G: the front camera for under a second, both of them, her face lit by the video light beside his open eyes aimed past the lens, then it flips back to him]: she raises the phone and the frame narrows back to upright. REC 01:04:55 on his open eyes. Her thumb goes to the flip-camera icon, rests there, then slides back. Her laugh, softer.
56-64 s [G, hand risky; fallback G, pistol visible in the pocket, no hand]: full width, last light, the peeling MOTEL sign. She walks the frontage alone, pistol grip in her back pocket, and checks the padlock and the unbroken dust on the office step.
64-72 s [G]: she unloads his black bag, her bag and the groceries. He stays in the seat, head toward the door.
72-82 s [G]: the room by battery lantern. Cans, water and oranges go on the table; above them, a wall of photos of him taken from far off, none of them together.
82-88 s [G]: held frame, nothing moves: the oranges, the cans, the wall. Only the wind outside.
88-90 s [C]: the frame holds; LIMERENCE card on the final frame, silent.
Shortest cut (V02, 15 s, 9:16, native phone frame, reads muted): 0-1.5 s REC 00:06:12 at the stop sign, her hand straightens his black collar; 1.5-4 s the timer jumps to 00:52:30, same pose, lower sun; 4-6 s dusk, her fingers lift his sunglasses off (reframed vertical); 6-9 s his open, unblinking eyes, REC 01:04:55 running; 9-11 s her laugh shakes the frame while his eyes stay in it; 11-13 s her thumb rests on the flip icon, then slides back (fallback: the under-a-second flip to both of them, then back to him); 13-15 s LIMERENCE card.
Stills: S01 from behind her right shoulder: her laugh in three-quarter profile, her phone screen showing Jean with the REC dot, and the real Jean beside it, pale and still in golden light, head toward the door; two of him, none of her; LIMERENCE title only. S02 is the wall, groceries and lantern. S03 as the brief specifies.
Recurring device: the front camera. Every episode opens on her phone recording the next man, and the front camera turns on only once per man, at the table, afterwards.
CTA: none. The exec should open D01 and request the pilot script; a forwarded V02 viewer should open V01.

### R3_brave_last_straw: Decorum isn't her trap; every straw is a chance she's praying he'll take.

- **emotional promise**: The held breath as her fingertip rests on the last straw still standing, hoping he'll save their house.
- **product role**: BRAVE. No product; the subject is Venus as a judge who wants to acquit. Job in frame: the straws, decorum's clock. The cold open braids a silent bar night into the drive; each straw she draws out at the bar cuts to the car. The braid stops with one straw still standing: her fingertip rests on it, and we hard cut to his open eyes. The last straw is his line about love in the white apartment (F16, A18), which D01 plays; V01 shows neither the tin nor the verdict causing the death. First appearance: straws at 0 s, the drive at 4 s, her face at 58 s. Brand assets: LIMERENCE title card on V01's final frame only and in V02's last 2 s. No bar, drink or car logos (A10). Proof: 'The bar game is called decorum; its goal is to see whether the house they build would last.' 'Limerence is infatuation marked by a desire for the feelings to be returned, not mainly for sex.' 'She carries a gun in her back pocket at the motel.' 'The walls where she lives are covered in photos of the man, and none show the two of them together.' That each lie costs a straw is premise content resting on A04, not a verified fact.
- **visual language**: Palette: #B8322A a red fringed lampshade over the bar; #2F6B4F the drinks fridge's green glow; #D9C08A the bar straws; #111111 Jean's black shirt; #C9A45C gold desert grass. Light: in the bar, candles in glasses on the counter and one red shade, with everything past the hands falling to black. In the desert, hard low sun from the side. Framing: close and still on hands at counter height in the bar and at the same height across the car seats, so every hard cut matches hand to hand; Jean's head turned to the door until 54 s. Texture: in the bar, film grain, a vignette and sticky dark wood; in the desert, dust on black cloth. Sound: straws laid on wood, a glass set down, ice, and a murmured room with laughter but no words (A15). In the desert, wind and tires. Each straw drawn out is a cut. No score.
- **execution example**: V01 cold open, 90 s, 16:9. Keeps: the drive, filming, the eyes, pistol, untouched motel, unloading and the wall. It moves decorum into the cold open with no words (question for Lucas) and leaves the last straw standing for D01. Look: hand-matched hard cuts between the candlelit bar and the hard-sun desert; straws on wood and wind; no score.
0-4 s [G]: the bar at night. Two pairs of hands lay the first straws on the bar top as a floor plan; a glass is set down; laughter, no words.
4-10 s [G]: the desert, close across the seats. A bent bar straw sits in his black shirt pocket; his head is turned to the door; she films him 'asleep', laughing.
10-16 s [G]: the bar. His hand adds a straw and the house gets a wall; her hand rests on his forearm.
16-22 s [G]: an empty desert town slides past; she straightens his collar.
22-30 s [G, hand risky; fallback G, the house already smaller, no pull shown]: the bar. His hand turns his glass; her hand pauses over the house, then draws one straw out. He drinks.
30-36 s [G]: the desert. A bump rocks his head against the door; she sets the phone in her lap, rights his head and picks the phone back up.
36-44 s [G]: the bar. Two more straws come out; the house is down to one wall.
44-50 s [G, hand risky; fallback G, the straw already back in the pocket, her hand leaving]: the desert. The wind lifts the bent straw half out of his pocket; her hand presses it back in.
50-54 s [G]: the bar. One straw still standing. Her fingertip rests on it and doesn't pull; the room's murmur drops away.
54-62 s [G, first try in the moving car (A16); fallback G, parked]: hard cut to his open, pale eyes behind slipped sunglasses; they don't blink in the wind. Her face for the first time, laughing as she pushes the glasses back up.
62-70 s [G, hand risky; fallback G, pistol visible, no hand]: the MOTEL sign. Pistol in her back pocket, she checks the padlock and the unbroken dust on the office step.
70-76 s [G]: she takes the bent straw from his pocket, then unloads the bags and groceries.
76-86 s [G]: the room. Groceries on the table with the bent straw laid beside them; the wall of photos of him, none of them together.
86-90 s [C]: LIMERENCE on the final frame, silent.
Shortest cut (V02, 15 s, 9:16, muted): 0-1.5 s the bar, her hand draws a straw out and a wall of the house drops; 1.5-4 s her phone records him asleep, the straw in his pocket; 4-6 s the bar, another straw out; 6-8 s one straw standing, her fingertip resting on it; 8-11 s his open, unblinking eyes; 11-13 s her face, laughing, pushes his glasses back up; 13-15 s LIMERENCE card.
Stills: S03 becomes the route's poster frame; S01 has the bent straw in his pocket.
Recurring device: the straw house, rebuilt with each man. The season question is the house that stands (A17).
CTA: none. The exec should open D01 and request the pilot script.

## Script

A vigilante who falls in love with every unpunished man she sentences drives her latest across the desert, laughing and filming him, still waiting for the dead man to love her back.

### Beat sheet

| Scene | Heading | Who | What happens | What turns |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SC01 | COLD OPEN · EXT. EMPTY DESERT TOWN AND DESERT ROAD, OPEN TWO-SEATER – LATE AFTERNOON TO DUSK (0:00-0:56, V01) | VENUS, JEAN | VENUS wants to film JEAN looking back at her. JEAN, in all black, cheek on the headrest and head turned to the door, wants nothing: he is dead, and his stillness is the obstacle. Seen mostly inside her phone's upright frame, she drives him through an empty town and open desert, laughing hard enough to shake the picture. Tactic 1: she films him close, at arm's length. Tactic 2: at a stop sign nobody else will ever reach, she brushes the dust off his black shirt and straightens his collar. Tactic 3: her thumb moves to the flip-camera icon, then slides away. But the light is going. At dusk she lowers the phone, lifts off his sunglasses and turns his chin with two fingers. His eyes are open, pale and dusted, and they do not blink. She hooks one open arm of the sunglasses into her neckline, raises the phone, laughs, rests her thumb on the flip icon and slides it off again. Her flaw under pressure: she wants him to look at her, but she won't turn the lens on herself. Detail: she stops at a stop sign in an empty desert. | From a woman filming her sleeping lover to a woman filming a dead man she still won't step into frame with. |
| SC02 | COLD OPEN · EXT. ABANDONED DESERT MOTEL – LAST LIGHT (0:56-1:12, V01) | VENUS, JEAN | But she can't bring him in until she knows the place is still hers. VENUS wants proof that nobody has found the motel since she left it. She stops in the carport, leans across JEAN's knees, takes the pistol from the glovebox where she left it outside the bar (SC04) and slides it into her back pocket. She walks the frontage alone, tugs the padlock on the office door and checks the dust on its step, which is unbroken. Only then does she unload his black bag, her bag and the grocery sacks and shoulder open the swollen door of a ground-floor room. JEAN stays in the passenger seat with his head to the door. Detail: the untouched dust on the office step is her proof. | From arming herself and checking to unloading: the place is still hers. |
| SC03 | COLD OPEN · INT. MOTEL ROOM – NIGHT (1:12-1:30, V01; LIMERENCE TITLE ON FINAL FRAME) | VENUS | So she sets up house. VENUS wants the table set for two. By one battery lantern her hands set out cans, water jugs, oranges and a palm-sized photo printer, then pull a second chair up to the table. A bedroll on the floor and a row of emptied jugs by the door show she has been living here. Above the table is a wall of long-lens photos of JEAN: at a bar window, at a gas pump, crossing a street. He is alone in every one, and none show the two of them together. The frame holds on the table, the two chairs and the wall until nothing moves. LIMERENCE title. Detail: the photo printer, planted for SC18. | From breaking into a derelict motel to setting a table for two in a room she has lived in for weeks. |
| SC04 | ACT ONE · INT. VENUS'S CAR (THE SAME OPEN TWO-SEATER, TOP UP), ACROSS FROM A DIVE BAR – THURSDAY NIGHT, THE NIGHT BEFORE (1:30-3:00) | VENUS, JEAN, BARTENDER | The wall was shot from far off, and this is how. VENUS wants to go in before JEAN, drunk over his breakup for the third night running, leaves alone again. Parked across the street, she films him through the bar window, alone at the counter. On her passenger seat are a rubber-banded stack of long-lens prints of him going back months and a photocopied incident report with a woman's name blacked out and 'NO FURTHER ACTION' stamped across it (fictional). Tactic 1: she zooms in on his face as far as the lens goes. Tactic 2: she waits, and he signals for another. But the BARTENDER, who wants to close Jean's tab, sets the tab slip in front of him. She takes the pistol from her back pocket and shuts it in the glovebox beside a small closed metal tin, then takes the tin and pockets it. The pistol stays in the glovebox until the motel (SC02). Detail: she trades the gun for the tin. | From watching him through a lens to walking into his frame without the gun. |
| SC05 | ACT ONE · INT. DIVE BAR – CONTINUOUS (3:00-5:30) · INCITING INCIDENT | VENUS, JEAN, BARTENDER | So she sits down beside him. VENUS wants JEAN to agree to play her game. JEAN wants to finish his drink without being seen. She opens with a test she already knows the answer to. VENUS: 'Have we met before?' JEAN: 'No. Not that I'm aware of.' VENUS: 'Hmm. You seem down. Why don't we play a game?' JEAN: 'Sure.' She lays one straw flat in the wet ring his glass left on the counter. VENUS: 'It's called decorum. The goal is to figure out if the house we build would last. We ask each other questions. Agree, we get a stick. Don't, we take a drink and lose one.' The house is straws laid flat on the counter: four walls and a pitched roof. He stalls with a counter. JEAN: 'Sure we can play, but you haven't gotten your drink yet.' BARTENDER, who wants the order taken and the tab closed: 'What can I get you guys?' VENUS: 'How about I get this first round. Two Naked and Famous.' JEAN: 'So what is in a Naked and Famous?' VENUS: 'Something that'll make you famous for being naked.' Her hand lands on his forearm. VENUS: 'Kidding. Mezcal with a touch of personality. Too strong? I can have him change it to a mimosa.' He props himself up. JEAN: 'Ah. Someone with a little character.' He slides the tab slip back to the BARTENDER unsigned. JEAN: 'Let the game begin. Cheers to having some decorum.' VENUS: 'Cheers.' Detail: the first straw lies in the wet ring from his glass. | From a man drinking to be left alone to a man pushing back his tab and raising a glass to her game. |
| SC06 | ACT ONE · INT. DIVE BAR – LATER (5:30-8:00) | VENUS, JEAN | But the game she runs is a trial he doesn't know he's in. JEAN wants to be the wronged man in her eyes. VENUS wants to hear him tell the truth about his ex. JEAN: 'First question. What is your name, and what do you think it means?' VENUS: 'I was wondering when you'd ask. Venus. Apparently some Roman goddess of love. What about you?' JEAN: 'St. Jean. But just call me J, it's easier.' VENUS: 'I've met a saint. The Lord has answered my prayers. I think we just earned a stick toward our home.' She lays a second straw flat against the first: a wall. VENUS: 'Why do you look so defeated?' JEAN: 'Damn, I'm that easily read. You see it in my eye or my face?' VENUS: 'I felt it in your soul. Damaged, like you'd just been slayed.' She laughs. VENUS: 'A true martyr on a Thursday night, alone in an empty bar. Who slayed you, my friend?' JEAN: 'She left. Packed while I was at work. I never once raised my voice.' VENUS drinks and pulls the wall straw back out. JEAN: 'That's a no?' VENUS: 'That's a drink.' She closes the gap between the stools and leans to his ear. VENUS: 'Why do they call you a saint? Are you willing to save me?' She pulls back, laughing. VENUS: 'I'm just fucking with you. My turn.' His flaw under pressure: he lies to stay the victim. Detail: he denies something nobody asked him. | From a straw earned to a straw taken: her first verdict, which he hears as flirting. |
| SC07 | ACT ONE · INT. DIVE BAR – LATER, BY THE CANDLE (8:00-9:45) | VENUS, JEAN | But she wants a reason to let him pass. JEAN wants to take her home tonight, and he takes her turn: he pulls the bar candle between them so he can see her eyes. JEAN: 'Let's go deeper.' VENUS: 'Sure. My heart's an open book, if you want to read it.' JEAN: 'What makes you tingle inside?' VENUS: 'Ahh. Good one.' She holds his eyes. VENUS: 'A man who doesn't have any fear and is willing to tame a beast.' JEAN: 'What dog doesn't want a true owner?' He picks up a straw and, instead of laying it flat, stands it upright in the candle's soft wax: a chimney he awards himself. Her fingertip rests on top of it, then slides away, and it stays standing. VENUS: 'Who would have known a stranger could be my next lover?' A three-second cut at real speed: her hand on a black shirt against white sheets, no nudity. She shakes her head and is back at the bar. Detail: the chimney straw standing in candle wax. | From judge to lover: her fingertip leaves standing a straw she should have pulled. |
| SC08 | ACT ONE · INT./EXT. DIVE BAR, THROUGH THE WINDOW, THEN THE STREET – LAST CALL (9:45-11:00) · ACT ONE OUT | VENUS, JEAN, BARTENDER | So the house never settles. Seen silent through the window and cut on the laughs, the flat outline grows when he adds a straw and shrinks when she pulls one. Once it has its whole roof; on the next drink her fingertip draws a roof straw out of the line (the S03 frame), and he laughs. Inside at last call, the counter is bare except for the chimney, upright in the candle wax: the only straw still standing. JEAN wants a picture of the night. He holds his phone out at arm's length to get the two of them and the chimney in one frame. VENUS takes the phone from his hand, photographs him alone beside the upright straw and hands it back without a word. The BARTENDER wants the counter cleared and reaches for the glasses. JEAN wants to end the game as the winner and reaches for the chimney. VENUS plucks it first and slides it into her jacket pocket beside the tin. They walk out with their arms tangled and climb into an unbranded rideshare, leaving her two-seater parked across the street. Detail: the last straw standing in candle wax. | From his phone framing the two of them to a photo of him alone and the last straw in her pocket: she has him, and the verdict is still open. |
| SC09 | ACT TWO · INT. RIDESHARE BACK SEAT – NIGHT, DASH CAM (11:00-12:30) | VENUS, JEAN, DRIVER | But the tin and the straw are in the pocket his hands go for. Seen from the dash cam, its timestamp running in the corner, they kiss and grab at each other in the back seat with their clothes on. The DRIVER wants his back seat kept clean: he flicks the dome light on, then watches in the mirror. VENUS reaches up and flicks it off. JEAN wants his hands inside her jacket, and his hand finds the pocket with the tin in it. She takes his wrist and moves his hand to her waist, laughing against his mouth. Detail: the dash cam is a second recording of him, one she doesn't control. | From his hand on the tin to her hand steering it away: the tin stays hers to open. |
| SC10 | ACT TWO · INT. VENUS'S BUILDING, LOBBY AND STAIRS TO HER DOOR – NIGHT (12:30-14:00) | VENUS, JEAN | So he arrives with questions. JEAN wants to know who she is and how she pays for this, but he wants the night more. He takes in the marble lobby and the quiet stairwell. JEAN: 'So what do you-' VENUS: 'Mind the step.' He lets it go. VENUS wants him inside without questions and leads up the stairs a step ahead. VENUS: 'Please don't judge me. It's a little messy.' JEAN: 'We aren't all perfect. Who said Rome was built in a day?' She opens the door on an all-white apartment: white floor, white furniture, no dust anywhere. He stands on the threshold in all black. JEAN: 'I happened to miss the mess. Can I use your bathroom?' VENUS: 'Of course. Right on the left. I'm gonna change and make us something to drink.' Detail: she lines his black shoes up on the white mat by the door. | From a man with questions to a man who stops asking and walks in. |
| SC11 | ACT TWO · INT. WHITE APARTMENT, BATHROOM – NIGHT (14:00-15:00) | JEAN | But the white room has a flaw. JEAN wants to get back out to her fast. He grins at himself in the mirror and checks his teeth. He lifts the white toilet seat: a few small dried red drops on the back of the rim, left from the previous man (A13) and never explained on screen. He tears off toilet paper and wipes, looks at the faint pink on the tissue, drops it in the bowl and flushes. Through the door, ice drops into glasses. Detail: he checks his teeth before he deals with the blood. | From a man who finds blood to a man who wipes it away and walks out smiling. |
| SC12 | ACT TWO · INT. WHITE APARTMENT, LIVING ROOM – NIGHT (15:00-17:00) | VENUS, JEAN | So she sets her last question. JEAN wants her in the bedroom now. VENUS wants to ask the last question first. She waits in an oversized team T-shirt with no logo, bare legs, a drink in each hand; the chimney straw from the bar stands upright in the ice of her own glass. JEAN: 'Did you miss me?' VENUS: 'You haven't shown me anything worth missing.' He takes the other glass, downs it like a shot and blows out hard. JEAN: 'Whew!' VENUS: 'You alright, champ?' JEAN: 'I ain't won a ring yet.' She sets her glass on the white table, the straw still standing, and slides a small metal tin across to him, closed. He turns it over like a prize and tries the lid, but her fingertip stays on it. VENUS: 'Let's take a trip.' JEAN: 'Like the one where we get on a plane, or the one where we have a good time?' VENUS: 'Both. You feel like this space isn't safe?' JEAN: 'No. Not at all.' Detail: the bar straw standing in her ice. | From a drink he can swallow to a tin he can't open: the last straw stands again, in her glass. |
| SC13 | ACT TWO · INT. WHITE APARTMENT, LIVING ROOM – CONTINUOUS (17:00-20:00) · ACT TWO OUT | VENUS, JEAN | But he answers with a line. VENUS wants one true thing about love: his last straw. JEAN wants to get her to the bedroom. VENUS: 'Trust is a thing that is earned. You can't assume things are great. What's glamorous can be a trap for a black hole. Do you think love is built on trust or lust? What makes one lust, the words or the sex appeal?' He inches closer and puts his hand on her thigh. JEAN: 'Hmm.' VENUS: 'Who is to say love has a conditional standard? Who is to say lust doesn't have great intentions? Who created the standard of what's wrong or right?' He reaches for a comeback. JEAN: 'Why does love have to be right or wrong? Why does pain have to be felt? Words have made me stronger, yet pain has made me hopeless. Everyone's standard must belong to who they are within.' VENUS: 'How does that correlate to love, or what I just asked?' JEAN: 'That's for you to find out.' She draws the straw out of her ice and lays it flat on the white table, where it leaves a wet mark. She lifts her fingertip off the tin and opens the lid toward him. What's inside stays out of frame. Detail: the wet mark the straw leaves on the white table. | From a straw standing in her ice to a straw lying flat and the tin open: he gave her a line, and the house is down. |
| SC14 | ACT THREE · INT. WHITE APARTMENT, HALL TO BEDROOM – NIGHT (20:00-21:30) | VENUS, JEAN | So the sentence begins, and the room gives him a second warning. JEAN wants to get her to the bed. He lifts VENUS and carries her toward the bedroom, past the open tin and the flat straw on the table. At the white threshold he looks down: three small dried red drops on the floor. He stops for half a step. VENUS wants him to keep walking; she never looks down. She turns his face back to hers. VENUS: 'J.' He steps over the drops and carries her through. Detail: the drops are at the threshold, where she never looks. | From stopping at the drops to stepping over them. |
| SC15 | ACT THREE · INT. WHITE APARTMENT, BEDROOM AND BATHROOM – LATER THAT NIGHT (21:30-24:00) | VENUS, JEAN | But his breathing won't let her rest. Afterwards, JEAN sleeps face-down in the white sheets, breathing slow and loud, his black clothes on the white floor. VENUS wants to stop hearing his breathing. Tactic 1: she folds his black clothes square on a white chair. Tactic 2: in the bathroom she opens the mirror cabinet. Inside is a four-week pill organiser with every lid still shut and full, and a pharmacy label turned to the wall. She opens one lid, looks, closes it and puts the organiser back. No drug name, no diagnosis. She lies down beside him. His breathing stops for a long gap, then catches with a rasp. Her phone lies on the nightstand beside a small white speaker. Tactic 3: her hand passes the phone, puts a trance-soul track on low from the speaker, then turns it up until his breathing is under the music. Detail: weeks of full compartments. | From a breath that catches while he could still be helped to the music turned up over it: she reaches past her phone and stays in bed. |
| SC16 | ACT THREE · INT. WHITE APARTMENT, BEDROOM – MORNING (24:00-25:30) | VENUS, JEAN | But he doesn't wake. The track is still looping. JEAN lies face-down, mouth open, one arm off the bed, where he has lain since the night. VENUS lies awake beside him, a hand pressed to her forehead. She wants him to turn over and reach for her. Tactic 1: she waits through another loop of the track. Tactic 2: she lifts his arm over her waist, and it slides off. Tactic 3: VENUS: 'J. Stay for breakfast.' He doesn't move. She switches the music off, and the room is silent. Detail: the same four bars coming round again. | From waiting in the bed for him to turn over to getting up to strip it. |
| SC17 | ACT THREE · INT. WHITE APARTMENT – DAY (25:30-28:00) | VENUS, JEAN | So she cleans. VENUS wants the apartment spotless before she takes him away. She dresses JEAN in his black clothes, seats him in the white armchair and puts his sunglasses on him, a plant for SC01. She strips the bed and remakes it in fresh white sheets, pulling the corners tight and smoothing until no crease is left. But at the bedroom threshold she finds the three dried drops JEAN stepped over; she had missed them. She kneels and scrubs until the white cloth is pink and the floor is white, then folds the pink cloth into a square before she bins it. She closes the tin and pockets it with the flat straw. The frame holds on the finished room, with JEAN in black in the white chair, longer than is comfortable. Detail: the pink cloth folded square. | From a room she thought was clean to a flaw she missed and scrubs out: spotless, with a dead man in black at its centre. |
| SC18 | TAG · INT. MOTEL ROOM – NIGHT, CONTINUOUS FROM THE COLD OPEN (28:00-30:00) | VENUS, JEAN | Back at the motel, VENUS wants to get into one photograph with JEAN. JEAN is already in the second chair at the table under his wall, with the lantern and oranges in front of him. She unhooks his sunglasses from her own neckline and sets them down by the oranges. From her bag she takes two long-lens prints of a different man on courthouse steps (fictional) and pins them at the edge of the wall, next to the last photos of JEAN. She sits close, rests her head on his shoulder and raises her phone. Her thumb goes to the flip-camera icon and this time presses it. The screen shows both of them: her face lit by the lantern, smiling, beside his open eyes, which aim past the lens. She turns his chin toward it with two fingers, and his eyes still miss it. She takes the photo anyway. The palm-sized printer whirs out a print, and she pins it between JEAN's photos and the new man's: the only photo of them together. This is the situation the series repeats. Detail: the printer's whir in a silent room. | From never in his frame to in it, while the wall already moves on to the next man. |
