### Writing decisions

| Choice | Why | From |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The pilot runs as a cold open, three acts and a tag across 18 scenes, about 30 minutes. The inciting incident ('Have we met before?') lands at about 3:00. Act One ends with the last straw going into her pocket, Act Two with the tin opening, and the tag with the first photo of them together. | The pilot shape needs 12 or more scenes, the inciting incident in the first sixth and a last scene that shows what repeats. Venus's own choices resolve the climax twice: she opens the tin after his line (SC13), and she turns the music up over his catching breath (SC15). | Craft brief (pilot; the protagonist's choice resolves the climax); A01; A05; A06; A18; story seed |
| The pilot runs on one continuous timeline. The bar (SC04) is Thursday night, the night before the drive. SC05-SC17 run on to the next day, the cold open is that evening, and the tag follows straight on from it. Her car in SC04 is the same open two-seater, with its top up. She shuts the pistol in its glovebox outside the bar and takes it out again at the motel (SC02, V01 56-60 s). | The prints going back months already show how long she has watched him. The gun is accounted for all the way from the bar to her back pocket. | Reviewer note (continuity chain); verified fact (gun in her back pocket at the motel); premise |
| V01 is SC01-SC03 and follows the order of the R2 execution example. Changes: the route's 56-64 s beat is split into the glovebox pistol (56-60 s) and the frontage check (60-64 s). B07 holds only the sunglasses lifted, the chin turned and the eyes. She hooks one open arm of the sunglasses into her neckline in the first second of B08, with no fold, and B08's frame shakes as she laughs. The table gets a photo printer and a second chair, and a bedroll and a row of emptied jugs by the door appear in B12, B13 and S02. | The make-or-break eyes shot (A16) carries no extra hand action. V02-B05's shaking frame now exists in V01's footage. The cold open shows the room she has kept. The printer and chair come back in SC18. | Route R2 execution example and revision log; A14; A16; reviewer note on V01; brief V02 ('rebuilt from V01 footage') |
| The sunglasses carry continuity: she puts them on him in SC17, lifts them off in V01-B07, hooks them into her neckline in B08, and sets them down by the oranges in SC18 before she sits. | She can't lift off sunglasses she is already wearing. The object moves from him to her, so the tag opens with his face bare for the photo. | Reviewer note (continuity chain); craft brief (plant an object that comes back) |
| V01 and V02 have no spoken lines, so every vo field is empty. Her laugh, written in the sound field, is the only voice. | V01 is specified with 0 spoken or lip-synced lines, and V02 must read with the sound off. | A15; brief V01 and V02 labels |
| The phone's REC timer goes in on_screen_text as on-screen UI added in finishing. LIMERENCE is the only title, on V01's final frame (88-90 s) and in V02's last 2 s. | The timer carries the hour-long drive without words, and in V02 it carries the time passing. Generated footage can't be trusted to render legible digits. | Route R2 visual language; brief deliverables; craft brief (words the viewer must read go in on_screen_text) |
| The straw house is defined once in SC05: straws laid flat on the counter as four walls and a pitched roof, the first straw in the wet ring from his glass. It gets its whole roof once and loses a roof straw (SC08, the same moment as S03). Jean's own straw stands upright in the candle wax as a chimney (SC07) and is the last one standing. In SC12 it stands again, in the ice of her glass. In SC13 she draws it out and lays it flat, leaving a wet mark on the white table, then lifts her fingertip off the tin. | Production design can build it without asking, and D01 and S03 show the same house. The last straw goes from standing to lying flat, so the verdict reads with the sound off, and the tin opens only after it (A18). | Reviewer notes (house form; last straw); Lucas's straw game; A04; A18; brief S03 |
| R2's hesitation is carried into the bar while Jean is alive. In SC07 her fingertip rests on the chimney he awarded himself, then slides away, the same rest-and-slide as her thumb on the flip icon. In SC08 she takes his phone when he tries to frame them together, photographs him alone and hands it back without a word. | The want to be in one photograph with him is tested before he dies, which plants SC18's front camera. The scene also reads as a killer's caution and seeds A17: the man she would turn the camera on while he is alive. | Route R2 central idea; reviewer note (R2 in the flashback); A17 |
| Jean's lie at decorum is a new line that Lucas did not write: 'She left. Packed while I was at work. I never once raised my voice.' It is followed by 'That's a no?' / 'That's a drink.' | It shows decorum as a verdict without voiceover. He denies something nobody asked, she takes a straw, and he hears it as flirting. | A02; A04; craft brief (exposition as a weapon); taste note (restraint over explanation) |
| In SC04 she trades the pistol for the tin. The dried blood on the toilet rim and the drops at the threshold belong to the previous man and are never explained on screen. | This makes the tin her chosen method and leaves room for A13 without showing or telling it. | A05; A13; must avoid (showing how the tin's contents are prepared or dosed) |
| SC15: Venus's want is to stop hearing his breathing. The organiser beat plays as written: every lid full, the label to the wall, no drug name or diagnosis. His breath then stops for a long gap and catches, and her hand passes her phone on the nightstand to turn the speaker up until his breathing is under the music. | The want is now a verb and an object. The breath catch is something a microphone can record, so turning the music up reads as her choice to stay in bed while he could still be helped. The meds are seen but never given as the reason. | Reviewer note (SC15); craft brief (want as verb and object; the protagonist's choice resolves the climax); verified facts (meds untaken for weeks; trance-soul to quiet her); must avoid (meds as the explanation) |
| The trance-soul track plays only from her speaker, in SC15-SC16, and she switches it off in SC16. Every other scene carries its own sound: wind, tyres, straws on wood, ice, a glovebox latch, a printer. | The music stays inside the scene, and sound design leads everywhere else. | A08; tone (music); taste note (sound design before music) |
| The horror in the white apartment is its tidiness: dried drops only, on the toilet rim and at the threshold. Jean wipes one set and Venus scrubs the other. What's in the tin stays out of frame. The frame holds on the finished room longer than is comfortable. | The tone calls for clean horror with no spray, no gore and the method off screen, as in ref 05's calm next to horror (going by the brief's description). | Tone (clean); must avoid (gore; the tin's contents or dosing) |
| Brands: the rideshare is unbranded, the T-shirt is an oversized team shirt with no logo, and Lucas's 'Cracker Jack' reference is dropped from Jean's reaction to the tin. 'Naked and Famous' stays as a cocktail name from Lucas's dialogue. | Nothing is cleared yet. The producer confirms whether the cocktail name needs clearance. | A10; must avoid (real brands) |
| The only sex on screen is a three-second real-speed insert of her hand on a black shirt against white sheets, at the bar (SC07). The bedroom scenes start afterwards. There is none in V01, V02 or the stills. | The pitch pieces must be able to circulate. There is no slow motion anywhere. | Must avoid (explicit sex; slow motion); Lucas's 'visions' and 'aftermath' beats |
| The next man's photos are two long-lens prints of a fictional man on courthouse steps. She takes them from her bag in SC18 and pins them up before the printer runs. The photo of her and Jean then goes up between his wall and the new man's. | The new photos come from somewhere on screen. They show the system lost him without any text, and they set up the situation the series repeats. | A06; reviewer note (continuity chain); craft brief (the last scene shows what repeats); must avoid (real cases) |
| Taste notes followed: hands before faces (her face first at 34 s, Jean's only at 42 s as the one face shot); restraint over explanation (no voiceover or code; the meds, the blood and the phone passed on the nightstand are shown, never explained); sound design before music (no score in V01 or V02); dry over sentimental ('That's a drink'); one detail per scene, named in each. Broken on purpose: S01 shows her laughing face, because a poster needs its lead and the laugh beside a dead man is the performance that earns it. The fallbacks for V01-B04 and B08 also show part of her face for under a second, as the route already accepts. | The notes ask for a flag wherever one is broken. 'The product is a tool, then a hero' doesn't apply because there is no product. | Taste notes; route R2 notes |
| S01 carries LIMERENCE only. S02 and S03 carry no text, so their headline and subline are left empty. | The brief specifies 'LIMERENCE title only' for S01 and 'no text' for S02 and S03. | Brief deliverables S01-S03 |


## Cast

### VENUS

- **role in story**: Principal. A vigilante who watches unpunished men for months, then plays decorum with them over a date. She films everything and is never in her own frame. V01 objective: get JEAN to look back at her through her phone. Pilot want: one true thing about love from him, then one photograph with him. Obstacle: he is the man she is sentencing, he answers with lines, and by the cold open he is dead. Change: from never being in his frame to the first photo of them together (SC18), while the wall moves on to the next man. Age 30-38, target 34. Every face reads 30+ at the bar (alcohol, no market code given), and she is old enough to have kept a wall for months and paid for the white apartment. Defaults: I keep Lucas's height, slimness and long brown hair with highlights. I break the genre default (a glossy femme fatale in her late 20s) and the generator's stock face (symmetrical, poreless, smiling at the lens) with pores, laugh lines, one higher brow and marked working hands. She smiles at him, never at us. Heritage: Black American, recorded and never prompted. It is a choice, not a story need; Lucas to confirm (see blockers). Restate it if the setting is Toronto.
- **screen presence**: In one frame: a woman in her mid-thirties drives with one hand and holds a phone up at arm's length toward the man beside her. Her shoulders are loose and her eyes are on him, not the road. The read: in love, not driving. Her hands lead (taste note: hands before faces). They brush dust off his collar and straighten it, and a thumb rests on the flip icon before it slides away. She handles things slowly, and what she puts on a surface lands square: cans on the table, his shoes on the mat, the pink cloth folded before she bins it. Between actions she is still and watching. She laughs with her whole torso, so the phone shakes. At the bar she sits turned fully to him, elbows off the counter, one fingertip on the straws. She never looks down at the drops.
- **performance style**: Naturalistic, played as a love story. The laugh is the only heightened thing: real, from the belly, never a cackle. One laugh voice is locked across V01, V02 and D01: low, breaking high at the end, non-verbal. It syncs to her face only at 34-42 s and in the front-camera fallbacks, and it softens after the eyes (B08). At rest her face stays pleasant and busy with him. No knowing glance, no half-closed stare, no lip bite (taste note: restraint over explanation). Blink: at the bar she blinks less than he does. Beside the dead JEAN she blinks normally, so in a shared frame her blink is the one difference between them. Lens rule: her eyes never go into any lens. That includes the under-a-second flips in the B04 and B08 fallbacks, where they stay on him. The first time is SC18, when she looks into her own front camera while his eyes aim past it. D01: sync dialogue, dry and quick. She plays the bar flirt as genuine because she hopes he will pass (A18), and her verdicts land with no change of face ('That's a drink'). Registers without the laugh: SC15, the organiser gets the same face and hands as the padlock check (V01-B10). She opens one lid, looks and closes it, with no wince, no tremor and no pause on the turned label, so the meds are seen and never played as the reason. SC16 (ref 06, as the brief describes it) is the one scene where her hands have nothing to do. She lies still, a hand to her forehead, eyes on the back of his head, not the ceiling. She says 'J. Stay for breakfast.' at the same dry pitch as 'That's a drink', not pleading. SC17: she cleans with the same unhurried care she gave his collar. Repeated gesture: a digit rests, then slides off. Her thumb does it on the flip icon; her fingertip does it on the chimney straw and on the tin. Its central use is SC12-SC13, where her fingertip holds the tin's lid shut, then lifts off after his line. SC18 is the first time she presses.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: 177 cm: 7 cm shorter than JEAN, 7 cm taller than BARTENDER. Long neck and arms; shoulders narrower than hips. Right-handed; the pistol rides in the right back pocket. US women's 4 / S, tall sleeve length (US sizing assumed; see setting). Constraints: the right sleeve stops above the wrist bone so the whole hand and its scar read in the collar and chin touches (V01-B02, B07). The right back pocket is deep and high enough to show a compact pistol grip above the pocket line while she walks (B10), without dragging the waistband. The V01 neckline holds one open arm of the sunglasses (B08, SC18). The bar jacket pocket takes a small tin and a straw with no visible bulge (SC08-SC09). The SC12 T-shirt covers to mid-thigh and carries no logo (A10). No nudity in any piece. Hair in the open car can be loose or tied (stylist's call), but it is held for all of V01 and never falls over the hand shots.
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Build: 177 cm, long neck and long arms, narrow shoulders over wider hips.; [macro] Natural hair dark brown #3A2417, loose 3B curls, dense, soft rounded hairline. Worn past the shoulder blades with grown-out honey highlights #B98A55 from mid-length (joint lock, stylist). The only long hair in the cast.; [micro] Long face, narrow jaw, heavy straight brows with the right one 3 mm higher; eyes dark brown #4A2E1C.; [micro] Skin #A36A48 at the cheekbone, open pores across the nose and inner cheeks, fine laugh lines at the outer eyes, three small dark freckles on the left cheekbone.; [micro] Hands. Right: long fingers, prominent knuckles and a pale 2 cm scar across the back between the thumb and index knuckles, the anchor for the collar and chin touches (V01-B02, B07). Left: a pale nick across the back of the thumb at its first joint, which reads nail-side over the screen UI where the thumb rests on the flip-camera icon (V01-B04, B08, V02-B06, SC18).
- **fictional talent**: true

### JEAN

- **role in story**: Principal. St. Jean, called J: a serial dater three nights into drinking over a breakup. His ex's domestic-violence report went nowhere (A02, fictional). Bar objective: win the game and take her home. Apartment objective: get her to the bedroom. Want: to stay the wronged man in her eyes and win the night. Obstacle: every lie costs him a straw, and she already knows the answers. Change: from alive to dead, and from trying to frame the two of them (SC08) to being framed with her once he can no longer look (SC18). His V01 job is to be carried: dead weight, with the one face shot at 42 s. Age 34-42, target 38. Lines and a thickening waist show the charm is older than the looks it was built on. This breaks the category default (a sculpted late-20s leading man) and the generator's drift toward young and symmetrical, so his 'hit list' swagger reads as habit. Heritage: Haitian-American, recorded and never prompted. The reason is the name St. Jean and the family D01 can give him. Because heritage is not prompted, it cannot show in his face. He is Black like VENUS so the killing doesn't read as a cross-racial story the pilot isn't telling. Lucas to confirm with S01 and V02 in view (see blockers).
- **screen presence**: In one frame: a big man in black slumped against the passenger door, cheek on the headrest, turned away from her camera so only his left jaw, ear, neck and collar show. Nothing moves but his hair, and he could be asleep. Alive at the bar, he starts with shoulders rounded over the glass, elbow planted, tab slip within reach: a man drinking to be left alone. When she speaks he props up, turns his stool to her and pulls the candle over. His hands grab and claim: straws, one stood upright for himself, his phone held out to frame them both, her pocket. His eyes go to price and surfaces (the lobby marble, the white room) before they go to her. He checks his teeth before he deals with the blood.
- **performance style**: Two registers. Alive (SC04-SC15): naturalistic, a charmer running on habit. He laughs a beat before his own punchlines, and his eyes drift to her mouth and the room. At rest he is slack and heavy with drink until she gives him a game. Sync dialogue, delivered as if rehearsed. The SC06 lie ('I never once raised my voice') comes out easy, with no tell, because he believes it. The white-apartment speech is a man stretching for depth and missing. SC15 is breathing only, recorded wild: slow and loud, one long stop, then a rasp. He is face down, so there is no lip-sync. Dead (V01, SC16-SC18, S01): absolute stillness. No blink, no swallow, no breath in the chest. His eyes are open the same amount in every shot, upper lid at the top edge of the iris, aimed past the lens and never into it. Rigor, an assumption from the timeline (dead most of a day by dusk): his neck is set, so his head never bobs over the road or shifts between cuts. When she turns his chin, no muscle helps. The head gives a few degrees, the shoulders turn with it, and it stops there with no loll. That is why his eyes land past her (B07) and past her front camera (SC18). In SC16's morning his arm is still loose enough to slide off her waist. Silent in V01 and V02.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: 184 cm: 7 cm taller than VENUS, 14 cm taller than BARTENDER, 4 cm taller than DRIVER. Broad shoulders well wider than the hips, thick neck, waist thickening over the belt. He must plausibly lift and carry VENUS (177 cm, slim) in SC14. Right-handed: he holds the phone out and stands the straw with his right hand (SC07-SC08). US men's L, 36 waist (US sizing assumed). Constraints: one black outfit carries SC04-SC18, because she dresses him again in SC17. The black must read as one mass against white rooms and gold light, yet let dust glint at the collar (V01-B05). The collar stands clear of the jaw with his cheek on the headrest, so collar and jaw read as two shapes in 0-42 s. Nothing rides up or gapes while he slumps as dead weight through the cold open. His face must take sunglasses with one arm visible from his left side. His seated head must clear a small 1950s two-seater's windscreen (A11; production design confirms).
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Build: 184 cm, broad shoulders well wider than the hips, thick neck, waist thickening over the belt.; [macro] Natural hair black #17110E, tight 4A curls, dense; squared hairline with the left temple receding further than the right. Worn 8-10 cm on top so the wind can lift it (V01-B07, V02-B04), tapered short at the sides; close beard with a hard cheek line to define the jaw in the turned-head frames (joint lock, stylist; top length subject to the A16 wind check).; [micro] Short, wide face, wide square jaw, heavy low brows that almost meet; irises dark brown #2A1A12, unchanged in death. Dead eyes (V01-B07, B08, V02-B04 to B06, SC18): the upper lid rests at the top edge of the iris in every shot; the cornea is dry and dulled with a faint grey film and no wet shine; dust sits on the lashes. 'Pale, lost' lives in these details, never in a lighter iris (joint lock, makeup/VFX).; [micro] Skin #6E4631 at the cheekbone, visible pores on the nose and cheeks, two faint forehead lines, crow's feet. Dead state (V01, SC16-SC18, S01): the same hex desaturated, grey-ash at the lips, lids and under the eyes, dust in the skin creases; never lightened (joint lock, stylist/makeup).; [micro] A dark, lentil-sized mole on the left side of the neck, below the ear and above the collar. It is the one mark visible in the turned-head frames (0-42 s), and no other character has it.
- **fictional talent**: true

### BARTENDER

- **role in story**: Supporting, one line. Works the dive bar's Thursday late shift. Objective: close JEAN's tab and clear the counter before last call. What only he does: his tab slip is what sends VENUS in (SC04), and the order goes through him. Age 46-54, target 50: well past 30 for the alcohol rule, and twelve years older than JEAN. Both onlookers are men because the script calls each 'him' or 'he'. Heritage: Mexican-American, recorded and never prompted. It is a choice, not a story need: the two onlookers are cast apart from each other and from the leads, so no heritage is tied to one job.
- **screen presence**: In one frame: a thick-set man of about fifty behind the bar, forearms on the counter, eyes on the tab. He wants to close up. He flicks the slip over face-down in front of JEAN without looking at it or at him, pours without watching, and reaches for glasses the moment they are empty. Nothing he puts down lands straight. He looks at the couple only when an order is due.
- **performance style**: Understated, deadpan. At rest his eyes are on the tab, the glasses and the till. One sync line ('What can I get you guys?'), said half to the counter. No reaction to the flirt. At last call he clears glasses without a word; in the SC08 window shots he is background only.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: 170 cm: 14 cm shorter than JEAN, 7 cm shorter than VENUS. Barrel chest, shoulders and hips the same width, heavy forearms. Right-handed. US men's XL. Constraints: forearms bare to the elbow so the hand that flicks the slip reads. No logo on him or behind him (A10).
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Build: 170 cm, barrel chest, shoulders and hips the same width, heavy bare forearms.; [macro] Shaved head with grey stubble #8E8882 at the sides and back; full grey-black moustache (joint lock, stylist). The only shaved head in the cast.; [micro] Round face, wide jaw softening under the chin, heavy greying brows with a pale scar splitting the outer end of the left brow; eyes dark brown #3A2618.; [micro] Skin #B07B55, deep forehead lines, crow's feet, broken capillaries across the nose.
- **fictional talent**: true

### DRIVER

- **role in story**: Featured, silent. A rideshare driver on a night shift, unbranded (A10). Objective: keep his back seat clean; the dome light is his only move. What only he does: he watches them without being part of her game, and his dash cam is the one recording of JEAN that she doesn't control. Age 58-64, target 61 (under 70, so no flag). His silver crown makes him the oldest face and reads at dash-cam resolution. Heritage: white American, recorded and never prompted. It is a choice, for the same reason as BARTENDER's.
- **screen presence**: In one frame, from the cabin-facing dash cam on the windscreen: a thin, grey-haired man sitting upright at the edge of frame, both hands on the wheel, his outline against his side window as streetlight slides past. His right hand rises to the dome switch and flicks it on with one finger; he doesn't turn his head. When VENUS switches it off he leaves it off, and his eyes keep lifting to the mirror.
- **performance style**: Understated, silent. He judges with his eyes only: they lift to the mirror, hold for about two seconds, then go back to the road. No reaction face and no words. The dash-cam frame is his whole performance space.
- **wardrobe fit notes**: About 180 cm; he reads long in the seat, head a hand's width below the roof lining. He is 4 cm shorter than JEAN and 3 cm taller than VENUS, but never stands beside either. Narrow, sloping shoulders; thin arms. Right-handed: his right hand goes to the dome switch. US men's M. Constraints: his head and shoulders must read as a clear silhouette against the driver's side window and passing streetlight at dash-cam resolution. His right sleeve stays clear of the wrist so the hand reads when it rises into the dome light. Nothing branded on him or the car (A10).
- **visual identity anchors**: [macro] Seated high and thin: about 180 cm, narrow sloping shoulders, long neck, a profile that reads against the side window.; [macro] Thinning silver hair #CFC9C0 cropped close, receding high, the scalp at the crown bright under the dome light; clean-shaven (joint lock, stylist). The only silver hair in the cast.; [macro] Right hand: long, bony fingers, swollen knuckles, pale backs with raised veins and brown age spots. It is the one part of him that crosses into the lit cabin, when it rises to the dome switch.; [micro] Long, narrow face, hollow cheeks, heavy grey brows; skin #DDBBA3 with red weathering across the cheeks and nose and a brown age spot at the left temple.
- **fictional talent**: true

## World

### LOC-01

- **description**: Venus's car, a vintage open two-seater roadster with a 1950s shape (A11). In SC04 the black canvas top is up and it is parked across from the bar at night. In SC01-SC02 (V01-B01 to B11, V02, S01) the top is down. It must not look like any real make: long bonnet, rounded wings, low cut-down doors, a plain oval grille with 6 horizontal bars, round lamps, no bonnet ornament, badge holes filled, plain body-colour hubcaps. US, 2026 ASSUMED. It is left-hand drive, so Venus drives on the left and Jean sits on the right with his head to the passenger door. It has a rear plate only (a rear-plate-only state, ASSUMED), and the plate is blank in every shot. She has owned the car for years ASSUMED. The last thing she did before V01-B01 was seat Jean and put his sunglasses on him (SC17). Wear: the black steering-wheel rim is rubbed from #2E2A26 gloss to #5A4636 matte in two 8 cm arcs at 10 and 2 o'clock, where her hands go. The driver's left seat bolster is darkened to #B9A98C over 20 x 8 cm from getting in and out. Dust is set as states under PR-57: the car is clean in the city (SC04). From V01-B01 it carries a gold film, with one 25 cm wiped arc on the passenger side of the dash where her phone forearm rests. That arc is the scene's one real detail. Background: only the road and the scrub move, and there are no other cars. These must stay blank: the plate (no characters), the dash dials (tick marks only, no numerals or logo), the steering-wheel boss and the wing flanks.
- **spatial layout**: Car 4.00 L x 1.55 W x 1.25 H with the top up; windscreen top at 1.10 m. ASSUMED. Walls in plan: A driver door (her entry), B dash and windscreen, C passenger door, D seat backs and boot. The anchor A/B is the driver-side end of the dash. x runs along A toward D (rearward) and y toward C, in cm. Bearing 0° = toward wall C, clockwise. Cabin 115 (x) x 125 (y). Seat cushions sit 30 cm above the floor and are 45 cm wide: the driver's is centred at y 32, the passenger's at y 93. Seat backs are at x 105, and the bone leather headrest pads top out 72 cm above the cushion. Jean's pose: cheek on the passenger headrest at x 108, y 100, face at bearing 0° (toward C, the door). Glovebox: on the passenger dash face at y 76-106, with a 30 x 14 cm drop-down lid hinged at the bottom, its lower edge 50 cm above the floor. She reaches it across Jean's knees (V01-B09). Boot: x 150-215, lid hinged at its front edge. It holds PR-05, PR-06, PR-07 and two PR-22 (V01-B11). SC04: top up, side curtains down, driver window open toward the bar. The car is parked on the far kerb with the driver side facing the bar at 14 m. SC01: the car heads north, so the low west sun is behind Venus (wall A) and Jean's face, turned to C (east), is in shade.
- **palette**: #D9CBB0 dominant: bone semi-aniline leather on the seats, headrest pads and door cards, satin, 30%; #A9ACAA secondary: silver-grey metallic paint on the body, doors and dash, satin (clean in SC04, dulled under dust in V01), 24%; #111111 secondary: Jean's black shirt (stylist), 16%; #2E2A26 neutral: black bakelite steering wheel and glovebox knob, gloss, 5%; #C9A45C accent: desert dust film on the dash top and his collar, matte, 4% in V01 only (PR-57; 0% in SC04); #D7261E accent: REC dot, composited UI only, under 1%
- **product placement**: No product (R2 stretch; no product reference). The device doing the job is PR-01, held in Venus's right hand at arm's length toward wall C with the lens on Jean. The phone UI is composited in finishing. It is an original design with no OS or maker's marks, and its clearance is pending (A10). The upright 9:16 picture is centred between #0A0A0A pillars. A #111111 top bar, 7% of frame height, holds the REC dot #D7261E (2.2% of frame width) and the timer in #F2EEE6. A #111111 bottom bar, 9% of frame height, holds the flip-camera icon at bottom right: a #F2EEE6 outline of two arrows around a circle. Contrast: REC dot on the bar 3.8:1; timer and icon 16:1; Jean's #111111 collar on the #D9CBB0 headrest 11.8:1. Clear zone: the lower-right 15% of the frame, around the icon, holds nothing but her thumb (V01-B04 and B08, V02-B06). Swap test stand-in: the icon is there only because she won't turn the lens on herself.

### LOC-02

- **description**: SC01 (V01-B01 to B08, V02-B01 to B06, the light for S01). US Southwest high desert, 2026 ASSUMED, within a day's drive of the city. A dead crossroads town, empty for decades ASSUMED: one block of six single-storey stucco storefronts, windows boarded, fascias sanded blank. Beyond it, a two-lane blacktop runs north through scrub flats toward low hills. Right-hand traffic, with a faded double-yellow centre line and white edge lines. No other vehicle, person, animal or wire appears in any shot; only the scrub moves in the wind. Wear: crazed asphalt. The stop sign's face is sun-chalked from red to #B8453A, with its white letters intact. A sand drift lies across the northbound stop line, and no tyre crosses it until hers (PR-58). One real detail: the stop sign nobody else will ever reach (V01-B02).
- **spatial layout**: Exterior, no walls. x runs north along the road and y east, in m. The road is 7.3 m wide (two 3.65 m lanes), and the car stays in the northbound lane. The town block is on the east side (Jean's side) at x 0-45, set back 4 m from the edge line, with fascias 4.2 m high. The crossroads is at x 120, with an east-west gravel road. The stop line is 1.2 m before the crossing, and PR-58 lies across it. PR-10 stands on the east shoulder 2.2 m off the edge line, the bottom of the sign 2.1 m above the road, facing south. With the car at the stop line, the sign stands about 3 m ahead and to the right of Jean's head, behind him in the phone frame. Beyond x 150: scrub flats, with hills 2-5 km to the east. The sun is low in the west, behind the driver; by V01-B05 it is blue dusk.
- **palette**: #9A8466 dominant: caliche ground and dry scrub, matte, 36%; #D8B77A secondary: low-sun sky and haze, V01-B01 to B04, 24%; #3E5670 secondary: blue dusk sky, replacing it from V01-B05, 24%; #4A4744 secondary: crazed asphalt, matte, 18%; #C8B79A neutral: bleached stucco storefronts, flat, 12%; #B8453A accent: sun-chalked stop-sign sheeting, 1%
- **product placement**: No product. The stop sign carries the scene's one detail (V01-B02). The only word in this location is 'STOP'. The storefronts, road and hills carry no text. The only background motion is the scrub, which stays lower in contrast than Jean's black collar in the same frame.

Its only legible text is verbatim 'STOP', white, composited if the letters distort. No stickers or graffiti. | S1 V01-B02, V02-B01: static, in the same condition in every shot. |

No other tracks. |


### LOC-03

- **description**: SC02 (V01-B09 to B11). An abandoned US Southwest roadside motel, built about 1958 and shut about 25 years ago ASSUMED (ref 08 as the brief describes it): a two-storey stucco room block with a gutted upper floor, a single-storey office, a long carport, a peeling sign on steel legs and dry palms. Venus has used room 3 for weeks (LOC-04). History in objects: the office step's unbroken dust is her proof that nobody has come (V01-B10). Her own traffic has left one bare track, 40 cm wide, #A89878 packed against #B7A98C loose dust, running about 5 m from the carport bay to her door. Background: palm fronds and scrub move, and a loose sign panel ticks. The highway stays empty, with no vehicles or people.
- **spatial layout**: Site 60 x 35 m. Wall A is the highway frontage (south). x runs east along A and y north, in m; bearing 0° = north (toward C). The driveway enters at x 17-24 on wall A. Sign PR-12 stands on steel legs at x 8, y 3. The MOTEL box is 6.0 x 1.8 m with its top at 9 m; the OFFICE arrow is 3.0 x 0.8 m, at 5 m, pointing east. Office: 6 x 6 m at x 9-15, y 12-18, with its door centred on the south face at x 12 and PR-14 in front at y 11.6-12.0. Room block: x 16-59.2, y 18-27, two storeys and 6.5 m high, with 12 ground-floor rooms 3.6 m wide (LOC-04). Each room's door is at the east end of its frontage. Her room is the third, at x 23.2-26.8, with door PR-15 at x 25.7-26.6 facing 180°. Carport: x 16-30, y 12-18, a corrugated roof at 2.6 m on steel posts at 3.6 m centres. The car noses in facing 0° at x 21.0-22.55, y 13.5-17.5, its passenger side (Jean's turned head) toward her door; the boot is 5 m from the door. Her walk in V01-B10 runs from the car west along the frontage to the office step, about 10 m. Palms stand at x 3, 34 and 46, y 5. Open desert lies to the north. US, right-hand traffic.
- **palette**: #CFC3A8 dominant: bleached stucco on the room block and office, flat, 32%; #B7A98C secondary: gravel and dust forecourt, matte, 24%; #7F8FA3 secondary: last-light sky, 20%; #5B6B4A neutral: dry palm fronds and scrub, matte, 8%; #6E6A63 neutral: galvanised sign legs and carport posts, weathered, 6%; #A83A2C accent: faded red enamel OFFICE arrow, peeling, 2%
- **product placement**: No product. The only legible text in this location is 'MOTEL' and 'OFFICE', verbatim. No room numbers, vacancy sign, brands or graffiti. The pistol grip (PR-02, #2B2B2B matte) has to read in her back pocket. Request to the stylist: light-wash denim around #8FA3B8, which gives 5.5:1. Dark denim would lose the grip.

### LOC-04

- **description**: The motel room: SC03 (V01-B11 to B14, S02) and SC18. Room 3 of the 1958 block ASSUMED, shut about 25 years and stripped (no bed frame, no TV). Venus has used it on and off for about five weeks ASSUMED, counted by the nine emptied gallon jugs. It is where she builds the wall and brings each man afterwards (A14). Walls: the period motel finish, knotty-pine tongue-and-groove paneling in 15 cm vertical boards, aged to amber #7A5A3C under dull satin varnish; the pins go straight in. Outlets are two-slot, ungrounded NEMA 1-15 with brown bakelite plates, and dead; one plate is missing on wall B at y 120, 30 cm up. The last thing before V01-B12: she left days ago with the bedroll laid out and the wall up. Wear, placed inside the held frame toward wall C: a chalky dust film #A08D72 over the fir floorboards, except two bare tracks 45 cm wide, dull satin #5C4A38, where her feet go (from the door north to the table's east end, and from the table's west end to the head of the bedroll). Three overlapping jug rings, 16 cm across, in dull #9C8468 on the table's west end. A paler 60 x 80 cm rectangle #A88458 on wall C at x 283-343, heights 155-235, where the motel's own picture hung for decades and the pine never ambered. About 20 empty pin holes, 1 mm with 3 mm dark halos #4A3626, along the east edge of the photos at x 278-284, where she re-pinned the last column as the wall grew; they read in SC18's close views. Background: nothing moves, and the wind pushes at the door. One real detail: PR-25, the printer, planted for SC18.
- **spatial layout**: 3.6 W x 4.8 D x 2.6 H ASSUMED. Walls clockwise: A south (entrance), B west, C north, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C. Wall A: the window at x 40-140 (sill 95, head 200) is boarded with plywood #8C7A5E, leaving a 15 cm gap at the top that shows the dark sky. Door PR-15 at x 250-340 is hinged at x 340 and swings in toward D, sweeping x 250-340, y 0-90; it sticks at 35° until shouldered. View out: the carport and the car. PR-05 and PR-06 sit under the window at x 40-140, y 20-48, outside the view toward C. Wall D: the nine emptied jugs PR-23 stand in a row at x 340-355, y 102-237, just past the door's sweep. PR-19 S1 is against wall D at x 315-360, y 280-325, facing 270°. Wall B: bedroll PR-26 at x 10-80, y 190-400, with the wall-B section of the photos (PR-20) above it at y 205-380, heights 150-234. Wall C: the main photo wall (PR-20) at x 15-278, heights 95-239. Bare pine at x 280-345 (with the paler rectangle) is left empty for SC18. Table PR-17 (120 x 70 x 74) at x 120-240, y 330-400, 80 cm off wall C. Chairs PR-18 and PR-19 are on the table's north side with their backs to wall C; the chair backs, at 88 cm, sit below the photos' 95 cm bottom edge. They face 180°, with seat centres at y 420, x 150 and 210 (seats 45 wide). Tabletop at V01-B12: PR-16 at x 180, y 365; PR-24 at x 130-160, y 340-360; PR-21 at x 182-198, y 382-397; PR-22 at x 206-238, y 382-397; PR-25 at x 200, y 340, its slot facing 180°. Planned held view (V01-B13, S02; placement is the DP's call): from near the door facing 0°, with wall C square on, the bedroll and the wall-B photos at frame left and the jug row at frame right.
- **palette**: #7A5A3C dominant: knotty-pine paneling, aged amber, dull satin varnish, on walls C, B and D around and above the photos, 30% of the S02 master; #A08D72 secondary: dust film over the fir floorboards, chalky flat, 14%; #1E2833 secondary: night-street tones in about half the prints (bar window, streets at night), gloss photo paper, 14%; #A09A90 secondary: daylight concrete and asphalt in the other half (gas-pump apron, crosswalk), gloss photo paper, 11%; #5E4632 neutral: oiled pine table and chairs, worn satin, 12%; #111111 accent: Jean's black clothes, the figure in every print, gloss, 4%
- **product placement**: No product. The jobs here belong to PR-20 (none of them together) and to PR-25 with PR-27, the pilot's payoff (A06). Lantern #F2EEE6 on the #5E4632 table: 7.6:1. The bare pine at x 280-345 on wall C, with the paler rectangle, plants the next man: it stays empty and unpinned until SC18. Frame lock: V01-B13 and S02 name more than five props, which is over the craft limit but forced by the script. Lock order: PR-20, PR-16, PR-24, PR-25, PR-19. PR-21, PR-22, PR-23 and PR-26 are held by count and position only. Nothing moves in V01-B13 to B14.

### LOC-05

- **description**: The dive bar and its street: seen from the car in SC04, then SC05 to SC08 and S03. A dive bar in an unnamed US Southwest city, 2026, open about 40 years ASSUMED. It is in a 1950s single-storey strip of painted brick #7E4B3A with a flat stucco parapet, with one fan palm in a sidewalk cut-out. The streetlights are LED cobra heads, so the only amber on the sidewalk is the bar's own window spill. Inside is a long, narrow regulars' room, going by refs 01-03 as the brief describes them: amber wall sconces, a red neon strip over the back bar, a green-glowing glass-front bottle fridge, candles in glasses on the counter, a chalkboard menu smudged past reading. It is Thursday and near empty: three patrons in silhouette at the far end and one bartender. It is Jean's third night on the same stool. Wear: the varnish is worn to raw #6B4A30 in a 40 x 25 cm patch at Jean's stool, from regulars' elbows. Dozens of faint dull rings #5A4030 mark the counter. The brass footrail is rubbed bright #C9A15A in front of each stool. Background: the patrons move slowly, stay lower in contrast than the straws and never cross the straw house or the candle. One real detail: PR-30 standing in candle wax.
- **spatial layout**: 6.0 W x 14.0 D x 3.2 H ASSUMED. Walls: A street front (south), B west, C back, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm. Wall A: glazed door at x 470-560, swinging in toward D. A plain front window with no lettering at x 40-440, sill 80, head 270. View out: the street, and 14 m away on the far kerb, Venus's car (LOC-01) with its driver side to the bar. Wall B: back-bar shelves at y 150-1250 (x 0-45), the bartender's aisle at x 45-100, and the counter at x 100-160, top at 107 cm, running y 200-1200. Ten stools at 90 cm centres from y 260, seats at 76 cm. Jean is on stool 2 (y 350), in the window's sightline; Venus takes stool 3 (y 440). PR-31 starts at x 115, y 260. The straw caddy is at x 130, y 480. The straw house is centred at x 130, y 395, between their glasses. The red neon strip is at 210 cm, y 250-900; the fridge at y 950-1050. Wall C: restroom door at x 400-480. Wall D: booths, with sconces at 190 cm. Street: an unnamed US Southwest city ASSUMED, right-hand traffic, two lanes plus parking lanes (12 m kerb to kerb), 3 m sidewalks, LED cobra-head streetlights (4000K ASSUMED) and one fan palm at x 620. The rideshare (LOC-06) stops at the bar's kerb, x 0-500, with its right side to the kerb.
- **palette**: #1A120D dominant: brown-black shadow on walls and ceiling, flat, 42%; #3A2418 secondary: varnished counter, satin, 16%; #C8873A secondary: amber sconce and candle glow on brass and glass, 10%; #9E1B1B accent: red neon strip, 3%; #2F6B4F accent: green bottle-fridge glow, 3%; #EFE9DC accent: white paper straws, uncoated matte, 1%
- **product placement**: No product. Here the straws are the job: decorum is read in straws. White straws #EFE9DC on the #3A2418 counter give 12:1, so the house reads with the sound off in S03 and through the window in SC08. In S03, nothing but her fingertip moves within 30 cm of the house; the chimney (PR-30 S1) may stand at the frame edge. These must stay blank: every bottle label on the back bar (blank cream #D8CFBC paper), the fridge glass and its contents, the chalkboard (smudges only), the neon (a plain line, no words), the coasters and the window.

### LOC-06

- **description**: SC09. The rideshare's back seat, seen from a cabin-facing dash cam. A generic late-model mid-size sedan, dark grey #2F3236, with no badges and no rideshare stickers, logos or trade dress (A10). The driver's history: a clean cabin, the rear seat cloth pilled to #3A3D42 fuzz over 25 x 15 cm at both outer bolsters, a plain black phone mount on the dash. Background: cool LED streetlight slides across the side glass (no sodium, as in LOC-05), and nothing outside is legible. The cam cannot see the mirror's face, so the driver's watching reads as his own face at the lower edge of frame, eyes lifted to the mirror. One real detail: the dash cam, a second recording of Jean that Venus doesn't control.
- **spatial layout**: Cabin 1.50 W x 2.00 L x 1.15 H ASSUMED. Walls: A is the right side, the kerb side; the right rear door at x 40-110 is their entry. B is the rear shelf and back glass. C is the left side, toward traffic; its doors stay shut. D is the windscreen and dash. Anchor A/B, the right rear corner; x along A toward D (forward) and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C. Rear bench cushion x 20-70, y 5-145, seat at 35 cm. Jean gets in first and slides across to y 100, behind the driver. Venus sits at y 40 by the door, so her left jacket pocket (PR-03) is on his side. Front seat backs at x 100. The driver sits front left at y 95-135 (US left-hand drive). Dash cam PR-36 is at the top of the windscreen, x 172, y 55, 20 cm right of the mirror (y 75), lens facing 270° (rearward) onto the bench. Rear dome light PR-37 is in the headliner over the bench at x 55, y 75, 110 cm above the floor, within Venus's reach.
- **palette**: #0E0F12 dominant: unlit cabin, headliner and pillars, 48%; #2C2F33 secondary: charcoal cloth rear bench, matte, 20%; #111111 secondary: Jean's black shirt, 12%; #B9C3CC accent: cool LED streetlight sliding across the glass and faces, 4000K ASSUMED, 5%; #E9E4D8 accent: lit dome-light lens, one beat only, 2%
- **product placement**: No product. PR-03 is present but never framed: it stays in her left jacket pocket, on Jean's side, where his hand finds it through the fabric. These must stay blank: the car's badges, the windscreen stickers, the dash cam body, the phone mount and the driver's phone screen (off).

### LOC-07

- **description**: SC10. Venus's building: the lobby and the stairs up to her door. A renovated 1920s downtown mid-rise in the same unnamed US Southwest city, 2026 ASSUMED, where she has lived about two years ASSUMED. At night there is no doorman, and no residents are seen or heard. Honed white marble, satin brass, a quiet stair. Wear: the marble is polished to a gloss path 90 cm wide from the street door to the stair foot (#EDEAE4 gloss within #E9E6E0 honed). One real detail: the marble curtail step at the stair foot, rounded and projecting, the step she warns him about ('Mind the step').
- **spatial layout**: Lobby 8.0 W x 6.0 D x 4.2 H; floor to floor 4.40 m ASSUMED. Walls: A street (south), B west, C north, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C. Wall A: glazed double street door in brass frames at x 300-480, swinging in. Wall B: mailbox bank PR-38, 120 x 90 cm, at y 150-270, bottom at 100 cm. Stair: a marble U-stair in the north-east corner with 120 cm flights, 25 risers of 17.6 cm (4.40 m) and 28 cm treads. Flight 1, 13 risers, rises north along wall D at x 680-800, y 120-456. Its first riser is the curtail step, rounded on the open side and projecting 20 cm to x 660. The half landing is at x 560-800, y 456-600, at 229 cm. Flight 2, 12 risers, rises south at x 560-680, y 456-148, through an open well in the lobby ceiling to the floor-2 landing at x 560-800, y 20-148. Her door PR-39 is on the landing's east side, facing 270°. A satin brass handrail runs on the wall side at 90 cm. The gloss path runs from the street door to the curtail step. Sockets: US NEMA 5-15.
- **palette**: #E9E6E0 dominant: honed white marble floor and treads, 38%; #F4F2EE secondary: white eggshell stair-hall walls, 30%; #B08D57 secondary: satin brass handrail, mailboxes and door frames, 8%; #9A9A98 neutral: grey marble veining, 6%; #111111 accent: Jean in black, 5%
- **product placement**: No product. These must stay blank: the mailbox cards, the door and any wall directory. The marble is the only thing here that reads as money, and nothing names what she does.

### LOC-08

- **description**: The white apartment: entry, living room and hall to the bedroom. SC10 (from the door), SC12-SC14 and SC17. This is her lure flat (A14), on the second floor, white on white; nothing sits on any surface unless a beat needs it. Kept about two years ASSUMED and cleaned daily. Before SC10 she vacuumed the rug, leaving fresh stripes, and filled the ice bucket. She missed the three drops at the bedroom threshold (PR-45), which belong to the previous man (A13). Wear, all of it marks of cleaning: 30 cm vacuum stripes on the rug, alternating matte and sheen. After SC17, the scrubbed 30 x 30 cm patch at the threshold is flatter and whiter (#F4F3EF matte) than the satin floor (#EFEDE8). A taste note is broken on purpose: there is no dish rack and no charging cable, because the tone makes tidiness the horror (the 'clean' tone line; ref 05 as the brief describes it). One real detail: Jean's shoes lined up on the white mat. Background: the sheer curtains hang still, and the city beyond shows no legible signage.
- **spatial layout**: 7.0 W x 9.0 D x 2.8 H ASSUMED. Walls: A entrance (south), B west, C north, D east. Anchor A/B; x along A toward D and y toward C, in cm; bearing 0° = toward C. Wall A: door PR-39 at x 60-150, hinged at x 60, swinging in toward B; PR-40 at x 65-145, y 5-55. Wall B: the bathroom door (LOC-09) at y 120-200 ('right on the left'). PR-44 stands at the table's north end, x 110-190, y 555-635, facing 180°, so her knees are about 35 cm from Jean's. A 200 x 300 rug at x 60-260, y 380-680 runs under the sofa's front legs, the table and the armchair. Wall C: a floor-to-ceiling window at x 80-420 with sheer white curtains and the night city beyond; the bedroom door (LOC-10) at x 540-630, threshold strip at y 895-900. The hall runs x 480-700, y 600-900, open to the living room; PR-45 sits just inside the threshold. Wall D: the kitchen run at x 640-700, y 150-450, white lacquer counter at 91 cm, with PR-59 at x 670, y 300 and PR-60 at x 650-680, y 470-500. ## Looks

### VENUS_L01 (VENUS)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: mid-to-high tier, bought plain and kept for years. She spends on fit, deep pockets and boots, never on a name, and dresses in the whites of her apartment plus pale denim. Story: Thursday night, early October 2026 (assumed), SC04-SC10, city at about 19 °C: jacket on and open, ankles covered. She changes off screen in SC11. Head to toe: bone moleskin jacket, single-breasted, 4 buttons worn open, small point collar. It is waist length, with the hem level all round 1 cm above the top of the jeans waistband. No outside pockets; one inside left pocket. Sleeves cut to mid-forearm with a 4 cm turned-back cuff. Under it, a chalk jersey shell with a high round neck, sleeveless with 7 cm shoulders, tucked in. Pale-blue high-rise bootcut jeans, full length. Brown-black belt, showing below the jacket hem. Brown-black pull-on ankle boots: 4 cm stacked heel, almond toe, shaft 3 cm above the ankle bone, worn under the jeans. Ecru ankle socks and an unlined bra, both hidden. Older than the story: the jeans.
- **materials colors**: Jacket: cotton moleskin 330 g/m², brushed face, matte, holds its shape; bone #D6CCBA (approx L*82, C*10). Fronts half-lined in cotton drill 200 g/m², natural #D8CFBE. Facings in the moleskin. Buttons: bone corozo #D2C7B2, matte. Inside left welt pocket, set across the facing and lining, with its drill bag bar-tacked at both bottom corners. A flat chain weight in the hem facing of both fronts, so they hang the same with or without PR-03. Shell: cotton-silk single jersey 160 g/m², solid, chalk #EEE9DF (approx L*93). It is plain jersey because a fine rib would moiré. Jeans: rigid 100% cotton denim 12.5 oz/yd², light stonewash, pale blue #8FA3B8 (approx L*66, C*13, h 260°), as LOC-03 asked. Tonal ecru thread. Plain back pockets: no decorative stitching, patch or tab. Rivets and shank button unstamped, gunmetal #5B5D60 approx, satin. Older than the story: the right back pocket is faded to #A9B9C9 in a 12 x 9 cm outline of the pistol, and its top edge is frayed. Rebuild by sanding and bleach-rubbing the outline and sanding the edge to white threads. It is a detail, not an identity mark, because models mirror. Belt: 3 cm bridle leather, brown-black #2E2724 (approx L*16, C*4), 5 holes. Roller buckle in blackened steel #2F3032 approx, matte. Boots: calf leather, brown-black #2E2724, hidden inside elastic gore, no hardware. Toes scuffed and re-polished, dark sole edge, no coloured sole. Socks: cotton, ecru #E4DED2. Bra: unlined, tan-brown #9C6A4C approx, matched to her skin. No logo, lettering or trade dress on any piece.
- **fit notes**: 177 cm, US women's 4/S, tall sleeve. Jacket: shoulder seam at the shoulder point, 10 cm ease at the chest. The fronts hang straight and open, 2 cm off the shell, so the tin prints no edge. The hem sits 1 cm above the waistband top, which is 7 cm above the tops of the back pockets, so all 6 cm of PR-02's grip clears it in SC04, seated or reaching back (PR-02 S1 as written). Inside left pocket: welt 14 cm wide, 2 cm above the armhole base. Bag 22 cm deep, ending 2 cm above the hem; her armhole base sits 22 cm above the hem line. PR-03 rides at the bottom. From SC08 the 20 cm PR-30 stands beside it, its top 2 cm under the welt. The jacket ends above her lap, so no sit fold crosses the pocket on the bar stool, in the car or in the rideshare, and the straw reaches SC12 uncreased. The pocket is on her left front, the side toward JEAN in the rideshare (SC09: 'inside her jacket'). Cuff: mid-forearm, edge 12 cm above the wrist bone, so both wrists, both hands and the right-hand scar read (SC05 forearm touch, SC07 chimney, S03 fingertip). In S03 no cuff comes within 30 cm of the straw house. Shell: 4 cm ease, neckline 2 cm below the collarbone notch. Jeans: waistband top 2 cm below the navel, 2 cm ease at the hip, fitted through the thigh. Bootcut from the knee to a 23 cm opening; hem 1.5 cm off the floor at the heel. Back pockets 15 x 14 cm, tops 6 cm below the waistband top, so the grip's 6 cm ends level with the waistband, 1 cm under the jacket hem. Belt on its middle hole in every look. The boots add 4 cm: she stands 181 cm, about 5 cm under JEAN in his shoes.
- **accessories**: Nothing worn. Ears, neck, wrists and both hands bare: no ring, watch or bracelet. Her hands touch the phone, his forearm, the straws and the tin (taste note: hands before faces).; Nails bare and short: 1-2 mm free edge, filed square, buffed.; Set-owned: PR-01 phone; PR-03 tin, then PR-30 straw with it, in the inside left pocket; PR-02 pistol in the right back pocket until the SC04 swap.; Car key loose in the right front jeans pocket, no fob or keyring. No bag, no sunglasses.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: medium-deep, warm undertone, #A36A48 approx at the cheekbone (casting hex). No base. Pores stay open across the nose and inner cheeks. The three freckles on the left cheekbone and the laugh lines are left uncovered. No contour, highlighter or powder. Brows: heavy and straight, brushed up with clear gel; the right one sits 3 mm higher and is left that way. Eyes: brown-black kohl #2A211C approx pressed into the upper lash line only; no wing, lower liner, mascara or lashes. Lips: bare, clear balm. Hair: dark brown #3A2417 approx, dense 3B curls with honey highlights #B98A55 approx from mid-length. Loose past the shoulder blades, centre part, set wash-and-go with curl cream and diffused. Curls defined, never stretched or straightened. Do not lighten the skin, de-age her or smooth the texture.
- **continuity locks**: [wide] Bone jacket open, all 4 buttons undone, hem at the waist 1 cm above the waistband, brown-black belt showing below it: bone over pale blue.; [medium and inserts] Cuff edge at mid-forearm, 12 cm above the wrist bone. Both wrists and hands bare.; [close-up] Centre part, curls loose. In every counter hand insert (SC05-SC08, S03) the hair sits behind both shoulders.; [close-up] Kohl on the upper lash line only, no wing. Lips bare.; [SC08-SC09 medium] Left front hangs straight; no drag at the inside pocket, and no straw tip above its welt.; [SC04 insert, reaching back] Pistol grip fully clear of the jacket hem, 6 cm above the right back pocket.

### VENUS_L02 (VENUS)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: the one thing at home she has worn for years. Story: SC12-SC16, the apartment at about 21 °C, night into morning. Head to toe: one oversized man's athletic T-shirt, raglan sleeves to the elbow, crew collar rib with a V-notch at centre front, hem at mid-thigh. Bare legs and bare feet; nothing else shows. The raglan line and the V-notch make it read as a gym or team shirt in a wide shot. Which team (Lucas's Raptors shirt) stays lost until A10 clears it or a fictional team is designed and cleared; this is flagged to Lucas. No team colour, logo, number or lettering. Older than the story: the T-shirt.
- **materials colors**: Combed cotton single jersey 180 g/m², solid, garment-washed. Body: ecru #E8E3D8 (approx L*90, C*4). Raglan sleeves, collar rib and V-notch: oatmeal #D2C9B7 (approx L*81, C*10, h 85°), so the diagonal from underarm to collar reads in a wide. Raglan seams twin-needle coverstitched in tonal ecru thread. Collar: 2 cm 1x1 rib with a 4 cm V-notch insert; no mesh, which moirés. Older than the story: more than 100 washes. The collar rib ripples, the side seams twist 2 cm toward the front, and the front hem hangs 1 cm longer than the back. Rebuild with an enzyme wash and by stretching the rib by hand. Modesty brief for production: seamless, #9C6A4C approx to her skin, never visible. Rule broken on purpose: the craft rule asks for a colour apart from the set. Both tones sit 5-14 L* under the walls #F4F2EE and the sheets #F8F7F4, so she belongs to the white room, her skin and dark curls carry her read, and JEAN stays the only dark mass (verified: 'The colour theme is white'; tone: clean). Story-red check (no product): the drops #6E1C18 are approx L*24, C*43, h 34°. Ecru clears by chroma at C*4; oatmeal clears at C*10 and sits 51° away.
- **fit notes**: A men's XL on a US women's 4. Raglan, so there is no shoulder seam: the sleeve line runs from the underarm to the collar, and the cloth drops past her shoulder point. 40 cm ease at the chest. V-notch point 2 cm below the collarbone notch. The sleeve hem sits at the elbow so both forearms and hands read: SC12 with the two glasses and her fingertip on the tin lid, SC15 turning the speaker ring, SC16 her hand to her forehead. Standing, the hem is at mid-thigh. Seated or carried (SC14) it rides no higher than 15 cm above the knee. Lucas's 'no underwear' is implied, never shown. In bed (SC15-SC16) the tee stays on under the duvet.
- **accessories**: Nothing worn. Ears, neck, wrists, hands and feet bare.; Nails as VENUS_L01: bare and short.; Set-owned props she handles: two PR-43 tumblers, PR-03 with her fingertip on the lid, PR-30, PR-01 and the PR-54 speaker (SC15).
- **hair makeup**: Skin: medium-deep, warm undertone, #A36A48 approx. No base. Pores, freckles and laugh lines as VENUS_L01. The L01 kohl is still on, upper lash line only. Hair: dark brown #3A2417 approx, 3B curls with honey highlights #B98A55 approx, loose past the shoulder blades, centre part, never straightened. SC12-SC13: she has pushed the curls back behind both shoulders with her fingers, and the hair tech resets them there between takes. SC15, from when she lies down: curls flattened at the back of the head. SC16 morning: crown frizzed into a loose halo, back flattened, kohl softened and smudged 2 mm below both lower lash lines, lips dry. No fresh makeup.
- **continuity locks**: [wide] Ecru tee to mid-thigh with oatmeal raglan sleeves: the underarm-to-collar diagonal reads. Bare legs and feet. JEAN is the only dark mass in the room.; [medium] V-notch at centre front of the oatmeal collar rib. No logo, number, lettering or team colour anywhere, front or back.; [insert] Sleeves end at the elbow. Both hands bare.; [SC12-SC13 tin and glass inserts] Curls behind both shoulders; no hair crosses PR-03, PR-30 or the PR-43 tumblers.; [SC16 close-up] Kohl smudged below both lower lash lines. Crown frizzed, back of the head flattened.

### VENUS_L03 (VENUS)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet as VENUS_L01: the same jeans, belt and boots, with a fresh shirt. Story: the day after the bar, early October 2026 (assumed). SC17, the apartment at 21 °C: at the end of the scene she puts the bone jacket on to pocket the tin and the flat straw. V01, V02 and S01, the open car at 27 °C in the late afternoon falling to 17 °C at dusk: the jacket is off and folded in the boot, never seen. SC18, the motel at night, about 15 °C: nothing changes. Head to toe: white man-tailored button-front shirt, point collar, 7 buttons. The top two are open and the third is fastened at the sternum. Tucked in all round, sleeves rolled in two 6 cm turns. With it, the pale-blue bootcut jeans, brown-black belt, ankle boots and ecru socks from VENUS_L01. No sunglasses of her own.
- **materials colors**: Shirt: cotton poplin 120 g/m², solid, matte, white #F2EEE6 (route palette; approx L*94, C*3). Buttons: shell-white corozo #EDE8DE. Poplin creases, so the creases are locked states. S1, all of SC17: pressed, no creases. S2, from V01-B01: two horizontal creases across the front just above the belt (the seated fold) and one diagonal crease in each inner elbow above the roll. S2 holds unchanged through V01, V02, S01 and SC18 and is never pressed out between shots. The shirt stays clean: no pink, no dust. Jeans, belt, boots and socks as VENUS_L01: pale blue #8FA3B8 with gunmetal #5B5D60 approx satin rivets; brown-black #2E2724 with the blackened-steel #2F3032 approx matte buckle; ecru #E4DED2. The pistol-worn right back pocket is older than the story. Hair elastics: two, fabric-covered in ecru cotton #E2DBCB, no metal. Jacket (end of SC17 only): bone moleskin #D6CCBA. Story-red check (no product): the REC dot #D7261E is approx L*47, C*82, h 37°. The shirt (C*3) and the belt and boots (C*4) clear by chroma; the denim is 223° away. Pistol grip #2B2B2B on the denim: 5.5:1.
- **fit notes**: Shirt: shoulder seam at the shoulder point, 12 cm ease at the chest. Tucked 10 cm deep, so it stays in when she leans across JEAN's knees (V01-B09) and walks the frontage (B10). Sleeves: unrolled, they end at the wrist bone. Two 6 cm turns put the roll's lower edge 12 cm above the wrist bone, the same landmark as the L01 cuff, so the right-hand scar and the left-thumb nick read the same at the bar and in the car. The roll band is 6 cm tall. Neckline open down to the third button at the sternum. From the first second of V01-B08, one open arm of PR-04 hooks over the placket edge above that button, lenses hanging outside the shirt, until she unhooks it in SC18. Belt on its middle hole, as in L01. From V01-B09 the pistol grip shows 6 cm above the right back pocket, with nothing covering it. Clear zone: no cuff, hair or strap enters the lower-right 15% of the phone frame; only her thumb does.
- **accessories**: Nothing worn. Ears, neck, wrists and both hands bare: no ring, watch or bracelet. Her hands touch the phone, his collar, his chin and the glovebox (taste note: hands before faces).; Nails bare, short, filed square. The right-hand scar and the left-thumb nick stay uncovered.; Set-owned: PR-02 in the right back pocket from V01-B09; PR-04 hooked in her neckline from V01-B08 to SC18; PR-01.; No sunglasses, hat, headscarf or bag worn.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: medium-deep, warm undertone, #A36A48 approx. Washed: no base, no kohl, no powder. Pores, freckles and laugh lines show, and her own heat sheen on the forehead and nose is left alone. Brows brushed up with clear gel. Lips: clear balm. Hair: dark brown #3A2417 approx, dense 3B curls with honey highlights #B98A55 approx. Gathered into one low puff at the nape, about 18 cm across, held with the two ecru elastics. Smoothed back from the hairline along a centre part with gel. The curl texture stays; never straightened. Nothing hangs loose at the face or neck, so the wind can't blow hair across her hands or the phone (the casting bible leaves this to the stylist). Holds unchanged from SC17 through V01 to SC18.
- **continuity locks**: [wide: V01 34-42 s and S01] White shirt tucked in all round, pale denim, brown-black belt showing, no jacket (the jacket is seen only at the end of SC17): white over pale blue.; [insert, phone-frame edges] Sleeves rolled, lower edge 12 cm above the wrist bone. Both wrists and hands bare.; [medium] Crease state: S1 through SC17 pressed; S2 from V01-B01, two horizontal creases just above the belt and one at each inner elbow.; [close-up] One low puff at the nape, centre part, no loose strands at the face or neck.; [medium, V01-B08 to SC18] PR-04 hooked at the centre of the neckline, above the fastened third button.; [medium back view, from V01-B09] Pistol grip 6 cm above the right back pocket, shirt tucked clear of it.

### JEAN_L01 (JEAN)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: mid-price and bought to be looked at. He spends on the shirt and the shoes and skimps on the trousers and belt, which he hasn't replaced since he got heavier. Story: Thursday night into Friday, early October 2026 (assumed), SC04-SC15. The city is at about 19 °C and the bar and apartment are warm: one black outfit, no jacket. Head to toe: black long-sleeve dress shirt, semi-spread collar on a 4 cm stand, no chest pocket, 7 buttons. Top button open, second fastened, cuffs buttoned, tucked in all round. Black flat-front trousers, mid rise, straight leg, half break. Brown-black belt on hole 1, the hole nearest the buckle (the loosest). Black mid-calf socks. Brown-black leather Derbies (PR-41). Plain black boxer briefs. SC15: clothes off (PR-56), bare back and arms, the white sheet at his waist. Older than the story: the belt.
- **materials colors**: Shirt: mercerised cotton twill 150 g/m², matte, blue-black #24262B (approx L*15, C*4, h 260°). Collar band 43 cm, removable stays. Buttons: matte black corozo #26272B. Trousers: cotton-stretch twill 280 g/m², matte, blue-black #25272C, in the shirt's cast so the two read as one mass (verified: 'The man is dressed in all black, as her black hole'). Boxer briefs: cotton-modal jersey, black #232428, plain waistband, no lettering. Socks: cotton-nylon, black #232427. Belt: 3 cm cowhide, brown-black #2A2624 (approx L*15), 5 holes 2.5 cm apart, buckle in matte black oxide #2B2C2E approx. Older than the story: a deep crease with cracked grain at the middle (third) hole, where it fastened before he got heavier, and the tip (tongue end) worn pale #5A4A40. Rebuild by bending and sanding. Derbies (PR-41): calf leather, brown-black #2A2624, stitched welt, black sole edge, no coloured sole, no logo; this hex replaces the set's #151515. The route's #111111 is the graded read. In a neutral grade the fabric is #24262B, which gives 9.5:1 on the bone headrest #D9CBB0. Nothing branded.
- **fit notes**: 184 cm, US men's L, 36 waist. Shirt: shoulder seam at the shoulder point. With the top button open the collar stands 1-2 cm clear of his neck. Cuffs at the wrist bone. Tucked 10 cm deep, the front bloused 2 cm over the belt where his belly sits over it. The button line pulls across the belly but never gapes: a hidden snap sits between the 4th and 5th buttons. Trousers: waist 3 cm below the navel and snug (he has outgrown them), 20 cm leg opening, half break on the Derbies, with room in the seat to lift and carry VENUS (SC14). Belt on hole 1, nearest the buckle and 5 cm out from the old crease, which sits just past the buckle. SC10: shoes off on the mat (PR-41 S2), socks on. Builds SH-1 and TR-1 (listed under JEAN_L02).
- **accessories**: Nothing worn: no watch, ring, chain or bracelet. Neck and hands bare; his right hand holds his phone and stands the straw.; Set-owned: PR-35 in his right front trouser pocket, no bulge above the pocket line.; No sunglasses in SC04-SC15; PR-04 goes on him in SC17.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: deep, warm undertone, #6E4631 approx at the cheekbone (casting hex). No base. Left uncovered: the pores on the nose and cheeks, the two forehead lines, the crow's feet, the under-eye puffiness from three nights of drinking and the forehead shine. Hair: black #17110E approx, dense tight 4A curls, 8-10 cm on top, tapered to 1 cm at the sides and back. No part, no razor line. Squared hairline, the left temple receding further than the right. Beard: close at 3 mm, with a hard razored cheek line and a neckline 2 cm above the Adam's apple; the 3 mm moustache joins the beard. The lentil-sized mole on the left side of the neck stays uncovered. Do not lighten him, de-age him or loosen the curl.
- **continuity locks**: [wide] All black head to toe, shirt tucked with a 2 cm blouse over the belt, belt showing. No jacket, no jewellery, no watch.; [SC04-SC15 close-up] Top button open, second fastened (against JEAN_L02's fastened top button). Collar on its stays; mole on the LEFT side of the neck above the collar.; [insert, belt] Fastened on hole 1; the cracked crease at the middle hole sits 5 cm past the buckle.; [close-up] Beard 3 mm with a hard cheek line. Hair 8-10 cm on top, sides 1 cm.; [SC15 wide] Clothes off and on the floor, then folded on the white chair. Bare back and arms, sheet at the waist.

### JEAN_L02 (JEAN)

- **garment silhouette**: Story: the same shirt, trousers, belt, socks, briefs and Derbies as JEAN_L01; the production builds differ (see fit_notes). SC16 morning: undressed, face-down under the sheet, right arm off the bed. SC17, the apartment at 21 °C: VENUS dresses him and seats him in the armchair with PR-04 on. V01, the open car in the desert, 27 °C falling to 17 °C: long sleeves, because she dressed him. SC18: the motel at night. He is dressed her way, not his: every button fastened, including the top one, with no tie. Cuffs buttoned. Tucked flat, with no blouse over the belt. Belt on hole 1, where he wore it. The fastened top button is the change the camera sees: it sits at the collar her hand straightens in V01-B02, and he'd never have done it himself.
- **materials colors**: As JEAN_L01: shirt twill 150 g/m², blue-black #24262B; trousers stretch twill 280 g/m², blue-black #25272C; belt and Derbies brown-black #2A2624; buckle black oxide #2B2C2E approx; socks #232427; briefs #232428. Build hardware: SH-4 snaps in flat black oxide #26272B approx, matte; TR-2 invisible zip, black nylon coil #232428. Fold state from PR-55, pressed into SH-2, SH-3 and SH-4: one horizontal crease across the chest 12 cm below the collar and two vertical creases 10 cm in from the side seams. Desert dust is set-owned PR-57, #C9A45C approx, matte. C1: a film on the shoulders and collar points at V01-B01. C2: her hand brushes the points back to blue-black at B02. C3: a fresh film on the points from B05, glinting under the video light, through B08, B11 and SC18.
- **fit notes**: Builds: all in the L01 fabrics, buttons and stays. He is seated or slumped in every L02 frame. SH-1, hero alive: JEAN_L01, SC04-SC15, then PR-56 S1-S2 (on the floor, then folded). Collar band 43 cm; its top button is never fastened on screen. SH-2, hero dead: collar band cut 1 cm larger (44 cm) so the fastened top button closes flat on his thick neck, with the mole still clear below the ear. Serves the SC17 held frame in the armchair, V01-B01 and B03-B14, V02, S01 and SC18. SH-3, dust repeat: identical to SH-2 and pre-dusted to C1 by production design (PR-57). It is swapped in for each retake of V01-B02 and V02-B01, so every brush take starts from C1 while SH-2 holds its later state. SH-4, dressing double: as SH-2, with a centre-back split from the collar band to the hem, closed by 7 flat snaps under a 3 cm underlap, unseen from the front and sides. Used in SC17 while she dresses him on screen, and for loading him into the car seat and the motel chair. Never used in V01 0-56 s, where the phone sees the back of his collar. TR-1, hero: JEAN_L01, SC04-SC15, including the SC14 carry. TR-2, dressing double: invisible zip in the right side seam from waistband to hem. Same waist as TR-1, 2 cm more seat ease so the seat seam doesn't strain as he slumps. Used in every L02 frame; in V01 the right seam lies against the passenger door. Belt x2: the hero and a matched copy, with the crease and pale tip rebuilt the same way. Derbies: the hero pair, plus a pair half a size up with the laces pre-set loose, for SC17 onward. Fit: slumped against the door, cheek on the headrest, the collar stands clear of the jaw on its stays, so collar and jaw read as two shapes (V01 0-42 s). The shirt is tucked 10 cm deep and flat all round; nothing rides up or gapes. Cuffs buttoned at the wrist bone. Seated, the trouser break rests on the Derby vamp. SC17: seated upright in the armchair, hands on the arms. SC18: seated upright in PR-19.
- **accessories**: PR-04 (set-owned): on him from SC17 to V01-B06, only the left arm showing to the phone. Lifted off at V01-B07 and never on him again.; Nothing else worn: no watch, ring or chain; hands bare.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: deep, warm undertone, #6E4631 approx, never lightened. The same hex desaturated, with a grey-ash wash at the lips, the lids and under the eyes. Lips dry and matte, nail beds greyed. Pores, forehead lines and crow's feet show. Eyes (V01-B07, B08, V02-B04 to B06, SC18): open, the upper lid at the top edge of the iris in every shot. Irises dark brown #2A1A12, unchanged. The cornea is dry and dulled with a faint grey film and no wet shine (joint lock with VFX). From V01-B07, dust #C9A45C sits on the lashes, in the lid creases, the forehead lines and the crow's feet, matching the collar. Hair: black tight 4A curls, 8-10 cm on top so the wind lifts it (B07, V02-B04), sides 1 cm, no part, a light dust film at the hairline in V01. Beard 3 mm with the hard cheek line, unchanged. The mole on the left side of the neck stays uncovered. SC16, face-down: the same grey-ash at the lips, mouth open. Do not lighten him or loosen the curl.
- **continuity locks**: [phone frame 0-42 s, V02, S01] Top button fastened: seen from his left side, the collar band runs unbroken to the front (against JEAN_L01's open top button, SC04-SC15). Collar stand clear of the jaw.; [phone frame, V01 0-42 s] Mole on the LEFT side of the neck, 2 cm above the collar band, facing camera. A mole on the right means the frame is mirrored: reject it. Only the left sunglasses arm shows, up to B06.; [phone frame] Collar dust follows PR-57: C1 at B01, C2 at B02, C3 from B05.; [close-up] Eyes open, upper lid at the top edge of the iris, no blink, dust on the lashes, no wet shine.; [wide] All black, tucked flat with no blouse over the belt, cuffs buttoned, no jewellery.; [medium, from SC17] One horizontal fold crease across the chest, 12 cm below the collar.

### BARTENDER_L01 (BARTENDER)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: work clothes bought in multipacks and worn until they give out. He spends on his shoes because he stands for ten hours. Story: Thursday late shift, early October 2026 (assumed), SC04-SC08, the bar at about 24 °C: short sleeves, forearms bare. Head to toe: slate short-sleeve crew-neck T-shirt, tucked in, sleeves to mid-bicep. Tobacco bib apron with a neck strap: bib up to the top of the sternum, hem at the knee, ties knotted at the back. Charcoal work trousers, mid rise, plain hem. Dark grey socks. Brown-black slip-resistant leather work shoes. Older than the story: the apron.
- **materials colors**: T-shirt: heavyweight cotton single jersey 220 g/m², solid, matte, slate #56657A (approx L*42, C*14, h 268°). Apron: waxed cotton duck 340 g/m², tobacco #6A4E36 (approx L*35); the neck strap and ties are sewn on, no hardware. Older than the story: the wax is worn off in pale cracks #8A7058 along the waist fold and at the corner of the bib pocket, and the ties are hand-darkened for 20 cm either side of the knot. Rebuild by flexing and sanding the wax. Trousers: cotton twill 280 g/m², charcoal #3D3E40. Socks: cotton, dark grey #4A4B4C. Shoes: leather, brown-black #2E2724, plain rubber sole. Pen: plain black plastic #1F1F21, matte, no print. Bar towel: cotton huckaback, off-white #E6E2D8. No logo, bar name or lettering on any garment, the pen or the towel (A10).
- **fit notes**: 170 cm, US men's XL: barrel chest, shoulders and hips the same width. T-shirt: shoulder seam 2 cm past the shoulder point, 12 cm ease. The sleeve hem sits at mid-bicep, so both forearms are bare to above the elbow for the tab-slip flick and the pours. Tucked. Apron: neck strap at the base of the neck, bib flat to the top of the sternum, ties at the natural waist. From the stools, the 107 cm counter hides everything below the apron waist. Trousers: 2 cm ease, the plain hem touching the shoe.
- **accessories**: Hands and forearms bare: no ring, watch or bracelet.; The black pen clipped in the bib pocket.; The off-white bar towel tucked into the apron tie at his right hip.; Set-owned: PR-34 tab slip and tray.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: medium, warm undertone, #B07B55 approx. No base. Left uncovered: the deep forehead lines, the crow's feet, the broken capillaries across the nose, and the pale scar splitting the outer end of the left brow. Head shaved to 1 mm, with grey stubble #8E8882 approx at the sides and back. Full grey-black moustache #4A4642 approx with grey through it, trimmed to the corners of the mouth and no further. No beard; jaw shaved. Heavy greying brows, untouched. Do not add hair or de-age him.
