Tobacco bib apron on, neck strap at the base of the neck.; [close-up] Head shaved to 1 mm with grey stubble at the sides. Moustache to the corners of the mouth. No beard.; [any frame] No lettering or logo on the tee, apron, pen or towel.

### DRIVER_L01 (DRIVER)

- **garment silhouette**: Closet: comfortable, bought once and kept. He spends nothing on clothes and keeps his car cleaner than his shirts. Story: Thursday night just after last call (about 01:47), early October 2026 (assumed), SC09. The cabin is at about 20 °C with the air on: short sleeves. Head to toe: sage short-sleeve button-front shirt, point collar, 6 buttons, top button open, tucked in. A white crew undershirt shows at the open neck. Grey-brown twill trousers, mid rise, brown belt. Grey socks. Brown leather walking shoes. Everything below the chest is out of the dash-cam frame. Older than the story: the shirt.
- **materials colors**: Shirt: cotton poplin 115 g/m², solid, sage #7D8A74 (approx L*56, C*14, h 131°); buttons in tonal plastic #76826E, matte. Older than the story: the left shoulder and left sleeve are sun-faded to #97A28E from the driver's window, and the collar edges are soft. Rebuild by bleaching and sanding the left side. It is a detail, not an identity mark, because models mirror. Undershirt: cotton single jersey 150 g/m², white #E9E7E1 (approx L*92). Trousers: cotton twill 260 g/m², grey-brown #6E665A. Belt: leather, brown #4A3A2E; buckle in brushed steel #8C8B87 approx, satin. Socks: cotton, grey #6A6A68. Shoes: leather, brown #4A3A2E, plain rubber sole. No rideshare badge, lanyard, logo or lettering (A10).
- **fit notes**: About 180 cm, US men's M: narrow sloping shoulders, thin arms. Shirt: the shoulder seam lands 1 cm past the shoulder point because of the slope; 14 cm ease. The sleeve hem sits at mid-bicep, so his right forearm and hand are bare when they rise into the dome light. Tucked, the collar open one button to show the undershirt's crew. He sits upright with both hands on the wheel, and his shoulder line reads as a clear slope against the side window.
- **accessories**: Hands and wrists bare: no ring or watch. His right hand rises to the dome switch.; No glasses, lanyard, badge or rideshare marks (A10).; Set-owned: PR-36 dash cam and PR-37 dome light.
- **hair makeup**: Skin: light, warm undertone, #DDBBA3 approx. Left uncovered: the red weathering across the cheeks and nose and the brown age spot at the left temple. No base. The crown scalp is left unpowdered, so it shines under the dome light. Hair: thinning silver #CFC9C0 approx, straight, cropped to 1.5 cm, receding high, no part. Clean-shaven. Heavy grey brows, untouched. His right hand keeps its raised veins, age spots and swollen knuckles.
- **continuity locks**: [dash-cam frame] Sage shirt, top button open, white undershirt crew showing. Right sleeve at mid-bicep, forearm bare.; [dash-cam frame] Silver crop receding high, crown scalp showing. Clean-shaven. No glasses.; [dash-cam frame] No badge, lanyard or lettering anywhere on him.

## Director's treatment

### SC01

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat B07 (42.0-50.0): close on her right hand turning his chin; his open eyes are the one face shot. Across the scene she wants him to look back at her. Two things stop her: he is dead, and she won't turn the lens on herself. B01 (0-4): she wants to wake him to the drive. She laughs loud on the mic, the way you laugh at a lover sleeping through the best part, and the frame jolts. B02 (4-10): she wants to make him presentable. She comes to a full stop at a sign nobody else will reach, 50 cm short of the untouched sand drift (PR-58), brushes the dust off him and straightens his collar. Her hand rests there a beat before she pulls away. Real detail: she obeys the stop line on an empty road and still finishes dressing him. B03 (10-18): hold. She keeps filming across three jumps and he never moves; the joke is that the pose never changes. B04 (18-26): she wants into the picture. Her thumb rests 2.2 s on the flip icon, played as shyness: a lover who won't appear in her own home video. It slides off and she laughs. B05 (26-34): she wants to keep seeing him as the light goes. Her index finger presses the phone's side button and the video light comes on him. B06 (34-42): the first time we see her face. She laughs at the wheel with her eyes on him, not the road. No knowing look, no lip bite, and her eyes never go to any lens. B07: she wants him to look at her, and the dark is her excuse to take off the sunglasses. Her laugh stops and she holds his chin 1-2 s. This is not shock; she already knows. B08 (50-56): she goes back behind the lens. She takes his sunglasses to herself, laughs more softly, and rests her thumb only 1.2 s ('not like this, not yet'). JEAN is absolutely still: no blink, no breath, and rigor has set his neck.
- **blocking**: LOC-01 in LOC-02 with the top down, travelling north in the northbound lane. The low sun is in the west, behind the driver's side. VENUS sits in the driver seat (y 32), left hand on the wheel in the worn 10 o'clock arc. PR-01 is upright in her right hand at arm's length, just west of Jean's left shoulder, lens aimed east across it, so the east-side storefronts (PR-11) and the stop sign (PR-10, east shoulder) sit behind his head. JEAN has his cheek on the passenger headrest (x 108, y 100) and his face to the passenger door (east), PR-04 on. The lens sees only his collar, left jaw, left sunglasses arm and neck mole. The wind is relative, nose to tail. B02: front tyres stop 50 cm short of PR-58, with PR-10 about 3 m ahead and east of his head. Line of action: the road, with the car always heading north. Every full-width setup stays west of the car's centreline. B06 and B07 up to 45.0 are on the driver's (west) side: she is nearest the lens and he shows only the back of his head and the glasses. From 45.0 the B07 axis sits ahead of the windscreen, on or just west of the centreline, looking back south along the road. The chin turn brings his face from east to north-east, up the road's east verge, 45° off the door. From this axis it reads as a three-quarter with both eyes. His gaze runs past the lens on its east side and never reaches her. Rigor holds that set for the rest of V01 and SC18. B08: her right hand holds the phone forward of his face on the windscreen side, a hand's width west of his gaze line, lens aimed back at his eyes, so they sit centred in frame and aim past the lens. For 9:16: the phone-frame shots are natively upright. In B07, her hand and his face stay inside the middle third on both axes, because V02-B03 uses 42.8-43.8 and 45.0-46.0. Her face may fall outside it.
- **action progression**: Entry at 0.0: PR-01 recording with the light off, collar in state C1, dash in D2, PR-04 on him. Left hand on the wheel, right hand on the phone. B01, 0.0-4.0, one plate: hold on collar and jaw (1.8); a full-torso laugh jolts the frame (1.6); frame settles (0.6). B02, 4.0-10.0: the car rolls to a full stop and the frame dips (0.8); her left hand leaves the wheel and enters under the lens (0.7); two strokes brush the shoulder and collar points back to black, C1 to C2 (1.2); she pinches the left collar point flat (1.0); her hand rests on the collar (0.8); hidden cut at 8.9 as the withdrawing hand wipes the lens (0.4); the frame lurches as she pulls away over PR-58 (1.1). Plates: 4.0-8.9 and 8.9-10.0. Left hand back on the wheel. B03, 10.0-18.0, three plates: 10.0-12.6 storefronts (a second pass through the block), 12.6-15.3 open desert, 15.3-18.0 scrub hills. The head pose is identical in all three, the dust builds toward C3, and each cut lands on a snatch of laugh. B04, 18.0-26.0: hold (0.8); right thumb slides onto the flip icon (1.0); rests (2.2); slides back to the frame edge (0.8); laugh jolt at 22.8, with the plate join at 22.9 hidden in its blur; laugh (2.0); frame settles on him (1.2). Plates: 18.0-22.9 and 22.9-26.0. B05, 26.0-34.0: dusk, noisy picture (2.7); her right index finger presses PR-01's side button, off picture because the picture is the rear lens (0.3); at 29.0 the video light comes on and the plate join hides in the exposure pop; C3 dust glints as the car vibrates (5.0). Plates: 26.0-29.0 and 29.0-34.0. B06, 34.0-42.0: the frame tips toward her lap in a short, mostly blurred plate (0.8); the pillars open to full width (0.6); the phone lies face up in her lap, still recording with the light on; her right hand rests on her thigh; she laughs at the wheel with her eyes on him (6.6). Cut at 39.8, inside the laugh. B07, 42.0-50.0, plate 1 on the west side: her right hand goes from her lap to his face (0.8); lifts PR-04 off by the left arm, clear of his right ear (1.2); drops to his jaw with the glasses held open in her palm (1.0). Cut on action at 45.0 to the front axis as two fingers meet his chin. She turns it: the head gives a few degrees, the shoulders follow, and his face stops pointing north-east with no loll (1.4). Hold on the open eyes, hair moving, no blink (3.6). Her left hand stays on the wheel throughout. B08, 50.0-56.0: her right hand hooks the open left arm of PR-04 into her neckline at centre, lenses out (1.0); lifts PR-01 from her lap (1.0); the frame closes to upright (0.4); inside it his eyes are centred under the light and the frame shakes as she laughs (1.0); thumb to the icon (0.8), rests (1.2), slides back (0.6). Plates: 50.0-52.4 full width on the front axis, and 52.4-56.0 phone frame, generated long for V02.
- **emotional shift**: Hers. Before (B01-B06): laughing, filming, eyes on him. Trigger (B07), which the camera sees: she turns his chin and his open eyes land aimed north-east, past the lens and never at her. After: no laugh for 8 s, and her fingers stay on his chin a beat. In B08 she takes his sunglasses to herself and goes back behind the lens. The laugh returns softer and the thumb rest is shorter (1.2 s against 2.2 s), so the same gesture now reads 'not like this, not yet'. B04's rest is a hold: no trigger, same state, shyness. Up to B07, the viewer's shift from lover to dead man is carried by his stillness, not by any change in her.
- **transition in**: The pilot opens with no fade: from black straight into the phone's upright frame at REC 00:00:04. Her laugh and the wind on the mic start on frame one.
- **transition out**: Hard cut with a time jump to last light. B08's phone frame (thumb leaving the icon, wind on the mic) cuts to B09's locked-off carport at full width. The mic wind cuts dead and the cooling engine's ping starts on the cut. The eye goes from his eyes at the centre of the upright frame to the car under the MOTEL sign at centre.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' B04 and B08 carry it: twice, her right thumb rests on the flip-camera icon over him, then slides back. Cut this and the viewer loses the want: a woman films a man for an hour and can't turn the lens round. Without it, the wall, the table for two and SC18's press read as a collector's trophies.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Phone UI (REC dot, timer, flip icon), B01-B05 and B08. Method: composite in finishing over G plates; original design, clearance pending (A10). Acceptance: the timer reads 00:00:04, 00:06:12, 00:21:40, 00:38:15, 00:52:30, 00:53:10, 00:58:40 and 01:04:55, runs continuously within each beat, and shows no OS or maker marks. The video light is switched by PR-01's side button in B05, off picture, so no UI element changes state before SC18. Risk: generated digits or icons come out garbled.; Thumb on the icon (B04, B08; V02-B06). Method: a practical plate of a hand double's right thumb over a blank phone on green, composited over G. Acceptance: the thumb enters nail-side, rests 2.2 s (B04) or 1.2 s (B08) without a twitch, and never presses, so the UI never changes state. Risk: a generated thumb presses or morphs and spends SC18's payoff. Fallback: the script's under-a-second front-camera flip, with her eyes on him and never in the lens.; Collar touch, B02 (V02-B01). Method: G; the hand is risky. Acceptance: one hand, two strokes; the collar points go from dusted to #111111 while the shoulders keep their film (C1 to C2); his head does not move under her touch. Risk: extra fingers, or a nod that reads alive. Fallback: her hand rests on his collar with no adjusting.; Dead eyes, B07-B08 (V02-B04/B05). Method: G, locked in composite. Acceptance: zero blinks and zero eye movement across 45.0-50.0 and 52.4-56.0; upper lids at the top edge of the iris; dry, dulled corneas with dust on the lashes; gaze aimed north-east, past the lens on its east side; hair moving while the eyes hold. If a take fails, freeze the eye region from the best frame and keep the hair and background live. Risk: one blink kills the reveal. A16: first try in the moving car. The fallback is parked at the motel, where the wind comes from the highway (south) and lifts his hair toward the windscreen; don't match it to the moving plates.; Axis change at 45.0, from the west side to ahead of the windscreen. Acceptance: both setups sit west of the car's centreline, Venus is nearer the lens in both, and his face in the front-axis plate reads as a three-quarter with both eyes, not a profile. Risk: a setup east of the centreline crosses the line and puts his gaze into the lens.; Three-step hand chains, B07 (lift the glasses, hold them open, turn the chin) and B08 (hook the glasses, lift the phone, thumb). Method: G; hands risky. Acceptance: PR-04 stays in her right hand across the 45.0 cut and is hooked, lenses out, by 51.0; it never vanishes or doubles. Fallback: it is already hooked at 50.0, cut on the lift.; Vehicle. Method: G. Acceptance: one open two-seater with a 1950s shape, left-hand drive, a blank rear plate, no badges and no numerals on the dials, travelling north in the right-hand lane; no other vehicle, person or animal in any frame. Risk: a real make's grille or a badge (A10).; Jump-cut registration, B03. Method: one locked G of Jean over varied background plates. Acceptance: head, collar and sunglasses overlay within a few pixels across all three; only the sun's height and the background change.; PR-10 'STOP': composite the letters if the generator distorts them. No other legible text in LOC-02.; Laugh sync, B06, the only sync in V01. Acceptance: laugh audio peaks align with the visible torso shakes within 2 frames. One laugh voice is locked across V01, V02 and D01.; Hair in wind: hers is held (stylist) and never falls over the hand shots. His top hair must move in B07: it is the life in the frame that the eyes don't have.; No slow motion anywhere; the laugh, the drive and the eyes all play at 24 fps real speed (taste note).; S01 comes from this scene's gold light, before B04. Stage it from behind her right shoulder: the phone in her right hand shows Jean under the REC dot, the real Jean sits beside it with his head to the door, and she laughs in three-quarter profile, eyes on him, never the lens. It breaks 'hands before faces' on purpose, as the script decided: a poster needs its lead.; Frame open and close (B06, B08): a graphic transition built in finishing. The pillars open in 0.6 s and close in 0.4 s, riding the phone's own motion. No dissolve.

### SC02

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat B10: close on the unbroken dust as she leans over the office step to tug the padlock rather than set a foot in it. She wants proof that nobody has found the motel, and she can't bring him in until she has it. B09 (56-60): she arms herself. She leans across his knees to the glovebox and takes the pistol by the grip, keeping the muzzle down and away from him the whole time. Real detail: trained handling, even beside a dead man. She seats it grip-up in her right back pocket. B10 (60-64): she checks. She walks the frontage alone, glances once at the empty highway, leans over the step and tugs the padlock twice, then looks down at the dust. B11 (64-72): she moves in. She unloads fast with both arms full, in one trip, and doesn't look back at him. She shoulders the swollen door. JEAN is still, his head where B07 left it.
- **blocking**: LOC-03 at last light. The wind comes from the highway (south), and the fronds lean toward the room block. The car is nosed in facing north in the carport at x 21.0-22.55, y 13.5-17.5, its passenger side (Jean) toward room 3's door PR-15 (x 25.7-26.6) and the boot at the south end. Entry: VENUS in the driver seat; PR-01 in her right front jeans pocket (set across the cut); PR-04 hooked in her neckline; PR-02 in the glovebox, muzzle toward the passenger door. B09: she leans east across Jean's knees to the glovebox (passenger dash, y 76-106). The lid drops and stays open. B10: from the driver's side she walks west along the frontage to the office door (x 12, south face) and stops on the gravel at the east end of PR-14, toes off the step. The way back to the car runs east and is not seen. B11: from the boot along her worn track (40 cm wide, packed dust) north-east to PR-15. She shoulders the latch edge at the rubbed patch (143-173 cm); the door sticks at 35°, then opens to 90°. The bags drop inside under the boarded window (x 40-140, y 20-48).
- **action progression**: B09, 56.0-60.0, one plate: hold on the engine ping (0.4); she leans across his knees, right hand to the glovebox (0.9); thumbs the latch and the lid drops (0.4); lifts PR-02 out by the grip, muzzle down (0.7); sits back upright (0.8); lifts her right hip and slides the pistol grip-up into her right back pocket, 6 cm showing (0.8). Both hands end free. Hard cut; getting out of the car is elided. B10, 60.0-64.0, one plate: the last 2.3 m of her walk west, grip showing, with one glance south at the highway (1.8); she stops at the step's edge (0.5); leans over PR-14 and tugs PR-13 twice with her right hand; it knocks on the hasp and stays locked (0.7); she straightens and looks down at the unbroken dust (1.0). Hard cut; the walk back is elided. B11, 64.0-72.0. Plate A, 64.0-68.0, at the boot: lifts the lid (0.8); PR-05 onto her left shoulder (1.1); PR-06 onto her right shoulder (1.0); both PR-07 sacks hugged to her chest (1.1). Jean stays in shot in the seat and the boot is left open. Hard cut. Plate B, 68.0-72.0, at PR-15: she enters frame laden on her last step (0.4); turns her right shoulder to the rubbed patch (0.4); first shove, the door sticks at 35° (0.7); second shove, it scrapes open to 90° (1.0); she steps in (0.7); shrugs both bags off under the window (0.8). The sacks stay against her chest.
- **emotional shift**: Before: quick and armed, with one glance at the highway during the walk. Trigger: the unbroken dust on PR-14, which the camera sees. After: she turns back without another look at the road and unloads at speed, both shoulders loaded. She checks nothing else for the rest of the cold open.
- **transition in**: Hard cut with a time jump from SC01's phone frame to the full-width carport. The cooling-engine ping replaces the mic wind.
- **transition out**: Hard cut on the door scraping open: from her back going into the dark doorway, eye at centre, to inside the black room, where the lantern click lights the wall (SC03). The scrape carries over the cut, and the wind in the palms becomes wind at the door.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' B11 carries it: she carries his bag beside hers into the room, moving in with him rather than stashing a body. Cut this and the viewer loses that the motel is a home she has kept and checked for the two of them. The pistol and the untouched dust are her guarding it; without them the room reads as a lair for trophies instead of a house for two.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Pistol PR-02, B09-B10. Method: G, or a practical plate if the generator can't hold its shape. Acceptance: the muzzle never crosses Jean or the lens; grip up with 6 cm showing in the right back pocket, readable against light-wash denim (the world bible asks the stylist for #8FA3B8); no markings. Risk: a morphing or camera-pointed gun reads as femme-fatale gloss. Fallback: already in the pocket, no hand on it.; Leaning over a dead body, B09, a five-step chain. Acceptance: Jean's knees and head do not move while she leans across him. Risk: he shifts and reads alive.; PR-14 dust. Acceptance: no footprint on the step in any frame of B10 or later.; Carry, B11. Method: G. Acceptance: two shoulder bags and two sacks, with nothing appearing or vanishing across the 68.0 cut. The two full jugs ride inside sack 2 (see blockers).; PR-12: 'MOTEL' and 'OFFICE' verbatim, composited if garbled; no other text. The loose panel may move and tick.; Door, B11. Method: G. Acceptance: two shoves and one stop at 35°, not a single swing.

### SC03

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on her hands and the chair legs as she pulls the second chair up to the table and sets it square beside hers. She wants the table set for two. We play her hands; her head stays above the lantern's pool and never turns to the door (taste note: hands before faces). She unpacks fast, in pairs and handfuls, like someone who has done it many times. She gives one look, to the empty boards beside her own chair, and slows only for the chair. Then she leaves to bring him in. The held frame has nobody in it.
- **blocking**: LOC-04. The camera is near the door (wall A) facing north toward wall C, the planned B13/S02 view; exact placement is the DP's. PR-15 stands open at 90° behind camera. She works from the table's east end (about x 245, y 365), facing west, so the tabletop and wall C stay clear. PR-16 is already on the table, off, at x 180, y 365. Placements: PR-24 at x 130-160, y 340-360; PR-21 in two stacks at x 182-198, y 382-397; PR-22 at x 206-238, y 382-397; PR-25 at x 200, y 340, slot facing south. As she sets PR-25, her head turns to the bare boards at x 210, y 420 beside PR-18, the one empty place at the table, in silhouette against the lit wall. PR-19 travels from against wall D (x 315-360, y 280-325, facing 270°) round the table's north-east corner to x 210, y 420, facing 180°, beside PR-18 at x 150, y 420. The empty sacks go crushed under the table. Her exit: south along the table's east end, past the PR-23 row and out through the lens toward the door, a hidden cut. The bare pine on wall C at x 280-345 stays empty.
- **action progression**: Entry at 72.0: dark room, sacks against her chest, bags already on the floor under the window. B12, 72.0-82.0. Plate A, 72.0-77.0: she sets both sacks on the table's east end (0.8); her right hand clicks the lantern slider and the wall of photos appears (0.6); cans out in pairs into two stacks of three (1.8); oranges from sack 2 into a loose cluster (0.8); both full jugs from sack 2, one per hand, set side by side (0.8); settle (0.2). Hard cut. Plate B, 77.0-82.0: printer from sack 1, set square with its slot to the door, her head turning to the bare boards beside PR-18 as she sets it (0.6); sacks crushed flat and pushed under the table with a foot (0.5); PR-19 dragged by its back round the north-east corner (1.4); both hands square it beside PR-18 (0.8); one nudge levels the seat edges (0.4); both hands rest on its back (0.7); she walks south out past the lens, her body wiping frame, for the hidden cut at 82.0 (0.6). B13, 82.0-88.0: locked hold and nothing moves; the wind works the open door behind camera. B14, 88.0-90.0: the same frame. The LIMERENCE card lands and the wind cuts to silence at 88.0.
- **emotional shift**: Before: fast unpacking, her hands never pause. Trigger, which the camera sees: as she sets the printer, her head turns to the bare boards beside PR-18, the one empty place at the table. After: her hands slow. The chair gets 3.3 s against 1.8 s for six cans: two hands to square it, one nudge, and both hands resting on its back for 0.7 s, the only rest in the scene, before she goes for him. B13-B14 are a hold: nobody is in frame, and the room carries it.
- **transition in**: Hard cut from SC02's doorway into the dark room; the door scrape carries across. The lantern click reveals the room.
- **transition out**: V01: the LIMERENCE card sits on the held frame in silence, then cut to black at 90.0. In the pilot: a hard cut from the card's silence to SC04's upright phone frame. Nothing matches across the cut; instead SC04's first frame echoes V01-B01's collar-and-jaw framing, with Jean alive.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' PR-20 over the table carries it, with PR-19 pulled up beside PR-18: a wall of him alone, none of them together, above a table set for two. Cut this and the viewer loses the gap the idea lives in, hundreds of photos and not one of her with him, and the printer (PR-25) that SC18 pays off.
- **practical or compositing flags**: PR-20 wall (verified fact: none of them together). Method: composite. Generate or shoot about 40 unique long-lens images of the locked JEAN (a bar window, an unbranded gas pump, crossing a street; half night, half day; always in black), then tile them to about 250 prints as a CG texture on the pine. Acceptance at S02 resolution: no print contains a second person or any woman; no legible text; Jean in black in every print; the pine at x 280-345 left bare. Risk: a freely generated wall invents couple photos and breaks the fact.; Held frame, B13-B14 and S02. Method: lock one frame and hold it as a still; the lantern runs on batteries, so nothing flickers. Acceptance: zero pixel motion from 82.0 to 90.0 (a flat difference matte). Risk: generator drift, floating dust or breathing light.; Hands unpacking. Method: G across two 5 s plates, many objects. Acceptance: the counts hold (6 cans, 2 full jugs, 5 oranges, 1 printer, 9 empty jugs by the door), and nothing appears on the table without a hand placing it. Risk: a hand merges with a can or jug.; Head turn at the printer. Acceptance: it reads in silhouette against the lit wall, her face never lit and never toward the lens.; Chair. Acceptance: PR-19 ends square beside PR-18 with the seat edges level and the backs below the photos' 95 cm bottom edge; the drag is visibly slower than the unpacking.; Title: LIMERENCE only, final 2 s, silent, composited. S02 is the B13 frame, with no text.

### V02

- **performance beats**: No new performance; V02 is rebuilt from SC01's plates. It stays Venus's piece, seen through her phone. Hero beat V02-B06: her thumb rests on the flip icon over his open eyes, then slides back, and it has to read with the sound off.
- **blocking**: Native 9:16. The phone-frame plates (V02-B01, B02, B04-B06) are the upright frame at full height, so nothing is restaged, but they must be generated at 1080x1920 or better so V02 isn't an upscale. V02-B03 comes from V01-B07's two axes: 42.8-43.8 from the west side and 45.0-46.0 from the front axis, with her right hand and his face held in the middle third on both. V02-B04 pushes in on the B08 phone plate until his eyes fill the frame.
- **action progression**: B01, 0.0-1.5: V01 5.5-7.0 (the brush and collar). B02, 1.5-4.0: V01 15.3-17.8 (00:52:30, scrub hills). B03, 4.0-6.0: V01 42.8-43.8 (the glasses lift, west side), then a jump to 45.0-46.0 (the chin turn, front axis). B04, 6.0-9.0: a handle from the B08 eyes plate, pushed in and still. B05, 9.0-11.0: the B08 laugh shake (01:04:58). B06, 11.0-13.0: the B08 thumb rests (1.2) and slides back (0.6), then the mic cuts out (0.2). B07, 13.0-15.0: the upright frame goes black, the REC dot disappears and LIMERENCE sits centred.
- **emotional shift**: The same shift as SC01, compressed. Before: care (the collar) and time (the timer jump). Trigger: his open eyes under the light (B04). After: her laugh shakes the frame while his eyes stay still (B05), and the thumb rests and retreats (B06). With the sound off, the timer and the unblinking eyes carry time and death, and the thumb carries the want.
- **transition in**: It opens straight on the phone frame mid-action at the collar, with wind on the mic from frame one.
- **transition out**: The recording stops: hard cut to black at 13.0 with the mic wind cut dead, then LIMERENCE in silence until 15.0.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' B06 carries it: her thumb on the flip icon over his open eyes, sliding back. Cut it and V02 becomes a dead-body reveal; the want, and the reason to open V01, are gone.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Footage length. V01-B08's phone section runs 3.6 s, but V02-B04 to B06 need about 7 s on his eyes. Method: generate the B08 eyes plate with handles (two plates of up to 5 s joined on a laugh jolt) and shoot the thumb plate long. Acceptance: no frame is used twice in V02.; Muted read. Acceptance: shown with the sound off, a test viewer reads 'a man who never wakes, and she won't turn the camera on herself'. The REC timer is the only on-screen text besides the title.; Push-in resolution: generate the eyes plate at 4K equivalent so the B04 push keeps the dull corneas and the dust on the lashes.

### SC04

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on her hands as the pistol goes into the glovebox and the tin comes out. She wants to go in before he leaves alone again, and walking into his frame means going without the gun. Tactic 1: from the collar-and-jaw framing of V01-B01, she zooms to his face until the lens runs out and the picture breaks into grain, and keeps it there. Real detail: she would rather have his face soft than the room sharp. Tactic 2: she waits while he raises two fingers for another. Trigger: the BARTENDER flicks the tab slip face-down in front of him, and she moves at once. Jean's living face stays soft and grainy inside her phone, a picture rather than a performance shot.
- **blocking**: LOC-01 with the top up and side curtains down, parked on the far kerb with the driver side to the bar at 14 m and the driver window open. VENUS is in the driver seat, PR-01 upright in her right hand at the window, PR-02 grip-up in her right back pocket. PR-08 (band on) lies on the passenger cushion with PR-09 half under it; PR-03 is closed in the glovebox. Through the window (LOC-05 wall A, x 40-440): JEAN on stool 2 (y 350), PR-33 at x 148, y 395, the BARTENDER in the aisle. After the trade she gets out of the driver door, on the street side facing the bar, and crosses to the glazed door (x 470-560).
- **action progression**: It opens inside her upright phone frame on his black collar and jaw through the glass, V01-B01's framing, with him alive and lifting his glass (2.0). She zooms to his face until it breaks into grain (4.0) and holds (to about 0:40). He raises two fingers and the bartender pours (to about 1:00). The bartender flicks PR-34 face-down at x 150, y 360 (1.0). The trade: phone face-down onto PR-08 (0.8); her right hand draws PR-02 from the back pocket, hip lifted (1.2); she leans right and her left hand drops the glovebox lid (0.8); her right hand lays PR-02 in, muzzle toward the passenger door (0.8); her right hand takes PR-03 (0.6); her left hand shuts the lid (0.4); her right hand pockets the tin in her left jacket pocket (1.0); PR-01 goes into her right jacket pocket (0.8). Out of the door (2.0) and across the street (4.0). Her hands end empty: tin in the left pocket, phone in the right.
- **emotional shift**: Before: still, phone up, zoomed in, barely blinking. Trigger: the tab slip lands in her frame. After: she lowers the phone for the first time and her hands move fast. It is the only hurry in the pilot until SC17's scrub.
- **transition in**: Hard cut from V01's silent held card. Nothing matches across it; instead SC04's first frame repeats V01-B01's framing (upright phone frame, REC dot, black collar and jaw) with him alive, lifting his glass. Muffled bar noise through the glass and street sound come in on the cut.
- **transition out**: Cut on action: her hand hits the bar door's glass from outside, and SC05 opens inside as the door swings in, Jean's back in the foreground. Street sound becomes bar room tone across the cut.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' PR-08 and the lowered phone carry it: months of him alone, all shot from behind her lens, and then she puts the phone down and walks into his frame. Cut this and the viewer loses where the wall came from, and the first time she steps out from behind the camera toward him.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Her phone screen. Method: a practical plate of Jean through real bar glass, with the UI and the grainy zoom composited. Acceptance: the REC dot and flip icon match V01's UI exactly, and Jean stays readable through the reflections at the end of the zoom (soft is fine, lost is not).; PR-09. Acceptance: only 'NO FURTHER ACTION' is legible, in one 1.5 s insert; no agency, seal or case number; fictional (A02).; Pistol and tin swap. Method: practical. Acceptance: the muzzle never points toward her or the lens, and the tin is never opened.; Street. Acceptance: no brand, plate or sign is legible (A10).

### SC05

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on the straw as her fingertip lays the first one in the wet ring his glass left. She wants him to agree to her game; JEAN wants to finish his drink without being seen. 'Have we met before?' is a test she already knows the answer to. Play it straight: she is listening for a lie. He tells the truth, and she gives nothing away. Her phone stays in her right jacket pocket all scene and her hand never goes to it: the first time she sits in his frame with no lens of hers between them. The Naked and Famous joke lands with her right hand on his forearm and a real laugh, and we cut on the laugh. JEAN starts rounded over his glass with his elbow planted on the worn patch. The mimosa jab gets to him: he props himself up and turns his stool to her. BARTENDER: eyes on the tab, 'What can I get you guys?' said half to the counter. Real detail: Jean slides the tab slip back unsigned with one finger, without looking at it.
- **blocking**: LOC-05. VENUS comes in by the glazed door (wall A, x 470-560) and walks north behind the stools, behind Jean's back so he doesn't see her coming, to stool 3 (y 440). She turns the stool fully to him (facing south) and keeps her elbows off the counter. Her right side, with PR-01's top edge showing at the jacket pocket, is on the counter side. JEAN is on stool 2 (y 350), with the caddy PR-29 at x 130, y 480 by her elbow. Jean lifts PR-33 off x 148, y 395 and sets it down at x 140, y 320, away from her, leaving the wet ring between them. She lays the base straw in the ring, parallel to the counter edge at x 148. PR-32 lands at x 140, y 350 (his) and x 140, y 440 (hers). PR-34 is slid back to x 105, y 360 and the bartender takes it.
- **action progression**: Her walk from the door to the stool (5.0); she sits and turns the stool (1.5). Dialogue up to 'Sure.' Jean drinks and moves his glass away (2.0). Her right hand draws a straw from the caddy (0.8) and lays it flat in the ring (1.0). The rules: her fingertip traces the house outline on the bare counter. Jean's counter, the bartender, her order. Her right hand passes her pocket and lands on his forearm on 'naked' (0.5), stays through 'Kidding' and lifts on the laugh (1.0). He props up and turns his stool (2.0), then slides PR-34 back unsigned (1.0). The bartender sets down the coupes (3.0). The coupes touch on 'Cheers' (1.0).
- **emotional shift**: This shift is Jean's. Before: rounded shoulders, eyes on his glass. Trigger: her hand on his forearm and the mimosa jab. After: he sits up, turns his stool to her and pushes the tab back unsigned. Venus holds the same warm attention throughout; nothing has been tested yet, so nothing moves her.
- **transition in**: Cut on action from SC04: the door swinging in.
- **transition out**: Hard cut on the clink of the coupes; the clink rings over into SC06's first question.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' PR-01 carries it: its bare black edge shows at her right jacket pocket through the whole scene, and her right hand goes to the straws and to his forearm, never to it. This is the first scene where she shares his frame with no lens of hers between them. Cut it and the viewer loses her first minutes in his picture without her camera, the step that SC18's front camera completes.
- **practical or compositing flags**: PR-01 in her pocket. Method: practical. Acceptance: its top edge reads as bare black glass with no case, as in V01, in at least one two-shot, and her hand never touches it. The stylist confirms the pocket shows it without it falling.; Wet ring and straw. Method: practical. Acceptance: the base straw lies inside a visible 8 cm wet ring, white on #3A2418.; 'Naked and Famous': clearance of the name is pending with the producer (A10), and no bottle label is legible.; Background patrons. Acceptance: they stay low-contrast and never cross the straw house or the candle.; Contact (her hand on his forearm): practical only. If it is ever generated for the pitch, restage it with no contact. Nothing is handed from person to person; drinks are set down and the slip is slid.

### SC06

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on her fingertip, face unchanged, as she drinks and draws the wall straw back out. She wants to hear the truth about his ex; JEAN wants to be the wronged man. 'I was wondering when you'd ask' is a private joke, since she has known his name for months, and she lets it pass as flirting. She lays the wall straw on 'a stick toward our home'. 'Who slayed you, my friend?' is the trial question: play it light, leaning in and laughing. The lie, 'I never once raised my voice', comes out easy and without a tell. Real detail: he denies something nobody asked. Her verdict, 'That's a drink.', comes at the same pitch as her jokes. Then she moves closer, not further away: 'Are you willing to save me?', and pulls back laughing.
- **blocking**: Same marks as SC05. Straw house S1 to S2: she lays the left wall along x 128-148 at y 385, making an L. Then S3: she draws it back out and drops it in the caddy (x 130, y 480). Her coupe goes to two-thirds. On 'save me' she slides her stool toward his until the gap between the seats is gone, leans to his right ear, then sits back.
- **action progression**: Names (dialogue). She lays the wall straw (1.0). The 'defeated' exchange and her laugh; cut on the laugh. The lie. The verdict: she lifts her coupe, takes one swallow and sets it down (2.0); her fingertip draws the wall straw out along the counter (1.0) and drops it in the caddy (0.5). 'That's a no?' 'That's a drink.' Stool slide (1.0); lean to his ear (1.0); the line; she pulls back laughing (1.0).
- **emotional shift**: Hers. Before: laughing, leaning in. Trigger: 'I never once raised my voice', a denial nobody asked for. After: she drinks without taking her eyes off him and takes the straw with no change of face, then leans closer. His: he hears it as flirting and leans into her lean.
- **transition in**: The clink from SC05 bridges the cut.
- **transition out**: Hard cut with a time jump, as SC07's LATER heading requires: her laugh after 'My turn' is cut off, and room tone carries. The eye goes from her face, pulling back, to the candle PR-31 at x 115, y 260 in SC07's first frame. The script reads continuous here; blocker filed with the writer.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' The wall straw coming back out carries it: the house for two loses its wall the moment he lies. Cut this and the viewer loses the rule behind her want, that she will only be in the picture with a man who tells the truth, and the night plays as seduction instead of a trial she hopes he passes.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Dialogue is practical, live-action sync. No generated lip-sync anywhere.; Straw removal. Method: practical. Acceptance: the base straw stays in its wet ring when the wall comes out.; The lie line is new, not Lucas's (writer's flag). Play it plain.

### SC07

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on her fingertip as it rests on top of the chimney straw, then slides away, and the straw stays standing. It is the same rest-and-slide as her thumb on the flip icon. She wants a reason to let him pass; JEAN wants her home tonight, and he takes her turn. She holds his eyes and blinks less than he does. On 'tame a beast' / 'What dog doesn't want a true owner?' he awards himself a straw without asking. Then 'Who would have known a stranger could be my next lover?', the 3-second vision, and one shake of her head brings her back to the bar.
- **blocking**: Staged as written, LATER: both coupes lower than in SC06, the house still S1 (base only). JEAN slides PR-31 from x 115, y 260 to x 112, y 418, 13 cm off the roof line on her side. He reaches across her to the caddy (x 130, y 480), so his arm crosses in front of her, and draws a straw. He pushes it 1 cm into the wax pool at the pool's rim on his side of the wick, about 3 cm from the flame, where it stands 19 cm tall and leans 5° toward him, away from the flame (PR-30 S1). Her right fingertip goes on top of it, then off.
- **action progression**: The candle slides (1.5); the flame leans and settles. Questions (dialogue). He reaches across her (1.0), draws a straw (0.5), stands it in the wax (1.0) and lets go; it stands (0.5). Her fingertip rests on its top (1.0) and slides off (0.5); it stays. Her line. An insert, 3.0 s at real speed: her hand on a black shirt against white sheets. Back at the bar, one shake of her head (0.8).
- **emotional shift**: Before: the judge, eyes steady, fingertip over the straws and ready to pull. Trigger: she watches him stand his own straw upright. After: her fingertip rests on it and leaves it standing, the vision comes, and she shakes it off. She has gone from judge to lover.
- **transition in**: Hard cut with a time jump: both coupes lower, the bar emptier, room tone carrying. First frame: Jean's hand closing on PR-31's glass, the eye on the flame.
- **transition out**: Hard cut from her head shake to the street outside the window, silent. The bar sound drops to a muffled street.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' Her fingertip resting on PR-30 and sliding off carries it: the flip-icon gesture, made while he is alive, on the one straw he stood up for them. Cut this and the viewer loses that her hesitation is want, not caution; her thumb in the car would read as a killer's discipline.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Straw beside a live flame. A paper straw 1 cm into the wax pool of a lit 7.5 cm pillar stands within about 3 cm of the flame for the whole take. Method: practical; set it at the pool's rim on Jean's side of the wick, leaning away from the flame, with a fire-safety check on set. Acceptance: no scorch, smoke or curl on the straw at the end of the take, so SC12's chimney matches. Risk: it chars and reads as a burn effect.; Straw in soft wax. Method: practical, with a hidden stiffener if production design needs one. Acceptance: it stands 19 cm, leans 5° and survives the fingertip rest for the whole take. Risk: it topples and reads as pulled.; Vision insert: 3.0 s at 24 fps real speed, no slow motion, no nudity. The hand is hers (right-hand scar), the shirt #111111, the sheets white. Practical, with an intimacy coordinator.; Candle flame: practical pillar only.

### SC08

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on his phone in her hand as she takes it from Jean while he frames the two of them, and photographs him alone. Through the window, play a real flirt and cut on the laughs: he adds straws and she pulls them. At last call Jean wants a picture of the night. She takes it out of his hands without a word, pleasantly and quickly, as if doing him a favour. Real detail: he checks the photo of himself, laughs and pockets it, vain enough not to notice she's missing. His last reach is for the chimney, to end the game as the winner, and she plucks it first.
- **blocking**: Window montage: shot from the street side (wall A window, x 40-440), with the house at x 130, y 395 through the glass. The house goes to S4, the full six straws, then S5, a roof straw drawn toward the counter edge (the S03 frame), then the last flat straw drawn out. The coupes run low. Inside at last call: the counter is bare except PR-31 with PR-30 standing at x 112, y 418. JEAN holds PR-35 out at arm's length in his right hand, on its front camera. VENUS takes it in her right hand, leans back on stool 3 out of his frame, shoots him beside PR-30 and hands it back. The BARTENDER reaches for PR-32 and PR-33. She plucks PR-30 into her left jacket pocket beside PR-03. The exit: arms tangled, south through the glazed door to the unbranded rideshare at the bar's kerb (x 0-500, right side to the kerb). Her two-seater is visible across the street with the top up; no scene retrieves it (blocker with the writer).
- **action progression**: Window: five or six silent shots of 1.5-3.0 s each, every cut on a laugh; the roof-straw pull takes 1.5. Last call: he holds his phone out (1.0); she takes it (0.8), leans back out of frame (0.6), frames him and shoots (1.3), hands it back (0.8); he looks, laughs and pockets it (2.0). The bartender reaches (1.0) as Jean goes for the chimney (0.8). She plucks it first (0.5) and pockets it (0.8). They stand and tangle arms (2.0), walk out (5.0) and get into the car (4.0).
- **emotional shift**: Hers. Before: laughing, building and pulling straws. Trigger: his front camera frames the two of them, the picture she wants, offered on his terms by a man who lied. After: she takes the phone, takes herself out of the frame and keeps the last straw. His: he wanted a picture of both of them, gets one of himself, and is pleased.
- **transition in**: Hard cut to the window exterior.
- **transition out**: Cut on action: the rideshare's rear door shuts, seen from outside, and SC09 opens inside on the dash cam as the thunk lands.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' PR-35 carries it: he offers her the photograph of the two of them and she takes it out of his hands to shoot him alone. Cut this and the viewer loses that she could have the picture tonight and refuses it; it has to be the right one. That refusal is what makes SC18's press an ending, not a habit.
- **practical or compositing flags**: S03 is this scene's roof-straw pull, and a generated still is high-risk because straws bend, multiply and cross. Method: a practical tabletop shoot, or CG straws composited over a bar plate. Acceptance: exactly 5 straws in line plus 1 under her fingertip, all straight and uncrossed; the base in a wet ring; a normal fingertip; no text.; Phone handed between people, twice. Method: practical only, never generated. Acceptance: PR-35's cracked case tells it apart from PR-01, and its screen is composited if seen.; Window glass. Method: practical. Acceptance: the house reads through the glass, white straws on the dark counter.; Rideshare. Acceptance: no badges, stickers or trade dress (A10). No slow motion on the laughs.

### SC09

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene, seen from the dash cam. Hero beat: she takes his wrist off the pocket with the tin and moves his hand to her waist, laughing against his mouth. She wants the tin to stay hers to open; JEAN wants his hands inside her jacket. The DRIVER wants his back seat kept clean. He flicks the dome light on without turning his head, and when she flicks it off he leaves it off; his eyes go to the mirror for 2 s at a time. When her eyes open they stay on Jean and never go to the dash-cam lens; she looks into no lens until SC18.
- **blocking**: LOC-06. JEAN gets in first and slides across to y 100, behind the driver. VENUS sits by the kerb door at y 40, her left jacket pocket (tin and chimney) on his side. The dash cam PR-36 is at the top of the windscreen, lens facing rearward, and the DRIVER sits front left. The dome light PR-37 is over the bench at x 55, y 75, within reach of her right hand.
- **action progression**: They kiss with their clothes on while the timestamp runs from 01:47:12. The driver's right hand goes to the console rocker and the dome comes on (0.8). Her right hand goes up to the lens rocker and switches it off (1.0) without breaking the kiss. The driver looks to the mirror (2.0), then back to the road. Jean's right hand goes into her jacket and down to the left pocket (1.0); his fingers find the tin's edge through the fabric (0.5). Her left hand closes on his wrist (0.4) and moves it to her waist (0.8); she laughs against his mouth (1.0). Her eyes stay open for the rest of the ride.
- **emotional shift**: Before: eyes shut, all in. Trigger: his hand on the tin's hard edge, visible through the jacket. After: her eyes open, she steers his hand away and laughs, and her eyes stay open until the car stops.
- **transition in**: The door thunk bridges; hard cut into the dash-cam frame.
- **transition out**: Hard cut from the dark cabin to the white marble lobby (SC10). The jump in brightness carries the move into her world, and the first sound is the brass street door closing.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' PR-36 carries it: the only recording of the two of them together tonight belongs to a stranger, and she keeps her eyes off it. Cut this and the viewer loses that not every picture of them counts; hers has to be taken by her, on her terms. They also lose the moment the tin nearly slips out of her control.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Dash-cam overlay: composite only the running time from 01:47:12; no date, speed, GPS or brand.; Clothed kissing: practical, with an intimacy coordinator; nothing from it goes into the pitch pieces.; Dome light. Method: practical, with two rockers. Acceptance: on for under 3 s.; Car: no badges or rideshare trade dress (A10).

### SC10

- **performance beats**: Jean's scene. Hero beat: close on the shoes as he stops in black on the white threshold, toes off his shoes, and her hands line them up on the white mat. He wants to know who she is and how she pays for this, but he wants the night more. His eyes go to the surfaces first: marble, brass. 'Mind the step' cuts off 'So what do you-'. He looks down at the curtail step, and the question is gone. VENUS leads a step ahead, never looks back, and plays 'It's a little messy' as a genuine apology, though the flat is spotless. Once his shoes are squared she stands, steps back to the distance she holds her phone at and looks at them, hands empty.
- **blocking**: LOC-07. In through the street double door (wall A, x 300-480) and along the gloss path to the curtail step (x 660). Flight 1 runs north along wall D to the half landing; flight 2 runs south to the floor-2 landing. She stays a step ahead. PR-39 is on the landing's east side, facing west, and opens inward. From the door, LOC-08: JEAN on the threshold, PR-40 at x 65-145, y 5-55, the bathroom door on wall B at y 120-200. After squaring PR-41 she steps back about 60 cm north of the mat, into the room, and looks at it.
- **action progression**: Lobby crossing (6.0), his eyes on the floor and the mailboxes. The line is cut off at the step (1.0). Stairs (20.0), with the dialogue on flight 2. The door opens (1.5). He stands on the threshold (2.0). He toes off his shoes heel against toe onto the mat, and they land askew (2.0). He asks for the bathroom and walks to wall B (3.0). She crouches and sets the shoes side by side, toes to the door, 2 cm apart (2.5); stands and steps back to arm's length and looks (0.8); then goes to the kitchen run.
- **emotional shift**: Jean's shift. Before: his eyes are on the surfaces and he starts to ask. Trigger: 'Mind the step', then the white room. After: no more questions, only a joke and a request for the bathroom. Venus holds; her plan is running.
- **transition in**: Hard cut from the dark cabin to the white marble.
- **transition out**: Cut on action: the bathroom door closes, seen from the living room, and SC11 picks it up from inside as it clicks shut.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' The step back carries it: after squaring PR-41 on PR-40 she steps back to the distance she holds her phone at and looks at his black shoes in her white doorway (0.8), with nothing in her hands. Cut it and the viewer loses her framing him into her home as if through a lens, the composing that SC17's posed armchair and SC18's photograph finish.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Lobby and landing. Acceptance: no directory, names or numbers are legible.; Shoes: no visible logos (the stylist confirms).

### SC11

- **performance beats**: Jean's scene. Hero beat: close on the tissue as he looks at the faint pink on it, drops it in the bowl and flushes. He wants to get back out to her fast. Real detail: he checks his teeth before he deals with the blood. He stops at the drops for half a second, then does quick, efficient housekeeping without a question. Through the door, ice drops into glasses; he grins and goes.
- **blocking**: LOC-09. JEAN comes in by the door (wall A) and goes to the mirror cabinet PR-49 above the basin (wall D). He crosses 1.4 m to the toilet PR-47 (wall C); the roll is on wall B at y 200; back to the basin tap, back to the toilet, and out. The seat is left up. Off screen, heard only as ice through the door, VENUS makes the set for SC12: jacket off to its hook (position pending the production designer); PR-30 from her left jacket pocket into the ice of her tumbler; PR-03 closed onto PR-42 at x 175, y 535; PR-01 from her right jacket pocket face down on PR-53 in the bedroom. She changes into the T-shirt and fills both tumblers from PR-59.
- **action progression**: At the mirror: grin and teeth check (3.0). To the toilet (1.5); lifts the seat (0.5); sees the drops and stops (0.5). Tears off three sheets (1.0); wets them at the basin (1.5); wipes the rim (1.5); looks at the pink smear (1.5); drops the tissue in the bowl (0.5); flushes (0.5). Ice through the door; one more grin at the mirror (1.0); out.
- **emotional shift**: Before: grinning at himself. Trigger: the drops under the seat. After: a half-second stop, then he wipes them away and walks out smiling. He removes whatever doesn't fit the night he wants.
- **transition in**: Cut on action: the door clicks shut.
- **transition out**: Cut on action: he opens the bathroom door, and SC12 picks him up stepping into the living room. The ice bridges the cut.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' SC11 carries plot, not the idea: the drops on PR-47 are the previous man's (A13), the first sign that Jean is not the first man in this room. Cut it and the viewer loses that history and the pink tissue, but not the idea, which SC18 carries. Staged as written; blocker filed with the writer.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Dried drops. Method: practical makeup/SFX, 2-4 mm of #6E1C18 crust. Acceptance: after the wipe, a haze of 10 x 3 mm at most; no smear, run or spray. Risk: any more reads as gore.; Keep the tap beat: the wet wipe (PR-48 S2) is what makes the scripted pink physically true.; Mirror. Acceptance: only Jean is reflected.; The off-screen set is heard only (ice); nothing of it is shown, and nothing passes between people.

### SC12

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on her fingertip as she slides the closed tin across and lands on the lid when he tries it. She wants to ask the last question first; JEAN wants the bedroom now. She plays it light and dry: 'You haven't shown me anything worth missing.' He downs his drink like a shot and blows out, breath only. The chimney straw stands in her ice: the last straw, standing again. She asks 'You feel like this space isn't safe?' warmly.

Her left hand sets her glass at x 175, y 520; her right fingertip slides PR-03 west to x 140, y 525. After turning it over, he sets it back at x 140, y 525, hinge east, seam west toward him; SC13's lid direction depends on this.

She sits (1.5); her left hand sets her glass down with the straw standing (0.8); her right fingertip slides the tin across (1.0), then lets go. He picks it up and turns it over (1.2), sets it back at x 140, y 525, hinge east, seam toward him (0.5), and puts his thumbnail to the seam (0.3). Her right fingertip lands on the lid (0.3); he presses and it holds (0.8); he lets go, grins and sits back (1.0). Her fingertip stays on the lid to the end of the scene.

PR-30 standing in her ice, and her fingertip holding PR-03 shut, carry it: the picture with him is still possible if he tells her one true thing. ### SC13

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on her hands as her left hand draws the straw out of her ice and lays it flat to leave a wet mark, her right fingertip lifts off the tin, and her right thumb opens it toward him. What's inside stays out of frame. She wants one true thing about love, his last straw; JEAN wants the bedroom. She asks her speech as a real question, warmly. His near hand goes to her thigh. While he reaches for a comeback, his eyes go to the tin (eyes to the prize). On 'That's for you to find out' he grins, thinking he has won. Her verdict lands with no change of face; only her hands move.

Her left hand draws PR-30 from her tumbler (x 175, y 520) and lays it flat at x 160, y 488-508 along the table's long axis, leaving a 20 x 1.2 cm wet mark. Her right fingertip lifts, and her right thumb raises the lid from the west seam. It swings up about 100° on its east hinge, so the open well faces Jean and the lid's back faces her. The camera stays on her (east) side of the lid.
- **action progression**: Her speech (dialogue). He leans in (1.0) and puts his left hand on her right thigh (0.5). His speech, his eyes going to the tin twice. Her question; his line. The verdict: her left hand draws PR-30 out (1.0) and lays it flat (1.0); her right fingertip lifts (0.5); her right thumb raises the lid from the west seam toward her (1.0). Hold on his face (2.0). Cut.
- **emotional shift**: Before: fingertip on the lid, straw standing, face open and warm, hoping. Trigger: 'That's for you to find out', a line instead of a truth. After: her hands lay the straw flat and open the tin, while her face stays pleasant. The change is only in her hands.
- **transition in**: Hard cut in continuous time from SC12.
- **transition out**: Hard cut with an elision: from the open lid straight to SC14, with Jean already lifting her. What he takes from the tin, and how, stays off screen.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' PR-30 laid flat and PR-03 opening carry it: the hope of a picture with him alive goes down with the last straw, because he gave her a line. Cut this and the viewer loses why he dies, and that she wanted him to pass; SC18's photo would read as a trophy.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Tin interior. Acceptance: no frame ever shows into the well; the camera stays east of the raised lid. No contents, no preparation, no dosing (must avoid).; Wet mark on white gloss. Method: practical. Acceptance: it reads as a streak with no pooled liquid (lighting is the DP's).; Hand on thigh: practical, with an intimacy coordinator.

### SC14

- **performance beats**: Jean's scene. Hero beat: close on his black-socked foot as it stops at the three drops, then steps over them. JEAN wants to get her to the bed; VENUS wants him to keep walking, and she never looks down. She turns his face back to hers with her right hand and says 'J.' It is the gesture she will repeat on him dead (V01-B07, SC18). Here, alive, he looks back.
- **blocking**: From the sofa's north end JEAN stands and lifts VENUS from PR-44. He carries her past the table's north end, with the open tin and flat straw in view as they pass, then east across the rug to the hall and north to the bedroom door (x 540-630). PR-45 lies at x 575-600, y 880-886, just inside the threshold on the hall side. He stops with his lead foot short of the drops, then steps over into LOC-10.
- **action progression**: He stands (1.0) and lifts her (2.0). The carry, about 7 m (8.0). He looks down and stops for half a step (0.8). Her hand turns his face to hers (0.8); 'J.' (0.5). He steps over (1.0) and through the door (1.5).
- **emotional shift**: Jean's shift. Before: striding, eyes on her. Trigger: the drops at his feet. After: a half-step stop, eyes down. Second trigger: her hand on his jaw and 'J.' After: eyes up, he steps over and never looks back. Venus holds: she never looks down, because she doesn't know the drops are there.
- **transition in**: Hard cut with an elision from the open tin.
- **transition out**: Match cut: his black foot stepping over the drops on the white floor becomes his black shirt lying on the white floor just inside the bedroom door, later that night (SC15). The match is black on white at floor level.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' Her hand turning his face toward hers carries it: the last time he looks back. Cut this and the viewer loses the gesture's first, living use, so her turning his dead chin toward her (V01-B07) and toward the lens (SC18) has nothing to answer.
- **practical or compositing flags**: The carry. Method: practical, rehearsed with a stunt coordinator. Acceptance: steady, with no stumble.; Drops: 3-5 mm of dried crust that reads only in the close shot; no smear.

### SC15

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on her hand as it passes her phone on the nightstand and turns up the speaker ring over his catching breath. She wants to stop hearing his breathing. Tactic 1: she folds his black clothes square. Tactic 2: the mirror cabinet, with the same face and hands as the padlock check. She lifts the organiser, opens one lid, looks, closes it and puts it back, with no wince and no pause on the turned label: the meds are seen and never given as the reason. Real detail: she shuts the mirror door with her eyes down and never meets her reflection. Tactic 3: she lies down. His breathing stops, then catches with a rasp, and her hand passes the phone to reach the speaker. JEAN is breathing only, face down, recorded wild.
- **blocking**: LOC-10: JEAN lies face down on the wall-D side of PR-52; VENUS is on the wall-B side. PR-56 is on the floor, the shirt at x 200, y 60 and the trousers at x 280, y 150; she folds them onto PR-55 (wall D). She goes out through the hall, passing the threshold drops without looking, to LOC-09: PR-50 on the middle shelf of PR-49, PR-51 on the top shelf with its label to the wall. Back in bed she lies on her back, head to wall C. PR-53 is on her right, with PR-01 face down (since SC11) 10 cm left of PR-54.
- **action progression**: She gathers the shirt (1.0) and the trousers (1.0), folds them (6.0) and sets them square on the chair, shirt on top (1.0). Walk to the bathroom (8.0). Opens the cabinet (0.8); lifts the organiser (1.0); opens row 1, column 1 (0.8); looks (1.5); closes it (0.5); puts it back (1.0); shuts the mirror door with her eyes down (0.8). Back to bed (8.0); lies down (2.0). His breathing runs slow and loud, stops for a gap (5.0), then rasps (1.0). Her right hand leaves the sheet (0.8), passes over the phone without slowing (0.5) and presses the speaker's dot, so the track plays low (0.5). She turns the ring to about 80% (2.0) and brings her hand back to her side (0.8). She lies still, eyes on the back of his head.
- **emotional shift**: Before: her hands stay busy with the folding and the cabinet. The organiser is a hold, with no trigger and no change, and the same face she gave the padlock. Trigger: his breath stops and catches with a rasp, which we hear. After: her hand goes past the phone to the music and turns it up until his breathing is under it. Then, for the first time, her hands go still.
- **transition in**: Match cut from SC14, black on white at floor level.
- **transition out**: Hard cut to morning with the track continuous: the same four bars loop across the cut, and the light on the same bed goes from night to morning.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' Her hand passing PR-01 for PR-54 carries it. The phone is her camera and the one thing that could call for help. Cut this and the viewer loses that she let him die while he could still be helped, so her waiting in SC16 and the photo in SC18 become love for a man she let go, not a collector's calm.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Meds. Acceptance: seen and never named; no legible label; all 28 compartments full. Risk: a close on the vial label or any diagnosis reads as the explanation (must avoid).; Breathing: wild sound, no lip-sync, face down.; Music: the original trance-soul track (A08), heard only from PR-54. Level changes come from her hand on the ring, not from the mix.; Mirror. Acceptance: no crew in the reflection, and she never meets her own. Speaker and phone back stay blank; no screen is ever lit or seen.

### SC16

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on his arm as she lifts it over her waist and it slides off. She wants him to turn over and reach for her. The look follows ref 06 as the brief describes it: he lies face down with his mouth open; she lies awake with a hand to her forehead, eyes on the back of his head, not the ceiling. This is the one scene where her hands have nothing to do. Tactic 1: she waits through another loop of the track. Tactic 2: the arm. Tactic 3: 'J. Stay for breakfast.', at the same dry pitch as 'That's a drink', not pleading. Then she switches the music off.
- **blocking**: PR-52 is rumpled. JEAN lies face down on the wall-D side, his right arm off the bed on that side and his left arm toward her. VENUS is on the wall-B side. PR-54 is still looping at about 80%, with PR-01 face down beside it.
- **action progression**: Hold through a full loop (10.0); nothing moves but her breathing. She turns onto her side toward him (1.0), takes his left wrist (0.5) and lifts his arm over her waist (1.5). She lets go and it slides off onto the sheet (1.0). Her line (2.0). She waits (3.0). Her right hand goes to the speaker's dot and switches it off (1.0). Silence (hold 4.0).
- **emotional shift**: Before: still and waiting, a hand at her forehead. Triggers: his arm slides off her waist, which we see, and her line gets no answer, which we hear. After: she ends the music and the room goes silent. She stops waiting and gets up.
- **transition in**: The music bridges the cut from SC15.
- **transition out**: Hard cut on the silence to her hands at his shirt buttons (SC17).
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' His arm lifted over her waist carries it: she stages the picture of a couple waking up together, and it slides off. Cut this and the viewer loses the want at its plainest, feelings returned rather than sex (limerence), and the picture she can't make even here.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Jean's stillness. Method: practical breath-hold takes, or VFX paint-out of breathing. Acceptance: no rise in the chest across the take. Risk: one breath reads alive.; Arm slide. Method: practical, dead weight. Acceptance: it slides off rather than dropping stiffly.; Ref 06 is a look only, with no likeness; the bedding is white, not patterned, because the colour theme wins.

### SC17

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on the cloth as she scrubs out the three drops she missed until it is pink and the floor is white, then folds it square before she bins it. She wants the apartment spotless before she takes him away. She cleans with the same unhurried care she gave his collar, except at the threshold, the only place she speeds up. Real detail: the pink cloth folded into a 10 x 10 square before it goes in the bin. She puts her jacket back on before she touches the tin. The last image holds on the finished room longer than is comfortable: JEAN in black in the white armchair, sunglasses on, hands on the arms.
- **blocking**: In LOC-10 she dresses him on the bed from the clothes folded on PR-55, and brings his shoes from PR-40. Cut: he is seated in PR-44 at the table's north end, facing south, hands on the arms, and she puts PR-04 on him with both hands. Back in the bedroom she takes PR-01 off PR-53 into her right front pocket, then strips and remakes PR-52. At the threshold (drops at x 575-600, y 880-886) she kneels on the bedroom side, facing south. The cloth goes into PR-60 (x 650-680, y 470-500). At the coffee table: the tumblers go to the kitchen run; her jacket comes off its hook (wall A, pending the production designer) and goes on; she closes PR-03 at x 140, y 525 from the east, behind its raised lid, and pockets it with PR-30 in her left jacket pocket; she wipes the table. She is out of frame for the final hold.
- **action progression**: Dressing: the shirt goes on (cut into the action), then the buttons (cut), about 20 s of screen time. Hard cut: he is seated in the armchair, the move itself elided. PR-04 on him (1.5). PR-01 off the nightstand into her pocket (1.0). She strips the bed (6.0), pulls the four fitted corners tight (8.0), and puts the duvet on and smooths it until only the ironed grid shows (6.0). Leaving the bedroom she looks down and stops (1.0), kneels (1.0) and scrubs, faster (6.0); the cloth is pink and the floor white (hold 1.0). She folds the cloth square (1.5), takes it to the bin, presses the pedal and drops it in as the lid soft-closes (4.0). Tumblers to the kitchen (3.0). Jacket off the hook and on (3.0). Her right hand pushes the raised lid shut from the east side (0.8); tin and straw into her left jacket pocket (1.5). She wipes the table, and the straw's wet mark goes with it (2.0). Hold on the room (10.0).
- **emotional shift**: Before: calm, methodical, never looking down. Trigger: the three drops at the threshold she had missed; it is the first time she looks down there. After: she kneels at once and scrubs harder and faster than at anything else, then folds the cloth square, back in control.
- **transition in**: Hard cut on SC16's silence.
- **transition out**: Match cut from Jean seated in black in the white armchair, sunglasses on (SC17's hold), to Jean seated in black in PR-19 under his wall by lantern light, sunglasses off (SC18). The white room's silence becomes the wind at the motel door.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' Jean on PR-44 carries it: dressed, sunglasses on, posed at the centre of her spotless room, a portrait she makes of him and stays out of. Cut this and the viewer loses where the cold open's sunglasses and pose come from, and that she composes him for a picture rather than disposing of a body.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Closing the tin. Acceptance: the camera stays east of PR-03 and her hand closes the lid from behind it; no frame shows into the well (must avoid).; Dressing and moving a dead-weight actor: practical with a body double, elided with cuts, no struggle shown.; Pink cloth. Acceptance: pink (#E3A9A6), not red, with no drips. The scrubbed patch reads flatter and whiter than the satin floor.; Final hold: 8-10 s with nothing moving. Jean's stillness is tested as in SC16.; Car: nothing yet returns the two-seater from across the bar before SC01 (blocker with the writer).; Taste note broken on purpose, per the world bible: no dish rack and no charging cable, because the tidiness is the horror.

### SC18

- **performance beats**: Venus's scene. Hero beat: close on her right thumb as it rests on the flip-camera icon and this time presses, and the screen shows both of them. She wants to get into one photograph with him. Her thumb rests as it did twice in the car. Then she lifts her head from his shoulder and looks round, past the back of his head, at the next man's two prints she has just pinned, and holds there: the wall has already moved on. She turns back, lays her head on his shoulder again and presses. For the first time her eyes go into a lens, her own front camera, and she is smiling. She turns his chin toward the lens; his gaze still passes under it. She takes the photo anyway with no disappointment played; the smile holds.
- **blocking**: LOC-04, continuous from V01-B14. Jean was brought in off screen: he sits in PR-19 (x 210, y 420, facing south), face bare, eyes open. His head keeps the set from B07, 45° to his own right, so it points south-west, toward her side. VENUS unhooks PR-04, folds it and sets it lenses-up at x 135-149, y 365-370. From PR-06 under the window (wall A) she takes PR-28, pins already through them, and pins them in the paler rectangle on wall C at x 305-315, heights 170-185 and 188-203. She moves PR-18 to x 165 so its seat edge touches PR-19's. Both chairs face south, so she sits on his west side, his right, and rests her head on his right shoulder; her left hand is the one beside him. Her right hand holds PR-01 at arm's length in front of them to the south-west, front lens toward them, wall C behind. The phone is raised above his eye height, so his level, set gaze passes under the lens. The trigger look: she lifts her head and turns over her left shoulder, past the back of his head, to PR-28 at x 305-315, then back. Her left hand turns his chin toward the lens; rigor gives a few degrees sideways and can't lift it. She sets the phone face up beside PR-25, which ejects PR-27 to the south. She pins the card on wall C at x 290-295.4, heights 165-173.6, between Jean's photos and PR-28, and sits back down beside him.
- **action progression**: Sunglasses: unhook (1.0), fold (1.0), set down (0.8). To PR-06 (3.0); PR-28 out (2.0); to wall C (3.0); pins both (3.0). Moves PR-18 (1.5), sits (1.0), rests her head on his right shoulder (1.0). Her right hand takes PR-01 from her right front pocket (1.0) and raises it above his eye line (1.0). Thumb to the icon (0.8); it rests (1.2). She lifts her head and turns over her left shoulder to PR-28 (1.0); holds (1.0); turns back and lays her head on his shoulder again (1.0). She presses (0.3), and the screen shows both of them. Her left hand turns his chin (1.2); his eyes pass under the lens. Shutter (0.3). She sets the phone face up by the printer (1.0). The printer whirs and ejects the card (5.0). She takes the card from the slot (1.0), stands and pins it (3.0), then sits back down beside him (2.0). Key actions sum to 38.1 s; the rest of the 2:00 is the final hold.
- **emotional shift**: Before: her thumb rests on the icon (1.2 s), as it did twice in the car. Trigger, which the camera sees: she lifts her head from his shoulder and turns over her left shoulder, past the back of his head, to the next man's two prints on the wall, and holds there 1.0 s. After: she turns back, lays her head on his shoulder again and presses. The press is the change: her own face on her screen, eyes in the lens, smiling. Second beat, a hold: after her chin turn his gaze still passes under the lens, and she takes the picture with no change of face.
- **transition in**: Match cut from SC17: seated in black in the white chair becomes seated in black under the wall, with the wind at the door.
- **transition out**: End of the pilot. Hold on the wall (Jean's photos, the one card-size print of them together, the next man's two prints) with the two of them at the table below. Hard cut to black after the printer falls silent; the wind carries over the black.
- **campaign idea link**: 'She isn't collecting trophies; she's trying to get into one photograph with him.' Her first press of the flip icon and PR-27 pinned between Jean's wall and the next man's carry it. Cut this and the viewer loses the answer to every thumb rest: she wanted one picture with him and gets it only when he can't look. They also lose the setup the series repeats.
- **practical or compositing flags**: Front-camera screen. Method: composite the UI. Acceptance: the lantern lights her face, her eyes are in the lens, and his gaze passes visibly under it.; Phone height. Acceptance: in the front-camera frame the lens sits above his eye line, so his eyes read level and below it, never into it. If a take shows his eyes meeting the lens, raise the phone; never re-pose his head (rigor).; Trigger look. Acceptance: her head leaves his shoulder, turns past the back of his head toward wall C, holds 1.0 s and returns before the press; it reads in the wide with the sound off.; PR-27. Method: a practical card pre-printed from the actual frame. Acceptance: with the sound off, it reads as the only card-size print on the wall.; Dead eyes: the same makeup lock as V01, no blink, lids at the top of the iris.; Contact with the dead man (head on his right shoulder, chin turn): practical, with a stillness-trained body double.; PR-28: a fictional man; the courthouse facade has no inscription or seal, and no real case is shown. The printer's whir is the only non-wind sound; no score.

| SND-D01-004 | D01 | foley + atmos + production dialogue (decorum at the bar) | Pilot SC05-SC08, about 180-660 s (3:00-11:00); 'Have we met before?' at the top of SC05 (about 180 s); the first verdict inside SC06 (about 330-480 s); picture-led | You hear the game on the counter; nothing scores it. Following A08, no jukebox or bar music plays. VENUS: "Have we met before?" opens it over the bare room. A straw laid flat in the wet ring makes a soft, tacky tick. A straw drawn back out makes a dry slide. A glass is set down for every drink, so each lost straw has its own sound. The first verdict (SC06): after JEAN: "She left. Packed while I was at work. I never once raised my voice." her glass goes down, then the dry slide of the wall straw, then JEAN: "That's a no?" VENUS: "That's a drink." In SC07 the chimney straw makes a faint waxy drag as it is pushed into the soft candle wax. Dialogue is recorded clean on set, with nothing under it but the room.
