| SND-D01-005 | D01 | foley (the tin and the last straw) | Pilot SC12-SC13, about 900-1200 s (15:00-20:00); the lid opens at the end of SC13, about 1195 s, the Act Two button; picture-led | No music in either scene (A08). SC12: her glass set down on the white lacquered table, the straw standing in its ice. The closed metal tin slides across to him in one continuous glide of metal on lacquer, a low even hiss with no stutter. He turns it over in his palm; nothing inside is heard. He tries the lid: it lifts a hair and clicks back shut under her fingertip, one dry metal click. Then VENUS: "Let's take a trip." Through both scenes the ice settles in her glass around the standing straw, a soft shift now and then between lines, never under a word. SC13: after JEAN: "That's for you to find out." the straw is drawn out of the ice with a wet drag and laid flat on the table with a wet tick. Her fingertip lifts, the hinged lid's catch releases, and the lid swings open toward him: a dry hinge, and a small knock as it meets its stop. Hard cut on the knock: the Act Two out. Nothing inside the tin is heard, now or later, because the method stays off screen. | Source: foley recorded for this production (a prop tin with a hinged lid, a white lacquered table, glass, ice, straw). The dialogue is covered by the Venus and Jean actors' written consent for the pilot. Scope: the pilot, as SND-D01-001; owned by the production. Status: pending. Owner: producer. |
| SND-D01-002 | D01 | voice, non-verbal + foley (Jean's breathing) | Pilot SC15, about 1290-1440 s (21:30-24:00); the rasp about 1400 s, before the track enters; picture-led | His breathing is Jean's last sound: slow and loud, face-down, muffled by the pillow. Her side of the room: his black clothes folded square (cotton on cotton), the magnetic catch of the mirror cabinet, and one lid of the pill organiser snapped open and shut, with the tablets shifting once. No label is read and no drug is named. Back in bed, his breathing stops for a long gap, 6-8 s of room tone with nothing in it, then catches with one rasp on the inhale (about 1400 s). No gurgle and no death-rattle cliché. This silence is the quietest point of the night. Then comes the track (SND-D01-001), put on low, and he breathes on in its holes. The speaker's volume button takes three soft presses, each a step louder, until the holes no longer let his breathing through. | Source: recorded for this production with the actor cast as Jean (not yet cast). His written consent is required for his breathing and the rasp in the pilot's scope (as SND-D01-001). No library or synthetic breath. The foley is owned by the production. Status: required, pending. Owner: producer. |
| SND-D01-001 | D01 | music, in scene (the trance-soul track) | Pilot SC15-SC16: entry about 1410 s (23:30), after the rasp in SND-D01-002; exit at her switch-off, about 1525 s (25:25); the line in a bar-4 hole inside SC16 (1440-1530 s); picture-led, moves with the pilot edit | The pilot's only music (A08). Venus plays it from the small white speaker on the nightstand to quiet herself; it is never score. Perspective: heard from the bed, from a small mono speaker that plays little below about 120 Hz. Brief for the composer: 100 BPM in 4/4 (beat 0.6 s, bar 2.4 s), one four-bar loop of 9.6 s that loops with no click, gap or tail at the join from bar 4 back to bar 1. It is written for the speaker's range. Bars 1-3 have a soft four-on-the-floor kick whose attack carries the pulse, an off-beat bass line pitched so its upper harmonics carry, electric-piano minor-ninth chords, a filtered pad and a low, wordless female vocal. Bar 4 (7.2-9.6 s of each loop) is the hole: kick, bass and electric piano all drop out, leaving only the pad, with its filter closed down, and one held low vocal note, so 1-4 kHz is nearly empty for 2.4 s. Entry: a click on the speaker's play button, with the loop starting low on a bar-1 downbeat. SC15: at low volume his breathing comes through each hole; she turns it up (the three presses in SND-D01-002) until the pad and the held note cover it. SC16: the same four bars come round, and now each hole carries no breath. VENUS: "J. Stay for breakfast." sits in one hole: 4 words in about 1.6 s of its 2.4 s. Read: said to him, landing on "Stay"; she is coaxing him up; not a plea, not grief. The music is laid in post, not played back on set, so the line records clean. Exit: she switches it off. The button clicks, the track stops hard mid-bar with no ring-out, and the room is silent. Swap test: the hole is built for his breath, so on another film it lands on nothing. | Source: commissioned original composition and master, with a wordless session vocalist. Scope: the pilot episode in all media, worldwide, in perpetuity, plus trailer, pitch and promo use of the pilot. Required: the composer's originality warranty and a similarity check, plus the vocalist's written consent for that scope. No generated vocals or stems. The line is covered by the Venus actor's consent (SND-V01-015). Status: pending. Owner: producer. Not used in V01 or V02. |
| SND-D01-006 | D01 | foley + room tone (the clean-up) | Pilot SC17, about 1530-1680 s (25:30-28:00); starts in the silence after the switch-off in SND-D01-001; picture-led | No music: the switch-off at the end of SC16 left the room silent, and it stays silent apart from her work. Room tone: sealed and white, with a faint hiss from an air vent. Absent: the street, the neighbours and any clock, because the windows are shut and nothing in the room is allowed to move. Dressing him: black cotton drawn over his arms, the white armchair's cushion sighing under his weight, and the acetate hinge of his sunglasses opened and slid on (the V01 acetate take, a plant for SC01). The bed: the old sheets dragged off, the fitted sheet's elastic snapping off each corner, one fresh sheet snapped open in the air, four corners pulled tight and tucked, her palms smoothing. The threshold: her knees on the hard floor, the damp cloth scrubbing the three drops in short hard strokes, two soft folds as the pink cloth goes square, then the bin's pedal and lid. The tin's hinge and a click as it shuts; fabric as it goes into her pocket with the flat straw. Then the hold on the finished room, with Jean in the white chair: room tone only, longer than is comfortable (tone: clean). Jean makes no sound. | Source: foley recorded for this production, with the sunglasses from the V01 acetate session and room tone recorded in a white-finished interior (location release required). Scope: the pilot, as SND-D01-001; owned by the production. Status: pending. Owner: producer. |
| SND-D01-003 | D01 | foley + sfx (the tag: the flip-camera press) | Pilot SC18, about 1680-1800 s (28:00-30:00); the flip-camera press about 1750 s; picture-led | This continues from V01, with the same motel room tone and wind (SND-V01-012 and 013). An acetate tick as she sets his sunglasses down by the oranges. Two push pins pressed into the wall for the new man's prints. Cotton as her head settles on his shoulder, and the phone lifted. The focal sound, ahead of the printer: her thumb squeaks across the glass to the flip-camera icon and makes the small knock in the phone body. These are the same foley takes as SND-V01-004 and 008, reused on purpose so the ear knows the gesture. This time there is no slide back: the press completes. The flip is heard as the 2-frame dropout from the V01 fallbacks: the room tone drops out for 2 frames and comes back. Two fingers turn his chin; his collar shifts and his chair creaks. The photo is taken with no shutter sound (original UI, A10). Then the palm-sized printer: a motor whir and the paper feeding out, the only mechanical sound in a silent room. One more pin as the photo goes up, then room tone and wind, echoing V01's held frame. Proposal, for approval: no laugh here. Every laugh so far was aimed at him from behind the lens; in front of it she only smiles, and the room stays quiet. | Source: foley recorded for this production, with the thumb takes reused from the SND-V01-004 and 008 sessions, the printer recorded on a debadged unit and the room tone reused from the V01 session. Scope: the pilot, as SND-D01-001; owned by the production. No manufacturer UI sounds (A10). Status: pending. Owner: producer. |


## Assumptions and blockers

### Development

- A01 | runtime | yes | The pilot runs about 30 minutes, as a half-hour drama | if_wrong: D01 → 3000 s (~50-min) hour-long outline with a B-story on Jean's ex and the dropped case
- A02 | story | yes | Jean is on Venus's list: his 'tough breakup' is with an ex whose domestic-violence report went nowhere | if_wrong: premise → Jean is innocent and her limerence alone chose him; the series turns on her misjudgment
- A03 | story | yes | Venus has watched Jean for months before the bar, so 'have we met before' is a test she already knows the answer to | if_wrong: premise → she picks him that night from a case file, and the motel wall is built after the kill
- A04 | story | yes | Decorum is her private verdict: she knows the true answers, and each lie costs him a straw | if_wrong: premise → the game is only flirtation, and the case file alone carries the verdict
- A05 | story | yes | Jean dies overnight in the white apartment from what is in the metal tin ('let's take a trip'), so the room stays spotless | if_wrong: premise → she uses the pistol off screen before dawn and the pilot never shows how
- A06 | story | yes | The pilot ends on the first photo of them together, pinned beside the first photos of the next man | if_wrong: premise ending → she takes the photo and burns Jean's wall; the next man arrives in episode 2
- A07 | route | yes | The route is commission: D01, V01 and V02 are pitch material for drama development execs | if_wrong: objective → festival selection; D01 → 12-min proof-of-concept short outline
- A08 | music | no | 'Trans/soul' means an original trance-soul track that Venus puts on herself, heard inside the scene | if_wrong: tone music line → licensed soul track, with the clearance cost flagged on the white-apartment beat only
- A09 | route | yes | V01, V02 and the stills are generated end to end | if_wrong: V01 → hybrid, with live-action performances on a parked-car rig composited over generated desert plates
- A10 | legal | no | Real brands in the idea (the Uber, the Raptors shirt) appear unbranded on screen. Source: Lucas's script. Scope: on-screen logos in the pilot and the pitch pieces. Owner: the producer. Status: pending, nothing cleared. | if_wrong: must_avoid → drop the brand line and add clearance to the budget
- A11 | visual | no | The car is the vintage open two-seater from refs 09 and 11, so her laughter and his stillness share one open frame | if_wrong: V01 → an older closed sedan, with the ride played through the windshield
- A12 | spec | no | Pitch video is delivered at 16:9, and V02 is a 9:16 rebuild from V01 footage | if_wrong: V01 → 2.39:1 letterbox, with V02 reframed from it
- A13 | story | yes | The dried blood behind the toilet and the red drops on the floor belong to the previous man, from a night the tin failed and she used the pistol | if_wrong: premise → the blood is hers, and the untaken meds and the blood are one secret
- A14 | story | yes | The white apartment is where she lures each man; the motel is where she builds his wall and brings him afterwards | if_wrong: premise → the white apartment is staged for the date and the motel is her only home
- A15 | production | no | V01 needs 3 locations (desert road and town, motel exterior, motel room), 2 on-camera roles and 2 consistent characters, 1 voice (her laughter, no words), 0 spoken or lip-synced lines, an estimated 15–20 generated shots, 3 hand shots (phone, pistol, groceries) and no product reference | if_wrong: V01 → if Venus must speak, she speaks from behind the phone with her face unseen, so no lip-sync is needed
- A16 | production | yes | The make-or-break shot is Jean's pale open eyes revealed while Venus keeps laughing. The first test is a 5 s shot in a moving convertible in which a still, open-eyed passenger holds consistency. | if_wrong: V01 → the reveal moves to the parked car at the motel, where a static frame holds him still
- A17 | story | yes | The season question is the one man who passes decorum | if_wrong: format_rationale → closed procedural, one man per episode
- A18 | story | yes | The tin sits closed on the table until Jean gives his line about love; opening it is her sentence, so she was hoping he would pass | if_wrong: premise → Jean opens the tin as soon as she slides it over, and his line only confirms what she had already decided

### Strategy

- Market and language: English-language drama for North American streamers and networks. This is inferred from the North American brands in Lucas's idea and is not confirmed.
- Client and money: there is no brand. The client is Lucas as creator, and LIMERENCE is the working title (development's working brand). The revenue path is a paid script commission or development deal, then a series order. Subscriber value downstream belongs to the buyer.
- Release cadence (weekly, or all episodes at once) is the buyer's call. H9 tests return from episode to episode, not a weekly schedule.
- Reach: V01 and D01 go privately from the producer or agent to a named list of execs, with no media buy. V02 and S03 go public only if the producer decides a public teaser helps the pitch.
- Viewing conditions: V01 is watched on a laptop with sound on. V02 must read muted. S03 is muted in-feed.
- Evidence: Lucas's raw idea text, including 'whose case get lost', is not used as evidence. F13 (the logline) carries 'the man the system lost' as content. Because the old F15 was dropped, the former F16-F19 are now F15-F18, and the ending proof point is now F17. F19 and F20 quote development's format_rationale and the D01 label as content only, as the reviewer directed.
- Proposition contingent on: A02 (Jean's ex's domestic-violence report went nowhere) for 'the man the system lost' in the pilot. F13 supports it for the series as a whole.
- Proposition contingent on: A18 (the tin stays closed until his line about love). It shows she hoped he would pass, which keeps her love real rather than a trap.
- Proof points are held to four. F2 is not a standalone proof point: F17's ending pays F2 off and carries 'can't make him', while F2 still does work in product_relevance, S02 and the competitor fallback. F18 restates F1's support in story terms. F3, F5, F14-F16, F19 and F20 are story mechanics or season content rather than proof of the proposition.
- Proof points leave out F4 and F7-F12 (gun, colour, meds, blood, music). They are execution, or they argue a second idea (her instability), and under must_avoid the meds must never read as the reason.
- Sample sizes are consistent: 5 development readers for H2, H5 and success criterion 4, 6 viewer calls for H1, and 5 cold viewers for H4 and H9.
- Comparable titles in H3 are common knowledge, unverified and internal only. The phrase 'female Dexter' stays out of all public-facing material.
- Success thresholds are studio go/no-go lines, not market benchmarks. No statistic is claimed anywhere.
- Taste notes, which the file flags as example notes and not Lucas's own: the proposition follows 'Restraint over explanation' (it names her want, never her code) and 'Dry over sentimental', and breaks none. 'The product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply because there is no product.
- Blocker (Lucas): Alternative: key_message ('Venus can punish any man the system lost, but her strong desire for reciprocation is the one thing she can't satisfy: no man she hunts ever loves her back.') is not carried as the proposition. The main reason: 'no man she hunts ever loves her back' contradicts A17, the season question D01 must carry (the one man who tells the truth at decorum and seems to love her back). It is also a 29-word line with two beats, and 'any man' and 'the one thing' are not supported by any fact. Proposed instead: 'Punishing the man the system lost can't make him love her back.' This leaves the season question open. → Approve the 12-word proposition, or keep key_message as pitch copy with 'any', 'the one thing' and 'ever' removed so it no longer rules out A17.
- Blocker (Producer): The competitor check (H3) has not been run. No one has checked the clause only LIMERENCE owns (she falls for every man she sentences, and the sentence is how she keeps him) against the nearest threats, You, Killing Eve and Promising Young Woman, or against current and announced slates. The comps stay internal. → Before the first send, check those three titles first, then scan current and announced slates and loglines. If one matches, sharpen the proposition to the wall with no photo of them together (F2).
- Blocker (Lucas / Producer): Permission is not established: there are no credits, attached producer or prior work that an exec could check. → Supply one checkable credential for the cover note (a produced short, an attached producer or showrunner, a festival selection), or confirm there is none.
- Blocker (Lucas): A02 is unconfirmed. Whether Jean is guilty decides whether 'the man the system lost' holds for the pilot. → Confirm A02. If Jean is innocent, the proposition becomes 'Punishing the man she chose can't make him love her back.' and H1 is retested.
- Blocker (Producer): There are no flight dates, no exec send list and no decision on releasing V02 and S03 publicly. Without them, the success windows, the rate denominator and whether social metrics apply cannot be set. → Supply send dates, the list size and a decision on public release. The client sets targets or accepts the labeled studio thresholds.

### Routes

- The three most familiar openings in this genre (internal, no titles named): (1) a vigilante killer's cold open with a voiceover laying out the code; (2) the femme-fatale seduction with slow-motion glamour and a last-scene reveal that she is the killer; (3) a true-crime pastiche with case-file cards, news audio and 'unsolved' titles. All three routes avoid 1 and 3. R1 subverts 2: her touch asks rather than controls. R2 subverts the killer's trophy photo. R3 subverts 2 by making the seduction game a chance rather than a trap.
- Ten one-liners were drafted; the three kept differ in reframe, device and structure. R1: linear, objective camera, the hand as device; its idea costs 'a killer's touch is control'. R2: told through her phone's frame, widening at the reveal, the front camera as device; it costs 'the photos are trophies'. R3: two braided timelines, omniscient POV, the straws as device; it costs 'the seduction game is a trap'. [changed] R1's previous idea ('The kill is already over; the only suspense left is whether he'll love her back') restated the development premise, so every route's example served it. It failed the pairing test and was replaced. Other cut drafts: 'the sentence is the first day he can't walk out' (reads as control, against F1) and 'a dead man can't lie at decorum'.
- There is no product. product_role describes the job of the device that carries the proposition and Venus's role in frame. The taste note 'the product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply.
- All beats are tagged G or C: V01, V02 and the stills are generated end to end (A09), no shoot budget is authorised and no renders were supplied, so no beat is tagged P or R. Every reveal beat depends on the A16 test shot.
- Creative additions not found in Lucas's idea or the facts, for Lucas to approve: the dead traffic light, the dust devil with her arm catching his weight, and the unreturned squeeze (R1); the stop sign nobody else will reach, the REC timer, the flip-camera icon, the under-a-second front-camera flip, the phone's video light, turning his chin with two fingers, and the battery lantern (R2); the bent straw in his pocket and the wind lifting it (R3); the oranges (all routes).
- [changed] R2, question for Lucas: it changes his cold open's camera. 40 of 90 s are seen inside her phone's upright frame (0-34 s and 50-56 s); her face is withheld until 34 s and Jean's until 42 s; the pilot's ending (A06) becomes her first use of the front camera for a photograph. The fallback's under-a-second flip is a live preview, not a photograph, so A06's 'first photograph of them together' still stands. No beat is dropped.
- [changed] R2's time and light hold together: the recording runs from REC 00:00:04 in low gold sun to 00:58:40 at blue dusk and 01:04:55 on his eyes. The third hand is gone: she fixes his collar only when stopped at the stop sign, and she only films or touches him one hand at a time while driving.
- [changed] R3, question for Lucas: braiding a silent bar night into V01 spends the pilot's flashback. The braid now stops with one straw still standing. The last straw is his line about love in the white apartment (F16), and the tin opens only after it (A18); both stay in D01. Lucas's bar dialogue stays in D01 because V01 has no spoken lines (A15). Decorum as a verdict rests on A04.
- V01 in every route never shows or implies how Jean died: no wound, no tin, no method. The only blood rule applies to the white apartment, which is in D01 and not in V01.
- The car is the vintage open two-seater (A11). Silver is used in R1's palette to sit against gold and black. Red (ref 11) is production design's call and does not change the routes.
- [changed] Every beat runs at real speed, and no route uses an aerial or drone transition. R1's 24-32 s is now the dust devil and her arm catching his weight; R3's 44-50 s is the wind lifting the straw from his pocket. There is no nudity or sex in V01, V02 or the stills. Nothing on screen is branded; clearance is still pending (A10).
- [changed] Rights: R2's phone UI (REC dot, running timer, flip-camera icon) is an original design with no OS or maker's marks. Source: this route. Scope: V01, V02 and S01. Owner: production design. Status: clearance pending with the producer under A10, not claimed as cleared. If it reads as a real operating system, simplify to the REC dot and timer and play the hesitation as the front-camera flip.
- [changed] R2's season extension, a proposal for Lucas that is not yet in D01: the front camera turns on only once per man, at the table, afterwards, so the season question (A17) can become the man she turns it on while he is still alive.
- Venus and Jean are fictional, and neither is modelled on a face in Lucas's references. The references are cited only as the brief describes them; no reference was inspected in this run.
- Taste notes followed in all routes: hands before faces (Venus's first face at 32 s in R1, 34 s in R2 and 58 s in R3; Jean's head stays turned to the door, showing only collar, jaw and sunglasses, until his eyes, the one face shot); sound design before music (no score in V01; the trance-soul track belongs to the white apartment in D01); restraint over explanation (no voiceover and no text beyond the title); one real detail per scene (R1 the dead traffic light, R2 the stop sign, R3 the bent straw). [changed] R2's camera-roll scroll is cut because the wall already says none of them are together (F2); 82-88 s is now a held frame. Bent on purpose: R2's 18-26 s fallback shows her lower face for under a second before 34 s. R3's braid still asks the viewer to link the straws to the drive, which leans toward explanation, but it no longer shows the last straw fall, so the bend is smaller and is accepted as the brave route's cost.
- 'Female Dexter' and the comparable titles stay internal and appear in no route or deliverable.
- [changed] Revision log for this round: R2 repeats the flip hesitation after the eyes (50-56 s) and the rationale quotes it; the no-thumb fallback is replaced by the front-camera flip; the third hand is removed (stop sign); the timer matches the light; Jean's face is held back until 42 s; the camera-roll scroll is cut; the recurring device is restated; S01 is recomposed; the phone UI's rights are named. R1's idea is replaced and both squeeze beats are tagged with fallbacks. Aerials are cut in R1 and R3. R3's braid stops with one straw standing, and its V02 opens on a straw being drawn out.
- Blocker (Lucas): The recommendation quotes the strategy's proposition ('Punishing the man the system lost can't make him love her back.'), which Lucas has not approved yet (strategy blocker 1). For the pilot it also rests on A02 (Jean's guilt). → Approve the proposition or the trimmed key_message, and confirm A02. All three routes hold either way; only the rationale's quote changes.
- Blocker (Generation supervisor): Every route's reveal depends on the untested A16 shot: a still, open-eyed passenger in a moving open car who stays consistent across shots. → Run the 5 s A16 test before building V01. If it fails, every route moves the eyes reveal to the parked car at the motel, and V02 is recut from that shot.

### Script

- The scene timings in the D01 headings are estimates for a roughly 30-minute half hour (A01). The director can adjust act lengths.
- The car is left parked across from the bar when they take the rideshare (SC08). Fetching it and moving Jean from the apartment into the passenger seat happen off screen, between SC17 and SC01. If Lucas wants that shown, it becomes a short scene after SC17.
- Creative additions in D01 for Lucas to approve or strike: the pistol traded for the tin, and the fictional incident report stamped 'NO FURTHER ACTION' (SC04); Venus speaking the decorum rules close to Lucas's own words, and the flat house of four walls and a pitched roof (SC05); the tab slip Jean pushes back (SC05); Jean's lie and 'That's a no?' / 'That's a drink.' (SC06); the chimney straw in the candle wax and her fingertip resting on it (SC07); his selfie attempt and the photo she takes of him alone (SC08); the dash-cam timestamp and dome light (SC09); 'Mind the step' and his shoes on the white mat (SC10); the straw standing in her ice (SC12) and its wet mark on the white table (SC13); the four-week organiser with its label turned to the wall, the breath catch and her hand passing the phone (SC15); 'J. Stay for breakfast.' (SC16); the sunglasses she puts on him and the drops she scrubs (SC17); the printer, the second chair and the courthouse-steps photos (SC03, SC18).
- Additions in V01 beyond the route's execution example: the pistol taken from the glovebox (56-60 s, split from the route's 56-64 s beat); one open arm of the sunglasses hooked into her neckline (50-51 s); the photo printer, the second chair, the bedroll and the emptied jugs (72-90 s). Shouldering open a swollen door stands in for Lucas's 'breaks into the motel'.
- In SC18 the next man's photos go up before the photograph of her and Jean, as the notes ask. She takes the photo even though Jean's eyes miss the lens, and the imperfect first photo is kept on purpose.
- Lucas's dialogue is kept with light spelling and grammar cleanup ('The Lord has answered my prayers'). The trust/lust exchange is trimmed, not rewritten.
- 'Naked and Famous' is treated as a cocktail name, not a brand. The producer confirms this under A10.
- The phone UI (REC dot, timer, flip-camera icon) is an original design added in finishing. Clearance is pending with the producer (A10) and is not claimed as cleared.
- V02's timer readings after 01:04:55 (01:04:58, 01:05:00) assume the V01 50-56 s shot runs as one continuous take.
- Jean, Venus, Jean's ex, the next man and every case are fictional. No character is modelled on a face in Lucas's references. No reference image was inspected in this run; they are cited only as the brief describes them.
- The phrase Lucas used as an internal comparison does not appear in any deliverable.
- Blocker (Director / generation lead): A16: Jean's open, unblinking eyes in a moving convertible (V01 42-56 s; V02 4-13 s) have not been tested for consistency. The glovebox reach across his knees (V01-B09) is a second hand-risk shot. → Run the 5 s test shot. If it fails, use the written fallback: the reveal (V01-B07) plays with the car parked at the motel. If the glovebox reach fails, use the B09 fallback, with the pistol already in her pocket. No story beat changes.
- Blocker (Producer): Clearance for the phone UI and the brands is pending (A10). → If the UI reads as a real operating system, keep only the REC dot and timer, and play the hesitation as the under-a-second front-camera flip (fallbacks are written into V01-B04, V01-B08 and V02-B06). Confirm that 'Naked and Famous' needs no clearance.
- Blocker (Lucas): The creative additions listed in assumptions need Lucas's approval. → Approve or strike each item. Any of them can be removed without losing a turn, except two: the last-straw chain (SC07-SC08, SC12-SC13), which carries A18 and the season question, and the breath catch in SC15, which carries Venus's climactic choice.

### Cast

- The cast is exactly the script's four IDs: VENUS, JEAN, BARTENDER and DRIVER. The next man in the courthouse-steps prints (SC18) is not cast; he appears in photos only. Production design supplies two long-lens prints of a fictional man who must not share JEAN's build or hair or resemble any real person, and he gets a profile when episode 2 is written. JEAN's ex (name blacked out) is never on screen, and there is no VO character.
- Setting: the script never names a country. The '-American' heritages, the US sizes and the assumed alcohol minimum of 25 all rest on a US setting, which the desert drive and motel fit. Lucas's Raptors shirt points to Toronto. If the city is Toronto, the heritages restate as Canadian (Black Canadian, Haitian-Canadian, Mexican-Canadian, white Canadian) with no change to any anchor, the sizes convert, and the 30+ reading floor stands. Lucas or the producer to confirm.
- Alcohol rule: no market code was given, so the minimum is assumed to be 25 and every visible face reads 30+. Targets are 34, 38, 50 and 61, and bar background patrons in SC05-SC08 also read 30+.
- Heritage: all four are casting choices, not script requirements. None goes into an anchor or a prompt, so none can show in a face. JEAN's reason is his name and the family D01 can give him. VENUS is matched to him so the killing doesn't read as a cross-racial story. BARTENDER and DRIVER are cast apart from each other and from the leads. Proposal for D01: the men she sentences vary in heritage across the season, so 'the man the system lost' is never tied to one heritage.
- Lucas's 'tall slim model straight off your latest Vogue cover' is kept as height, slimness and bearing, not a glossy finish, because the tone rejects the glossy femme fatale. His optional blonde highlights are kept as a joint lock with the stylist.
- 'Pale' on JEAN comes from desaturation, grey-ash at the lips and lids, dust, a dry, grey-filmed cornea and unblinking eyes. It never comes from a lighter skin or iris hex, so the generator doesn't lighten a Black man's skin to signal death.
- JEAN's identifying mole and hairline asymmetry are on his left side because his head is turned to the passenger door, away from VENUS. In 0-42 s the camera sees only his left jaw, ear, neck and collar.
- Proposal for the director (blocking is the director's call): the phone in VENUS's left hand and her right hand for the touches (collar, sunglasses, chin), in a left-hand-drive car. The anchors are built for this: the left-thumb nick reads over the screen UI, and the right-hand scar reads in the touches. If the phone changes hands or the car is right-hand drive, mirror the pair. Both marks are permanent either way.
- Rigor is a timeline assumption, not a medical claim. JEAN dies in the small hours and is filmed at dusk most of a day later, so his neck is played as set from V01 through SC18: no bob, and the head gives only a few degrees with the shoulders. This also explains why his head never moves between B03's cuts in a moving car. SC16's loose arm is read as early morning, before the limbs set. In SC17 she dresses him, seats him in the armchair and puts his sunglasses on, which runs against the stiffening. This is flagged to the director and writer: play it as effortful, or move the dressing earlier.
- Squaring objects belongs to VENUS alone as the clean-horror tell. BARTENDER puts things down off-angle, and JEAN turns his stool rather than 'squaring' to her.
- No reference was inspected; I worked only from the brief's descriptions. To stay clear of them, JEAN has no locs and no arm tattoo (ref 06), VENUS is not pale or auburn (ref 04), and no one is modelled on any face in refs 01-11.
- VENUS's laugh is one voice locked across V01, V02 and D01. It must be generated, or performed by a consenting fictional-cast performer. No real person's voice is cloned, and no one's availability or permission is claimed.
- There is no child in the script. No one is flagged for visible difference, religious dress, pregnancy or age 70+ (DRIVER's target is 61). Mental health is flagged for human review in the blockers.
- Taste notes. 'Hands before faces': VENUS's left-thumb nick and right-hand scar, and JEAN's neck mole, hold identity before either face appears. 'Restraint over explanation': no knowing look to the lens, no menace register, and the organiser played flat. 'Dry over sentimental': her verdicts and 'J. Stay for breakfast.' are at one dry pitch. Casting breaks no note on purpose; S01's face break was already accepted upstream.
- If D01 is ever produced live-action rather than generated (A09), the bedroom scenes need an intimacy coordinator and every performer's written consent. No performer is attached now.
- Blocker (Producer): No market alcohol code was given, and the bar, the drinks and the tin put every visible adult under the alcohol rule. → Confirm the target markets. Until then the cast assumes a minimum of 25, and every visible face, bar background included, reads 30+. If any market sets a higher minimum or bans showing people drinking, re-age the cast or recut the bar scenes and S03.
- Blocker (Producer, with a sensitivity reader): The script pairs a killer with psychiatric medication she hasn't taken in weeks, and Lucas framed it as mental health. That is the representation risk the must-avoid names (meds treated as the explanation for the killings), and the craft brief flags disability, which can include mental illness, for human review. → Get human or sensitivity review of SC15 and of D01's framing of her meds before the pitch pieces circulate. Casting holds the organiser beat to the padlock-check register (no wince, no tremor, no pause on the label) and never ties her performance to a diagnosis. The reviewer confirms this or asks for changes.
- Blocker (Lucas (creative approval)): Heritage for all four characters is a casting choice, not a script requirement, and the setting is unconfirmed (US assumed; the Raptors shirt points to Toronto). The circulating pieces raise the stakes: S01 and V02 make a dead Black man the key image beside a laughing woman, and they travel without D01's context. → Lucas approves or reassigns with S01 and V02 roughs in front of him, not from this text alone. If anything changes, redo skin hex, hair texture and JEAN's dead-state notes together, keep VENUS and JEAN matched or say why not, and restate the heritages for the confirmed country.
- Blocker (Director / generation lead, with the stylist): JEAN's anchors depend on the A16 test. In a moving convertible a still, open-eyed passenger must hold his identity, his dead eyes (upper lid at the top edge of the iris, a dry grey-filmed cornea, dusted lashes) and a set neck that doesn't bob. B07 and V02-B04 also prove death with 'the wind moves his hair', which an 8-10 cm tight 4A top may not show. → Run the 5 s A16 test on these anchors before locking them. Check that the top of his hair visibly moves in the wind within the 5 s; if it doesn't, the stylist loosens or lengthens it. If the pallor or the dry cornea doesn't read, carry the death through dust, unblinking eyes and a desaturated grade, never a lighter hex, and use the parked-car fallback.

### World

- Place and year: an unnamed US Southwest city, 2026 (ASSUMED), for the bar, the rideshare and the white apartment, within a day's drive of the desert road and the motel. The script runs on one continuous timeline: SC04 is Thursday night, and the drive is the next evening in the same car, fetched from outside the bar. The Canadian option is dropped. Details generators tend to mix up, now locked: right-hand traffic; left-hand-drive cars; a rear-plate-only state (ASSUMED) with every plate blank; English signage; a red octagon 'STOP'; NEMA 5-15 sockets in the 2026 apartment and lobby; two-slot, ungrounded NEMA 1-15 outlets in the 1958 motel room.
- Street lighting: LED cobra heads (4000K ASSUMED), since most US cities have converted by 2026. No sodium fixture is written. The bar's own window spill carries the amber on the sidewalk, and the rideshare cabin gets cool LED slides (#B9C3CC).
- All dimensions, ages and tenures are ASSUMED from the category, since none are verified: the car, the rooms, the tin, the printer, the prints, the 1958 motel build, about five weeks at the motel and about two years in the apartment.
- Motel room finish: knotty-pine tongue-and-groove paneling, the period roadside-motel wall, replaces the earlier lime plaster. It takes the pins, and the paler rectangle on wall C is where the motel's own picture kept the pine from ambering.
- Photo count: about 250 prints (ASSUMED), so the room meets the verified fact that the walls are covered in photos of the man, even though the script says 'dozens'. About 180 are on wall C and about 70 on wall B above the bedroll. Bare pine at x 280-345 on wall C stays empty until SC18, when the next man's prints and the photo of them together go up there.
- The marks that show how long she has lived in the motel room now sit inside the held frame toward wall C: the jug row along wall D just past the door's sweep, the bedroll along wall B at y 190-400, the paler rectangle and the pin holes on wall C. The bags stay under the boarded window, out of that view.
- White apartment: PR-44 moves to the table's north end so that SC12's fingertip on the tin and SC13's hand on her thigh can be staged. Her knees are about 35 cm from Jean's, and the tin sits between them at x 140, y 525.
- Rideshare: they get in on the kerb side (right). Jean slides across first, so her tin pocket is on his side. The dome light is in the rear headliner, with a front master switch for the driver (ASSUMED).
- Car: silver-grey paint with bone leather, so the REC dot stays the only red in the drive and the leather carries her white. The red car in ref 11 was the alternative and is rejected for that reason (upstream left the colour to production design).
- Straws are plain white paper, so the house reads on the dark counter at 12:1. The tin is satin gunmetal, not bright steel, because bright steel on white gloss falls below 3:1.
- The car heads north in SC01, so the low west sun sits behind Venus (the route puts the sun behind her) and Jean's turned face is in shade.
- In LOC-04 the chairs stand side by side with their backs to the photo wall, facing the door. V01-B13 then reads as a table set for two, and the SC18 front-camera photo has Jean's wall behind both of them.
- Dust is set-owned and tracked as states under PR-57: the dash, Jean's collar (agreed with the stylist) and the eyes (handed to makeup for V01-B07).
- Legible text, written verbatim for compositing: the phone UI 'REC 00:00:04' through 'REC 01:05:00' as scripted; 'STOP'; 'MOTEL'; 'OFFICE'; 'NO FURTHER ACTION'; the dash-cam time '01:47:12' (ASSUMED, just after last call). Nothing else is legible.
- Surfaces that must stay blank, because generators add fake logos: the car's grille, wings, wheels, dials and plate; the storefronts; the bottle labels, fridge, chalkboard, neon and coasters; the rideshare's badges and stickers; the dash cam; the mailboxes and door; the speaker, ice bucket and pedal bin; both phones; the organiser, vial and toilet paper; the cans, jugs and printer; the gas pump and courthouse in the prints; the T-shirt (stylist).
- There is no product (route R2 stretch). Each product_placement field gives the device doing the job, its contrast and its clear zone instead. The taste note 'the product is a tool, then a hero' does not apply.
- Taste notes: each location names its one real detail. Broken on purpose: the white apartment has no dish rack or charging cable, because its tidiness is the horror. Ref 06's patterned bedding is replaced with white to hold the colour theme.
- The craft limit of about five locked props per frame is exceeded in V01-B13 and S02, because the script names more. LOC-04 gives a lock order.
- References 01-11 were not inspected; there is no reference reader in this run. They are used only as the brief describes them, and no character or set is modelled on a real person or a real, recognisable place.
- Camera, lenses and lighting are left to the DP. The only requests are the stylist's denim tone for the pistol grip, the tin's mirror path, and the dust hand-offs to the stylist and makeup. The 'planned held view' in LOC-04 says where the marks must be visible; it does not place the camera.
- Blocker (Writer): V01-B11 has Venus unload 'his black bag' (PR-05), but D01 never gives Jean a bag, so it has no plant. → Default used here: Venus packs his things into a black duffel off screen. Better: plant the bag in SC10 on the white mat beside his shoes, or change V01-B11 to 'her bags'.
- Blocker (Writer): The two-seater stays across from the bar after SC08, and SC17 ends with Jean in the white armchair. No beat covers her fetching the car or getting him down to it. The SC04 prints and report (PR-08, PR-09) sit on the passenger seat that Jean later occupies. → Default used here: all of it happens off screen, and PR-08 and PR-09 are moved into her bag (PR-06) before SC01. Please confirm, or add a line to SC17.
- Blocker (Writer): SC11 has Jean wipe the rim and see faint pink on the tissue, but dry blood on glazed china flakes off brown. The pink needs a damp tissue, which is an action the script doesn't have. → Default used here: he wets the tissue under the basin tap before wiping (PR-48 S2). Please confirm or cut the pink.
- Blocker (Producer (A10)): Clearance is pending for the original phone UI and the 'Naked and Famous' cocktail name, and there is a risk that a 1950s roadster silhouette reads as a real make's trade dress. → Everything is built unbranded, with a generic grille and filled badge holes. If the UI reads as a real OS, simplify it to the REC dot and timer, as the route states. Nothing is claimed as cleared.

### Director's treatment

- D01 scenes SC04-SC18 are staged for a live-action pilot shoot. Only V01, V02 and the stills are generated now, so the 5 s rule and the no-hand-off rule apply to those. The hand-offs in SC08 and SC12 (a phone and a glass) are practical only.
- Venus holds the phone in her right hand throughout and drives with her left. This follows the world bible (PR-01 S2 and S6), S01 and her right-handedness, and it drives the hand staging in B02, B05, B07, B08 and SC18.
- Wind is my choice. In the moving car it is relative, running nose to tail. At the motel it comes from the highway (south), which also explains the wind at room 3's south-facing door in B13.
- B03's first jump (storefronts at 00:21:40, after the stop at 00:06:12) is staged as a second pass through the one block: she is circling the dead town.
- Head set: after B07, Jean's face points north-east, 45° off the door toward the windscreen, held by rigor (casting). 'Head to the door' in B11 is read loosely as this set. Seated facing south in SC18, the same set points his face south-west, toward her side, which is why she holds the phone above his eye line.
- SC13: Jean's near hand is his left, because the armchair (centre y 595) sits about 30 cm north of his seat at the sofa's north end (centre y 565).
- The SC11 off-screen set (jacket off, tin onto the table, straw into her ice, phone to the nightstand) is done by Venus alone while Jean is in the bathroom; nothing passes between people, and it is heard only as ice.
- SC07 is staged as written (LATER), with a hard cut and a time jump from SC06; the continuity question is filed with the writer, not assumed.
- Phone and printer pair automatically in SC18, so there is no on-screen tapping to print.
- The glovebox lid stays open after B09.
- D01 timings cover the key physical actions; dialogue and holds fill the rest of each scene's slot.
- There is no product, so claim_demo_needs_clearance doesn't arise. The verified story facts (the wall with none of them together, the gun in her back pocket at the motel, the meds untaken for weeks, the dried blood behind the toilet seat, the trance-soul music, Jean in black, the white colour theme) are staged exactly, with acceptance tests.
- Taste notes broken on purpose: S01 shows her laughing face (a poster needs its lead); the B04 and B08 fallbacks show part of her face for under a second, as the route accepts; LOC-08 has no dish rack or charging cable because the tidiness is the horror. All others are followed: hands before faces, sound design before music, restraint over explanation, no slow motion.
- No reference image was inspected. Refs 05 and 06 are used only as the brief describes them.
- Blocker (casting director): VENUS's hand anchors don't match the phone hand. The world bible (PR-01 S2 and S6) and S01 put the phone in her right hand, but casting puts the thumb nick on her left thumb for the flip-icon beats (V01-B04, B08, V02-B06, SC18) and the scar hand on the B02 collar. With the right-hand phone, her right thumb rests on the icon, her left hand touches the collar at the stop (B02), and her right hand turns his chin (B07). → Smallest fix: move the thumb nick to the right thumb and keep the right-hand scar, which then reads in B07 and on the phone hand. B02's left hand carries no anchor.
- Blocker (production designer): PR-22, the two full water jugs, ride loose in the boot. On her one on-screen trip in B11 both shoulders and both arms are full, so the jugs can't reach the table in B12. → Pack both full jugs upright in grocery sack 2 under the oranges (they fit its 30 x 18 cm footprint) and update PR-07 and PR-22. B12 takes them out of sack 2, one per hand.
- Blocker (production designer): PR-03 has no entry state for SC12. It is in her jacket pocket through SC10, and she enters SC12 in a pocketless T-shirt with a drink in each hand. → Pre-set it during SC11 (off screen, while she changes): closed on PR-42 at x 175, y 535, hinge east. PR-42's state at the head of SC12 becomes 'tin only'.
- Blocker (production designer; stylist): Her jacket has no position from SC11, when she changes into the T-shirt, to SC17, when she puts it back on to pocket the tin and straw. It also has no V01 state. The SC11 off-screen set also moves PR-30 (jacket pocket to her tumbler's ice) and PR-01 (jacket pocket to PR-53) with no stated states. → Proposed: a single white hook on LOC-08 wall A beside the door, x 160, 170 cm up, out of the SC12-SC14 table frames. Add states: jacket on the hook SC11-SC17; PR-30 in her tumbler from SC11; PR-01 on PR-53 from SC11. From SC17 the stylist decides whether she wears the jacket (with PR-03 and PR-30) in V01 or it travels in PR-06.
- Blocker (stylist and production designer): Jean's sunglasses (PR-04) first appear on him in SC17, with no earlier state or source. → Hook them in the placket of his black shirt from SC04 (a night-time affectation that suits his swagger). They ride on top of the folded shirt in SC15, and she takes them from there in SC17.
- Blocker (production designer (states); stylist (pockets)): PR-01 has no state between SC04 and SC15, or between V01-B08 and SC18, and she needs both hands in B09-B12. → Right jacket pocket SC04-SC10. Face down on PR-53 from SC11 (the off-screen set) until SC17, when she pockets it. In her right hand through V01-B01 to B08. Right front jeans pocket from B09 (set across the cut) until SC18. The stylist confirms a front pocket that holds 15 x 7.2 cm.
- Blocker (production designer): V01-B05 switches on the video light, but the picture is her screen, so an on-screen control would show; PR-01 as described has no physical button. → Add one plain, unmarked side button to PR-01's frame edge, under her right index finger when she holds it upright. The press happens off picture and the UI never changes.
- Blocker (production designer): In LOC-02 the storefronts sit south of the crossroads, yet B03's first jump shows storefronts about 15 minutes after the 00:06:12 stop at that crossroads. → Confirm a loop route (east on the gravel road and back to the town's south end) so B03's storefront cut is a second northbound pass through the same block. No new build is needed.
- Blocker (production designer): SC18 has no source for the three pins: two for PR-28 and one for PR-27. → The next man's prints come out of PR-06 with pins already through their top edges, plus one spare pin in the top print for PR-27.
- Blocker (cinematographer): V02-B04 to B06 need about 7 s of phone-frame footage on Jean's open eyes, but V01-B08's phone section runs only 3.6 s. → Generate the B08 eyes plate with handles (two plates of up to 5 s joined on a laugh jolt) at 1080x1920 or better, and shoot the thumb composite plate long.
- Blocker (writer): SC06 ends on Venus's 'My turn' and SC07 opens with Jean taking her turn, which plays as continuous, but SC07's heading says LATER. Staged as written: a hard cut with a time jump and lower coupes. → Smallest fix: change SC07's heading to CONTINUOUS, which lets SC06 cut on action into his hand sliding the candle. Otherwise keep LATER and leave the staging as it is.
- Blocker (writer): Her two-seater is left parked across from the bar in SC08 (they leave by rideshare), and she has it for the drive in SC01. No scene gets it back. → Smallest fix: add one line to SC17, before she dresses Jean: a 4 s insert of the two-seater, top down, pulled up at her building's kerb in daylight. No dialogue.
- Blocker (writer): SC11 carries plot (A13, the previous man's drops) but has no honest link to the route idea; nothing on screen ties the drops to a photograph. Staged as written, and the link field says so. → Writer's call: accept SC11 as a plot scene while SC18 carries the idea. Or add a 2 s beat before he lifts the seat, where Jean takes a mirror selfie in PR-49 (PR-35): him alone in her home, one more photo with no her in it.

### Looks

- Season and temperature are assumed: early October 2026 in the US Southwest (the world bible gives only 'US, 2026 ASSUMED'). That puts the city at night at about 19 °C, the apartment at 21 °C, the desert drive at 27 °C falling to 17 °C, and the motel at night at about 15 °C.
- US sizing follows the casting bible's assumption. Skin depth and undertone are read from the casting bible's cheekbone hexes. Heritage is recorded there and never prompted, so every look restates depth, undertone and curl pattern instead.
- Jean's black: the route and set palettes use #111111, which I treat as the graded read. The fabric in a neutral grade is blue-black #24262B (approx L*15), per the craft rule that blacks sit near L* 15-22.
- Note 1 is applied, with one number corrected. At the high hip, 2 cm above the back pockets, only 2 cm of the 6 cm grip would clear the hem; the other 4 cm would sit under the back of the jacket. I cut the hem 7 cm above the pocket tops instead (at the waist, 1 cm above the waistband), so PR-02 S1 holds exactly as written. To still end above that hem, the inside left pocket moves up: welt 2 cm above the armhole base, bag 22 cm (not 24). That still takes the 20 cm straw. A waist-length jacket never reaches her lap, so no sit fold forms at all. The wide lock reads 'at the waist' rather than 'at the high hip' for this reason. The old blocker 2 is withdrawn.
- Note 2: the sleeve landmark is locked at the roll's lower edge, 12 cm above the wrist bone. Two 6 cm turns on a sleeve that ends at the wrist bone put the top of the roll at 18 cm, so the lower edge is what matches the L01 cuff edge. The seated creases sit just above the belt, not below it, because below the belt the shirt is tucked out of sight. The 'second hole' claim is cut: her belt stays on its middle hole in every look.
- Note 3: Jean's builds (SH-1 to SH-4, TR-1, TR-2, belt x2, Derbies x2) are mapped to scenes and beats. SH-4's split never appears in V01 0-56 s, because the phone films the back of his collar there. The belt is on hole 1, nearest the buckle (the loosest), 5 cm out from the old middle-hole crease.
- Note 5: L02's fastened top button is carried by the 44 cm collar band on SH-2 to SH-4. The band size never shows in L01, where the top button is open.
- Note 6 is answered both ways. Raglan oatmeal sleeves and a V-notch rib give the tee an athletic read in a wide, and oatmeal stays within the white room (C*10, L*81). I also state plainly that the team itself (Lucas's Raptors) is lost until A10 clears it or a fictional team is designed; this is flagged to Lucas.
- Venus changes looks only when the story does: the bar (L01), the tee at home (L02, changed off screen in SC11), then the shirt for the drive (L03, from SC17). The bone jacket comes back at the end of SC17 to take the tin and flat straw, then goes folded into the boot, unseen, through V01.
- The nape puff for the open car is the stylist's call, per the casting bible. It keeps hair off the hand shots and avoids the slow-motion hair the brief bans.
- There is no product (R2 stretch), so the product hue rule was run against the story's two reds instead: the REC dot #D7261E and the dried drops #6E1C18. Every garment worn in frame with them is under C* 15 or far off in hue. Skin and hair are excluded: skin is never altered, and hair belongs to the casting anchors.
- Every look has one garment older than the story, with a rebuild method: Venus's pistol-worn back pocket and her L02 tee, Jean's belt, the bartender's apron, and the driver's sun-faded shirt (taste note: one real detail per scene).
- Taste notes followed: hands before faces (every acting hand is bare, with cuffs at mid-forearm or above) and restraint over explanation (no signature jewellery or symbolic pieces; her whites and his black carry the verified colour theme). One craft rule is broken on purpose: VENUS_L02 sits inside the white room's palette so that Jean is the only dark mass, because the tone makes the room's tidiness the horror.
- No reference image was inspected, since no reference_reader is bound. Refs 02 and 06 are used only as the brief describes them, and no character is modelled on a face in them.
- Blocker (Storyboard artist with casting director and production designer): The hands conflict between departments. PR-01 puts the phone in Venus's right hand while she records (V01-B01 to B05, B08), which makes her left hand the free one that brushes his collar in B02. The casting bible anchors the collar and chin touches on her right-hand scar, and the flip-icon thumb on her left-thumb nick. → Wardrobe works either way: both sleeves end 12 cm above the wrist bone and both hands are bare in VENUS_L01 and L03. Storyboard picks the hand for each beat, and casting moves the anchors to match (for example, the nick on the right thumb if the right hand holds the phone).
- Blocker (Cinematographer / colourist and production designer): Jean's black is #111111 in the route and set palettes (PR-04, PR-56, the LOC-01 contrast note of 11.8:1). The costume is specified as blue-black #24262B fabric, per the craft rule for blacks at L* 15-22. PR-41 is confirmed at #2A2624, not #151515. → The colourist grades Jean toward the route's #111111 read; ungraded, the fabric still gives 9.5:1 on the bone headrest #D9CBB0. Production design updates the PR-41 and PR-56 hexes and notes the SH-1 to SH-4 and TR-1/TR-2 builds against PR-56's states.
- Blocker (Lucas (creative lead) via producer, with production designer): Lucas's 'oversized Raptors t shirt' loses its team. The L02 tee reads as an athletic shirt by its raglan cut and V-notch, but no team, city or colour can be shown until A10 clears the real shirt or a fictional team is designed and cleared. → Lucas chooses: accept the unmarked athletic tee, or have production design create a fictional team for the producer to clear under A10. If team colours come in, wardrobe re-runs the white-room rule break, because a saturated or dark team colour would make a second mass beside Jean.
- Blocker (Lucas (creative lead) via casting director): Heritage for VENUS and JEAN is still for Lucas to confirm (carried over from the casting bible). Hair and makeup here are written in skin depth, undertone and curl pattern only. → If casting changes, only the skin hexes, curl patterns and grooming lines in VENUS_L01-L03 and JEAN_L01-L02 need updating. The garments stand.

### Sound

- All timings are seconds from the first frame of each deliverable at 24 fps, with frame = round(s x 24). No picture exists yet, so every sync point is a target at ±2 frames, picture-led unless the cue marks it sound-led.
- V01 and V02 have no spoken lines (A15). Venus's laugh is the only voice, and Jean makes no sound in either, not even breath.
- Native audio on every generated clip is muted, and no generated audio is used anywhere.
- The picture car is generated. Its sound is recorded on a hired vintage open two-seater. That such a car can be hired is a hypothesis, and no make is identified.
- V02's sound is re-cut from V01's recordings on V02's own beats, and each shot's V01 in-point is stated: B01 at 6.5 s, B02 at 15.0 s, B03 at 42.8 s. B04 to B06 assume a continuous phone-frame take of at least 7.0 s, which is flagged in blockers.
- V02-B02's REC 00:52:30 shot is V01-B03's third jump cut, which V01's picture calls scrub hills and V02's calls open desert. The sound follows the shot, and the editor confirms it is the same one.
- The V02-B01 script says 'wind on the mic', but the driving wind is gone at the stop sign (V01-B02). It is played as a light breeze bumping the mic, taken from the same stop-sign session.
- The D01 cues are pilot anchors (decorum, the tin, Jean's breathing, the track, the clean-up, the tag), not full coverage. Their times come from the outline's scene times and will move with the pilot edit.
- 100 BPM, the four-bar loop and the bar-4 hole are a proposal for the commission, not a spec from Lucas. The 2.4 s hole is sized to hold one slow exhale; this is a design intent, not a measured fact.
- The track is commissioned full-range. The small mono speaker perspective (little below about 120 Hz) is how it is heard in the scene; its treatment belongs to the mix.
- The tin has a hinged lid; this is a prop choice for production design to confirm. Nothing inside the tin is ever heard, so the method stays off screen (must avoid).
- Loudness targets and delivery specs are left to the mix, so none are stated here.
- The Venus, Jean and Bartender actors are not yet cast, so every voice consent stays pending until they are.
- Gun-handling foley is recorded with a non-firing replica; production safety to confirm.
- The 2-frame audio dropout at the front-camera flip is a design choice, not a claim about how any phone behaves. In the tag it is used on purpose in the room's perspective, the one time the room is heard the way the phone hears it.
- The tag with no laugh is a proposal marked for approval; the script has her smiling, not laughing.
- Taste notes followed: sound design before music (no score in V01 or V02, and the pilot's only music plays inside the scene); restraint over explanation (no stings or drones on the eyes, the gun, the tin or the title); hands before faces (hand foley leads every beat); dry over sentimental (the verdict heard as a glass set down). None is broken on purpose.
- Blocker (Generation supervisor and editor): V02-B04 to B06 (6.0-13.0 s) run on one unbroken timer (01:04:55, 01:04:58, 01:05:00), so they need 7.0 s of continuous phone-frame footage of Jean's open eyes, with V02's action timing: still for 3.1 s, laugh at +3.1 s, thumb on at +5.2 s, thumb back at +6.4 s. V01-B08 has only 4.4 s of phone-frame footage (51.6-56.0 s), and its laugh comes 0.8 s after the frame narrows, so V01's cut cannot supply it. → Either generate the phone-frame eyes take at 7.0 s or longer with V02's action timing (SND-V02-004 to 006 are written for this), or re-time V02-B04 to B06 from V01's 4.4 s. If it is re-timed, the sound designer re-times SND-V02-004 to 006 from the V01 in-points.
